## Introduction: Worlds Collide

> > Welcome, adventurer! You've come just in the nick of time. I am known as Chronormu, but you can call me Chromie. I am comfortable with such familiarity. But perhaps you already knew that. Have we met before, or am I simply remembering that we shall meet again?
> > You see, I am one of the bronze dragonflight, forever attuned to the ebb and flow of time. My job is to keep tabs on certain timelines—the really important ones—to make sure things unfold the right way. Which is to say, the way they already unfolded. Or is it the way they will unfold?
> > Anyway, can you keep a secret? The truth is, messing with time is actually super fun. What if two fierce rivals never met? What if they teamed up and became a heroic duo? What if they fell in love but their romance was forbidden by their families? So many possibilities.
> > So this codex is something I whipped up as a fun way to explore all those what-if questions without risking any actual damage to the timelines. With this book, we can delve into the secrets of Azeroth like never before! Together, we can jump into these pages to view different versions of Azeroth's past, present, and future as if we were really there!
> > Oh, and don't worry! I'm almost entirely sure that this codex is absolutely, nearly one hundred percent stable! There's practically no chance of your adventures causing any significant snarls in any important timestreams.
> > So what do you say? Adventure awaits!
> > — Chromie
> 

This book is a union of two worlds in more than one sense: D&D: *World of Warcraft* is the meeting of the multiverse of D&D with the world of Azeroth (and connected realms), as well as the meeting of the world of tabletop roleplaying games with the world of massively multiplayer online roleplaying games. This fruitful collaboration brings new dimensions to your exploration of the world of Azeroth and new ideas to your D&D gaming table.

To make best use of this book, you need the Dungeons & Dragons Player's Handbook, Dungeon Master's Guide, and Monster Manual. But you can also get started with the resources available on DnDBeyond.com, including ready-made characters and free basic rules.

### What Is D&D?

*[Image: image (book/WoW/000-00-002.playing-the-game.webp)]*

This book is a setting supplement for Dungeons & Dragons, the original and still the greatest tabletop roleplaying game (TRPG). If you're familiar with the *World of Warcraft* online game, you already know the basic idea of D&D: Players create characters and explore a fantasy world, using magic and weapons to overcome obstacles and defeat enemies. But the action in D&D takes place primarily in the players' imaginations at a physical or virtual game table. One player takes on the role of Dungeon Master (DM), providing narration that sets the scene and determining the outcomes of the player characters' actions.

If you're a WoW fan, this book invites you to take your exploration of the world of Azeroth to a new level. As you travel across Kalimdor or the Eastern Kingdoms, sail to Northrend or the Dragon Isles, and venture into the dungeons detailed in this book (or dungeons of your DM's creation), you can interact with anything you can see, talk to anyone you meet, and try anything you can imagine. And because the game unfolds in the players' imaginations, anything can happen.

If you play as the DM, this book gives you tools to create your own adventures in Azeroth. Those stories might echo the themes and characters from WoW, or they might draw on existing elements to create entirely new adventures to make Azeroth your own.

Here's a glimpse of what a D&D game looks like:

**Jared (as Dungeon Master):** Seated at the table, apparently waiting for you, is a hulking tauren with enormous moose-like antlers. He stands as you enter and says, "Greetings, adventurers. I am Spiritwalker Ebonhorn. Mayla has told me much about you."

**Phillip (as Delenus):** Does his name mean anything to me?

**Maeve (as Maiatsumi):** Can I get a read on this guy? What's his deal?

- **Jared:.** Jared: All right, one at a time. Phillip, Delenus is searching his memory for any knowledge of Ebonhorn?
- **Phillip:.** Phillip: Yeah. And what's a spiritwalker?
- **Jared:.** Jared: Make an Intelligence check.
- **Phillip:.** Phillip: Does my Religion skill apply?
- **Jared:.** Jared: Sure, at least as far as what "spiritwalker" means.

**Phillip (rolling a d20):** Ugh. Seven.

- **Jared:.** Jared: It must be a Highmountain tauren thing. Means nothing to you. And Maeve, you're trying to get a read on him?
- **Maeve:.** Maeve: Yeah. Maybe using Insight?
- **Jared:.** Jared: Sure, make a Wisdom (Insight) check.

**Maeve (rolling a d20):** Nineteen! Plus... well, plus zero is still nineteen!

The adventure continues from there, relying on the DM's descriptions to set the scenes and on the players' decisions to determine how the action unfolds. You can learn more about D&D—including more examples of play, free rules, and ready-to-play characters you can use to start your adventures in Azeroth—by visiting DnDBeyond.com.

### What Is World of Warcraft?

*World of Warcraft* (WoW) is a massively multiplayer online roleplaying game (MMORPG, usually shortened to MMO)—an online video game where many, many people enter into a fantasy world, through the avatars of their characters, and explore that world in groups or independently. From the perspective of a D&D player, WoW is a treasure trove of lore and inspiration—a world that feels both familiar and uniquely new. Adventurers of familiar species such as elves and dwarves travel alongside less familiar folk such as pandaren and tauren. And the history of WoW's night elves, blood elves, and three main dwarf clans—and the dwarves' ties to the ancient titans—sets them apart from their counterparts in D&D.

With over twenty years of the *World of Warcraft* MMO (plus multiple real-time strategy games in the *Warcraft* line before that), the story of the world of Azeroth is long and deep. This book only scratches the surface of that rich lore. The official World of Warcraft website (worldofwarcraft.blizzard.com) offers a starting point where you can learn more about the world and the MMO, while fan-made wikis—readily discoverable in any online search engine—carefully chronicle the details of these decades of stories. The "x" sidebar includes a list of resources to help you learn more about the world of Azeroth.

*[Image: Chromie waits for adventurers near the Stormwind Embassy. (book/WoW/001-00-001.chromie-waiting.webp)]*

### Inside This Book

D&D: *World of Warcraft* is a guide to creating characters and adventures in the world of Azeroth.

1 focuses on the thirteen character classes in the *World of Warcraft* MMO. Each is presented in alphabetical order, with an explanation of how to build and play that class as a D&D character. That explanation points you to one of the twelve classes in the D&D Player's Handbook, but it includes alterations, new options, and guidance to reflect WoW classes and their specializations. So whether you want to re-create the fury warrior you play in the MMO or make a new character, this chapter is your starting point for playing WoW classes in D&D.

2 focuses on character origins, particularly species. Each playable species in the MMO is described here. Some species use rules described in the D&D *Player's Handbook*, while other species have new rules. Whether you're playing a human or a vulpera, this chapter has the information you need.

3 covers rules and options related to character professions—both gathering and crafting—as well as mounts, pets, and new magic items.

4 details important factions of Azeroth, starting with the Alliance and the Horde and their subfactions. The chapter expands on the renown rules in the D&D Dungeon Master's Guide to map out the rewards you can gain as you build your reputation with these factions.

5 is a brief overview of the world of Azeroth. It includes a summary of each region of the world, suggestions for the kinds of adventure to be found in those locations, and a glimpse at the worlds and planes beyond Azeroth.

6, intended solely for the Dungeon Master, focuses on crafting adventures in the world of Azeroth. It outlines the world's major conflicts and encourages the DM to mix and match those conflicts to create unique adventures and campaigns. It then presents several familiar dungeons from the *World of Warcraft* MMO for characters to explore.

7, also intended for the Dungeon Master, includes descriptions and stat blocks for new monsters and important nonplayer characters (NPCs) from the *World of Warcraft* MMO. From dragons of the five ordered dragonflights and demons of the Burning Legion to lowly murlocs and furbolgs, this bestiary supplements the D&D Monster Manual to provide new adversaries and potential allies for characters in a *World of Warcraft* D&D campaign. And if characters want to join forces with Thrall or Jaina and test their mettle against Illidan or Arthas, this chapter provides stat blocks for those legendary characters, among others.

As Chromie suggests at the start of this introduction, this book helps you make Azeroth your own. Just as you would when you start any D&D campaign, you can adapt elements in this book to fit the way you want to play. Chromie has given you a separate timeline as your playground, so make of it whatever you will!

### Creating a Character

Making a D&D character to explore the world of Azeroth follows the same steps outlined in the *Player's Handbook*, but this book provides additional options you can select throughout the process:

- **1: Choose a Class.** 1: Choose a Class Use the descriptions of *World of Warcraft* classes in 1 to guide your class selection.
- **2: Determine Origin.** 2: Determine Origin Choose a background from the Player's Handbook and a species from 2. These two elements can combine in interesting ways to make unique origins. Also note your starting Renown Score, if any, from your species.
- **3: Determine Ability Scores.** 3: Determine Ability Scores Use the Player's Handbook to guide you in this step.
- **4: Choose an Alignment.** 4: Choose an Alignment The peoples of the Alliance tend toward Lawful Good alignments, while those of the Horde tend toward Chaotic Good. See 4 for more information.
- **5: Fill in Details.** 5: Fill in Details Use the Player's Handbook to guide you in this step.

> **Where to Learn More**
> The books listed here are a great place to dive more deeply into the lore of Azeroth:
> ##### World of Warcraft: Chronicle
> The four volumes in this series offer a comprehensive look at the history of Azeroth and Draenor, covering the stories of every game in the Warcraft franchise and each *World of Warcraft* expansion.
> ##### World of Warcraft: Exploring Azeroth
> This five-book series—Eastern Kingdoms, Kalimdor, Northrend, Pandaria, and Islands & Isles—surveys the world of Azeroth, providing more detail on the regions mentioned in 5 of this book.
> ##### World of Warcraft: Grimoire of the Shadowlands and Beyond
> This book provides a tour of the Shadowlands—Warcraft's mysterious afterlife.
> ##### World of Warcraft: The Dragonflight Codex
> This book offers a comprehensive guide to the dragons of Azeroth, as well as to the Dragon Isles.
> In addition to these reference books, dozens of novels, short stories, audio dramas, animations, and comics in both the *Warcraft* and the *World of Warcraft* lines tell the stories of Azeroth in narrative form.

> > Take care of yourself out there.

## Chapter 1: Character Classes

*[Image: Horde adventurers confront Onyxia in her lair. (book/WoW/002-01-001.horde-adventurers-splash.webp)]*

Creating a character for a game using this book begins with choosing a class. *World of Warcraft* offers thirteen classes, each with a variety of specializations. Each class and specialization maps to certain choices of features from the Player's Handbook plus new options in this book. Three sidebars in the next few pages—"x," "x," and "x"—provide more general information.

### World of Warcraft Classes

The thirteen *World of Warcraft* classes described in this book are summarized below:

Death Knight. Straddling the line between life and death, death knights emphasize debilitating curses and sinister magic.

Demon Hunter. Demon hunters are haunted outcasts who wield fel magic.

Druid. Druids are shape-shifters with an affinity for plants, animals, and the magic of nature.

Evoker. Evokers unleash devastating draconic spells on enemies and healing magic on allies.

Hunter. Hunters are at home in the wilderness and have a special connection with beasts. Hunters rely on weaponry and pets to deal damage.

Mage. Mages are magic-users who learn their craft through intense research and study. They wield a potent array of offensive and defensive spells.

Monk. Monks are known for their skill in hand-to-hand fighting, including unarmed combat.

Paladin. Paladins are heavily armored defenders whose holy magic heals wounds and combats evil.

Priest. Priests express their faith by serving the people as healers and champions.

Rogue. Rogues sneak around enemies and attack while hiding to finish foes quickly.

Shaman. Shaman wield elemental magic against their foes.

Warlock. Warlocks wield demonic magic, including summoned demons, to curse and immolate foes.

Warrior. Warriors are heavily armored fighters who strive for perfection in combat.

> **Hero Classes**
> Three of the *World of Warcraft* classes—death knight, demon hunter, and evoker—are hero classes, meaning that they are typically available only to characters starting at level 3 or higher. In the story of *World of Warcraft*, characters with these classes are already experienced and powerful adventurers by the time they enter the game.
> The death knight and the demon hunter classes are represented by subclasses (the Oath of the Ebon Blade Paladin and the WoW Ranger, respectively) that alter the character's base class. With your DM's permission, you can start as a level 1 character of one of these classes, but your subclass choice is fixed when you make the character.
> Playing an evoker doesn't alter the base Sorcerer class, but it slightly alters the Draconic Sorcery subclass.

### Death Knight

*[Image: image (classes/WoW/Death Knight Crest.webp)]*

*[Image: Orc Death Knight (classes/WoW/Death Knight.webp)]*

#### Death Knight

##### Level 1

###### Death Coil

This feature replaces the XPHB feature of the Paladin class.

Your creature type becomes Undead.

You also gain the ability to manipulate negative energy. You have a pool of d8s to fuel this ability. The number of dice in the pool equals one plus your Charisma modifier (minimum of one die).

As a Bonus Action, you can target a creature you can see within 60 feet of yourself and force it to make a Constitution saving throw, expending dice from the pool. The maximum number of dice you can expend at once equals your Charisma modifier (minimum of one die). Roll the dice you expend. The target takes Necrotic damage equal to the roll's total on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. If the target is an Undead, you can choose to have it regain Hit Points equal to the roll's total instead. Your pool regains all expended dice when you finish a Long Rest.

*[Unresolved feature reference: Spellcasting|Paladin|XPHB|1]*

*[Unresolved feature reference: Weapon Mastery|Paladin|XPHB|1]*

*[Unresolved feature reference: Fighting Style|Paladin|XPHB|2]*

##### Level 2

###### Runic Smite

This feature replaces the Paladin's Smite feature of the Paladin class.

You always have the Rune Strike spell prepared. You can cast it once without a spell slot, and you regain the ability to cast it in that way when you finish a Long Rest.

*[Unresolved feature reference: Channel Divinity|Paladin|XPHB|3]*

##### Level 3

###### Death Knight Subclass

You gain a Death Knight subclass of your choice. A subclass is a specialization that grants you features at certain Death Knight levels. For the rest of your career, you gain each of your subclass's features that are of your Death Knight level or lower.

*[Unresolved feature reference: Ability Score Improvement|Paladin|XPHB|4]*

*[Unresolved feature reference: Extra Attack|Paladin|XPHB|5]*

##### Level 5

###### Faithful Steed

You can call on the aid of an otherworldly steed. You always have the Find Steed spell prepared.

You can also cast the spell once without expending a spell slot, and you regain the ability to do so when you finish a Long Rest.

In addition to the benefits of the Paladin's XPHB feature, when you cast Find Steed, the **Otherworldly Steed** can be Undead. An Undead steed deals Necrotic damage with its Otherworldly Slam action and has this Bonus Action:

###### Ghostly Stride (Undead Only; Recharges After a Long Rest)

The steed and its rider can move through other creatures and objects this turn as if they were Difficult Terrain. Both steed and rider take 5 (1d10) Force damage if they end their turn inside an object.

###### Runic Strikes

This feature replaces the Paladin's XPHB feature.

When you hit a target with an attack roll using a Melee weapon or an Unarmed Strike, the target takes an extra 1d8 Necrotic damage.

###### Improved Death Coil

This feature replaces the Paladin's XPHB feature.

When you use WoW to deal damage, the target takes extra Necrotic damage equal to your Charisma modifier (minimum of 1) whether or not it fails its saving throw. If the target is an Undead, and you have it regain Hit Points, it regains additional Hit Points equal to your Paladin level.

#### Playing a Death Knight

Death knight is a hero class, which means you typically must start play at level 3 or higher (see the "x" sidebar). To play a death knight, choose the Paladin class from the Player's Handbook and the Oath of the Ebon Blade subclass, described below. This subclass alters some of your core Paladin class features beginning at level 1.

As a death knight, you can choose one of three specializations by inscribing the corresponding rune on your weapon using your Runeforging feature: Blood, Frost, or Unholy.

##### Blood Specialization

With the Blood rune, you are a dark guardian who manipulates and corrupts life energy to sustain yourself in the face of an enemy onslaught. The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose Greataxes, Greatswords, or Mauls for your Weapon Mastery feature. Choose Interception for a Fighting Style feat.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Charger, Crusher, Great Weapon Fighting, Great Weapon Master, Heavy Armor Master, Piercer, Slasher, and Tough.

##### Frost Specialization

Wielding the Frost rune, you are an icy harbinger of doom, channeling runic power and delivering vicious weapon strikes. The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose Light Hammers or Scimitars for your Weapon Mastery feature. Choose Two-Weapon Fighting for a Fighting Style feat.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Charger, Crusher, Dual Wielder, and Slasher.

##### Unholy Specialization

With the Unholy rune, you become a master of death and decay, spreading infection and controlling undead minions. The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose Greataxes, Greatswords, or Mauls for your Weapon Mastery feature. Choose Great Weapon Fighting for a Fighting Style feat.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Charger, Crusher, Great Weapon Master, Piercer, and Slasher.

> **Specialization**
> Every class in *World of Warcraft* offers options for specializing in different aspects of the class's powers and features. In this book, those specializations are reflected in these ways:
> - **Subclass Features.** Subclass Features The Oath of the Ebon Blade subclass's Runeforging feature reflects the three death knight specializations, allowing a character to choose a new specialization with every level gained.
> - **Feat and Spell Selection.** Feat and Spell Selection Some specializations are represented with different Fighting Style feats; the difference between an arms warrior and a protection warrior is largely a matter of the weapons they use and the corresponding Fighting Styles. Similarly, mage specializations are reflected in the character's spell selection, along with feats that enhance different spells.
> - **Subclass Choice.** Subclass Choice Often, specializations point to different subclasses within the same class. For example, the monk specializations of brewmaster, mistweaver, and windwalker map to three different Monk subclasses.
> 

#### Oath of the Ebon Blade (Paladin Subclass)

> **Other Class Options**
> Check with your DM if you want to build a character using options that aren't described in this book. For example, while Bard isn't a playable class in *World of Warcraft*, many magical musicians dwell on Azeroth, and your DM might allow you to use the Bard class from the Player's Handbook. Similarly, your DM can allow or disallow subclasses that aren't referenced in this book (such as Aberrant Sorcery or the XPHB Fighter) and the option of multiclassing.

##### Bonestorm

*Level:* 5

*School:* A

You conjure a whirl of battering bone and gore around yourself. You regain 4d6 Hit Points, and each creature in a 10-foot Emanation originating from you makes a Constitution saving throw. A target takes 4d8 Necrotic damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

##### Chains of Ice

*Level:* 1

*School:* V

You create chains of ice around a creature you can see within range. The target makes a Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, the target takes 1d10 Cold damage, and its Speed is halved until the end of its next turn. On a successful save, the target takes half as much damage only.

##### Death and Decay

*Level:* 2

*School:* N

Corruption ripples out in a 20-foot-radius Sphere from a point on the ground you can see within range. Each creature in that area makes a Wisdom saving throw. On a failed save, a target takes 2d6 Necrotic damage and is cursed until the end of your next turn. If you deal damage to a cursed target with an attack roll, the target takes an extra 1d6 Necrotic damage. On a successful save, the target takes half as much damage only.

##### Death Grip

*Level:* 1

*School:* A

You harness runic energies to bind a foe to you. One creature you can see within range must succeed on a Strength saving throw or be pulled straight toward you to the nearest unoccupied space within 5 feet of you.

##### Frostscythe

*Level:* 2

*School:* V

Sweeping your weapon before you, you unleash a blast of icy cold. Each creature in a 20-foot Cone makes a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 3d8 Cold damage, and its Speed is reduced by 10 feet for 1 minute. On a successful save, a creature takes half as much damage only.

##### Leeching Strike

*Level:* 3

*School:* N

The target hit by the strike takes an extra 3d8 Necrotic damage from the attack, and you regain 3d4 Hit Points.

##### Remorseless Winter

*Level:* 3

*School:* V

You drain the warmth from nearby creatures. Each creature of your choice in a 30-foot Emanation originating from you makes a Constitution saving throw, taking 6d6 Cold damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. Whether or not a creature succeeds on its saving throw, its Speed is reduced to 10 feet until the end of its next turn as clinging frost hinders its movement.

*[Image: image (book/WoW/005-01-003.death-knight-spells.webp)]*

##### Rune Strike

*Level:* 1

*School:* V

The target takes an extra 2d8 Necrotic damage. The damage increases by 1d8 if the target is a Celestial.

##### Scourge Strike

*Level:* 1

*School:* N

The target takes an extra 2d6 Necrotic damage from the attack, and it must succeed on a Constitution saving throw or have the Poisoned condition until the spell ends. At the end of each of its turns, the Poisoned target repeats the save, ending the spell on itself on a success.

##### Vampiric Blood

*Level:* 1

*School:* N

For the duration, your Hit Point maximum increases by 10. In addition, whenever you regain Hit Points, you regain an extra 1d6 Hit Points.

### Demon Hunter

*[Image: image (classes/WoW/Demon Hunter Crest.webp)]*

*[Image: Illidan Stormrage and Two Demon Hunters Under His Tutelage (classes/WoW/Demon Hunter.webp)]*

#### Demon Hunter

##### Level 1

###### Spellcasting

You have learned to channel the magical essence of nature to cast spells. See 7 for the rules on spellcasting. The information below details how you use those rules with Ranger spells, which appear in the class=Demon Hunter later in the class's description.

###### Spell Slots

The Ranger Features table shows how many spell slots you have to cast your level 1+ spells. You regain all expended slots when you finish a Long Rest.

###### Prepared Spells of Level 1+

You prepare the list of level 1+ spells that are available for you to cast with this feature. To start, choose two level=1. Cure Wounds and Ensnaring Strike are recommended.

The number of spells on your list increases as you gain Ranger levels, as shown in the Prepared Spells column of the Ranger Features table. Whenever that number increases, choose additional Ranger spells until the number of spells on your list matches the number in the Ranger Features table. The chosen spells must be of a level for which you have spell slots. For example, if you're a level 5 Ranger, your list of prepared spells can include six Ranger spells of level 1 or 2 in any combination.

If another Ranger feature gives you spells that you always have prepared, those spells don't count against the number of spells you can prepare with this feature, but those spells otherwise count as Ranger spells for you.

###### Changing Your Prepared Spells

Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can replace one spell on your list with another Ranger spell for which you have spell slots.

###### Spellcasting Ability

Wisdom is your spellcasting ability for your Ranger spells.

###### Spellcasting Focus

You can use an Arcane Focus (not a Druidic Focus) as a Spellcasting Focus for your Ranger spells.

You gain a Demon Hunter subclass of your choice. A subclass is a specialization that grants you features at certain Demon Hunter levels. For the rest of your career, you gain each of your subclass's features that are of your Demon Hunter level or lower.

#### Playing a Demon Hunter

Demon hunter is a hero class, which means you typically must start play at level 3 or higher (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter). To play a demon hunter, choose the Ranger class from the Player's Handbook. Your subclass, the WoW, alters some of your core Ranger class features.

All demon hunters in *World of Warcraft* lore are elves (blood elves, night elves, or void elves), so check with your DM before playing a demon hunter of another species.

As a Demon Hunter, you can choose one of three specializations: Devourer, Havoc, or Vengeance.

##### Devourer Specialization

With the devourer specialization, you harness the power of the Void to crush your enemies and feast on their souls. The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Handaxes, Scimitars, or Shortswords for your Weapon Mastery feature. Choose the Void Warrior option instead of a Fighting Style feat, and choose Chill Touch and Mind Sliver as your cantrips.
- **Spells.** Spells Choose the Devourer spells from the x.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Defensive Duelist, Mage Slayer, Shadow Touched, Slasher, Speedy, and Spell Sniper.

##### Havoc Specialization

With the havoc specialization, you are a fierce melee combatant who masters the destructive power of fel magic. The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Handaxes, Scimitars, or Shortswords for your Weapon Mastery feature. Choose Two-Weapon Fighting for a Fighting Style feat.
- **Spells.** Spells Choose the Havoc spells from the x.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Charger, Dual Wielder, Mage Slayer, and Slasher.

##### Vengeance Specialization

With the vengeance specialization, you embrace the demon within to incinerate your enemies and protect your allies. The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Battleaxes or Longswords for your Weapon Mastery feature. Choose Interception as your Fighting Style feat.
- **Spells.** Spells Choose the Vengeance spells from the x.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Charger, Durable, Sentinel, and Slasher.

> **Demon Hunter Warglaives**
> During the War of the Ancients, Illidan defeated a demonic commander and claimed the demon's weapons—called warglaives—as his own. These later became known as the Twin Blades of Azzinoth, and they inspired a whole category of weapons that are favored by demon hunters to this day. Warglaives vary widely in appearance and construction, giving different weapons the game statistics of a Battleaxe, a Handaxe, a Longsword, or a Scimitar.

#### Demon Hunter (Ranger Subclass)

#### Void Warrior

*Prerequisite:* {'otherSummary': {'entry': 'When Gaining the Level 2 Demon Hunter "Fighting Style" Feature', 'entrySummary': 'Special'}}

You learn two class=warlock of your choice. Chill Touch and Mind Sliver are recommended. The chosen cantrips count as Ranger spells for you, and Wisdom is your spellcasting ability for them. Whenever you gain a Ranger level, you can replace one of these cantrips with another Warlock cantrip.

#### Demon Hunter Spells

The following new spells appear on the Demon Hunter Spells table. Although they are Ranger spells, they are available only to Rangers with the WoW subclass. In the Special column, *C* means the spell requires Concentration, and *M* means it requires a specific Material component.

**Demon Hunter Spells**

| Level | Spell | Special |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Consume Soul | — |
| 1 | Fel Rush | — |
| 1 | Infernal Strike | — |
| 1 | Throw Glaive | — |
| 2 | Eye Beam | C |
| 2 | Fiery Brand | — |
| 2 | Sigil of Flame | — |
| 3 | Demon Spikes | — |
| 3 | Metamorphosis | M |
| 3 | Soul Reap | — |
| 4 | Chaos Nova | — |
| 4 | Collapsing Star | — |
| 5 | Sigil of Chains | — |

##### Chaos Nova

*Level:* 4

*School:* V

Chaos energy bursts out in a 20-foot Emanation originating from you. Each creature of your choice in the Emanation takes 2d6 Force damage and must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw or have the Stunned condition until the end of your next turn.

##### Collapsing Star

*Level:* 4

*School:* V

You channel Void energy into a cataclysmic blast that fills a 20-foot-radius Sphere centered on a point you can see within range. Each creature of your choice within the Sphere makes a Constitution saving throw, taking 4d12 Force damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

##### Consume Soul

*Level:* 1

*School:* N

You drain the essence from a creature within range, which makes a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, it takes 4d4 Force damage, and you gain Temporary Hit Points equal to half the Force damage dealt. On a successful save, it takes half as much damage only.

##### Demon Spikes

*Level:* 3

*School:* T

You surge with fel power and sprout hard, jagged spikes. You gain a +2 bonus to AC for the duration, and when a creature within 5 feet of you hits you with a melee attack roll, it takes 10 Piercing damage.

##### Eye Beam

*Level:* 2

*School:* V

You concentrate your magic into your eyes. When you cast the spell, and as a Magic action on your subsequent turns for the duration, you can unleash a burst of fel magic. Each creature in a 15-foot Cone makes a Dexterity saving throw, taking 3d6 Force damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

##### Fel Rush

*Level:* 1

*School:* T

Fiery fel magic surrounds you and grants you explosive speed. Until the end of your turn, your Speed increases by 30 feet, and your movement doesn't provoke Opportunity Attacks.

When you move within 5 feet of a creature or an object that isn't being worn or carried, it makes a Dexterity saving throw, taking 2d6 Fire damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. A creature or object can take this damage only once per turn.

##### Fiery Brand

*Level:* 2

*School:* V

You magically place a fiery demonic symbol on a creature within range, which makes a Wisdom saving throw. On a failed save, the creature takes 3d10 Fire damage and is cursed. While it remains cursed, you have Resistance to all damage it deals. On a successful save, it takes half as much damage only.

A cursed creature repeats the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the spell on a success.

##### Infernal Strike

*Level:* 1

*School:* V

You jump through the air without provoking Opportunity Attacks and land in an unoccupied space within 30 feet. Each creature in a 10-foot Emanation originating from you makes a Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 2d6 Fire damage and has Disadvantage on attack rolls against creatures other than you until the start of your next turn. On a successful save, it takes half as much damage only.

##### Metamorphosis

*Level:* 3

*School:* T

Uttering an incantation, you draw on fel or Void magic to transform yourself, taking on demonic features. You gain one of the following benefits of your choice until the spell ends:

- **Devourer.** When you roll damage for a spell, weapon, or feature that deals Force damage, you can treat any 1 or 2 on a damage die as a 3.
- **Havoc.** Whenever you hit a creature with an attack roll using a Melee weapon, the target takes an extra 1d6 Fire damage.
- **Vengeance.** You have a +2 bonus to AC, and your Hit Point maximum and your current Hit Points increase by 5 for the spell's duration.

*[Image: A Demon Hunter casts Metamorphosis. (book/WoW/008-01-005.metamorphosis.webp)]*

##### Sigil of Chains

*Level:* 5

*School:* C

A sigil appears at a point on the ground you can see within range, then erupts in a 20-foot-radius Sphere. Each creature in the Sphere makes a Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 5d6 Force damage, is pulled up to 10 feet straight toward the sigil, and has the Restrained condition until the end of your next turn. On a successful save, the creature takes half as much damage, and its Speed is reduced by 10 feet until the end of your next turn.

##### Sigil of Flame

*Level:* 2

*School:* V

You inscribe a sigil in the air that then appears on a creature you can see within range. The target makes a Constitution saving throw, taking 2d8 Fire damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. Whether or not the target fails its saving throw, it radiates heat in a 10-foot Emanation originating from itself until the end of its next turn. At the start of the target's next turn, the target and each creature in the Emanation must succeed on a Constitution saving throw or take 1d8 Fire damage.

##### Soul Reap

*Level:* 3

*School:* N

You cut a hole in the fabric of reality, sending a bolt of cosmic matter toward a creature in range. Make a ranged spell attack against the target, which gains no benefit from Half Cover or Three-Quarters Cover. On a hit, you deal 6d6 Force damage. If you roll a 6 on a d6 for this spell, you can roll another d6 and add it to the damage. When you cast this spell, the maximum number of these d6s you can add to the spell's damage equals your spellcasting ability modifier.

##### Throw Glaive

*Level:* 1

*School:* V

You create a demonic warglaive and hurl it at a creature or object in range. Make a ranged spell attack against the target. On a hit, the target takes 1d8 Slashing damage plus 1d8 Fire damage, and the glaive ricochets to a different target of your choice within 30 feet of the target. Make new attack and damage rolls against the new target.

### Druid

*[Image: image (classes/WoW/Druid Crest.webp)]*

*[Image: Night Elf Druid (classes/WoW/Druid.webp)]*

#### Druid

#### Playing a Druid

To play a druid, choose the Druid class from the Player's Handbook. You can choose one of four specializations to guide your choice of subclass, spells, and feats: balance, feral, guardian, or restoration.

Adopting the Druid class, either at character creation or through multiclassing, increases a character's Renown Score with the Cenarion Circle by 3 (see x).

##### Balance Specialization

By leveraging the sacred powers of the moons, the sun, and the stars, balance druids keep the elemental forces of the cosmos in an equilibrium. To that end, they wield both celestial spells that deal Radiant damage and nature-oriented spells that deal Acid, Lightning, or Poison damage, striving to keep the world and its celestial powers in balance. They use the shape-shifted form of a moonkin—an owlbear-like creature favored by the moon goddess Elune—to heighten their attunement to these natural forces.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose XPHB for your Primal Order feature. Choose the following cantrips: Druidcraft, Starry Wisp, and Wrath. Prepare the following level 1 spells to start: Cure Wounds, Entangle, Moonfire, and Thunderwave.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW. When you use XPHB, you can take on the appearance of a moonkin, but the other effects are identical.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Elemental Adept and War Caster.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Balance Druid Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 2 | Barkskin, Moonbeam, Summon Beast |
| 3 | Call Lightning, Daylight, Dispel Magic, Summon Fey |
| 4 | Fount of Moonlight, Starfall |
| 5 | Reincarnate, Scrying |
| 6 | Conjure Fey, Sunbeam |
| 7 | Fire Storm |
| 8 | Sunburst |
| 9 | Storm of Vengeance |

##### Feral Specialization

To feral druids, nature is an eternal dance between predator and prey, with death an integral part of the cycle that sustains life. These druids seek deeper understanding of this truth by pursuing a visceral connection to the wild. Their favored shape-shifted form is a deadly feline predator—a ferocious, agile stalker that rips, bites, and bleeds enemies dry.

The following choices will help you fill the feral druid's role:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose XPHB for your Primal Order feature. Choose the following cantrips: Druidcraft and Poison Spray. Prepare the following level 1 spells to start: Cure Wounds, Entangle, Speak with Animals, and Thunderwave.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW. When you use XPHB, you can adopt the **Cat Form** stat block below.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Skulker, Slasher, and Speedy.
- **Spells.** Spells After you gain XPHB at level 5, giving up a spell slot to gain another use of Wild Shape is often worthwhile. But consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Feral Druid Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 2 | Feral Prowl, Pass Without Trace, Summon Beast |
| 3 | Call Lightning, Conjure Animals |
| 4 | Dominate Beast, Freedom of Movement |
| 5 | Commune with Nature, Innervate |
| 6 | Wind Walk |
| 7 | Regenerate |
| 8 | Animal Shapes |
| 9 | Shapechange |

###### Cat Form

*Size:* Medium

*Speed:* walk 40 ft.

*Type:* {'type': 'special', 'typeHidden': True}

###### Bear Form

*Size:* Large

*Speed:* walk 30 ft.

*Type:* {'type': 'special', 'typeHidden': True}

> **Classic Druid Beast Forms**
> The beast forms that every druid in *World of Warcraft* learns are Cat Form, Bear Form, and Travel Form (including Aquatic Form and Flight Form).
> ###### Cat Form
> If you have the WoW, use the Cat Form stat block described above. If not, you can become a Panther at level 2 and a Tiger at level 8.
> ###### Bear Form
> If you have the WoW, use the Bear Form stat block described above. If not, you can become a Black Bear at level 4 and a Brown Bear at level 8.
> ###### Travel Form
> You can become an Elk starting at level 2. For fast swimming, consider a Giant Seahorse starting at level 4 (or level 3 for the Circle of the Moon). When you gain access to flying forms at level 8, you can become an Eagle, a Giant Bat, a Hawk, a Pteranodon, or a Raven.

##### Guardian Specialization

Guardian druids commune with the wild to better understand how life perseveres through adversity. These druids deeply value steadfastness and meet danger with fierce resistance. They prefer the shape-shifted form of a great bear—a massive wall of fur, claws, teeth, and rage aided by the magic of nature to protect their allies from danger.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose XPHB for your Primal Order feature. Choose the following cantrips: Druidcraft and Thorn Whip. Prepare the following level 1 spells to start: Entangle, Healing Word, Speak with Animals, and Thunderwave.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW. When you use XPHB, you can adopt the **Bear Form** stat block above.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Durable, Sentinel, and Slasher.
- **Spells.** Spells After you gain XPHB at level 5, giving up a spell slot to gain another use of Wild Shape is often worthwhile. But consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Guardian Druid Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 2 | Aid, Barkskin, Roar of Ursoc |
| 3 | Aura of Vitality, Call Lightning |
| 4 | Grasping Vine, Stoneskin |
| 5 | Commune with Nature, Innervate |
| 6 | Wall of Thorns |
| 7 | Regenerate |
| 8 | Animal Shapes |
| 9 | Shapechange |

##### Restoration Specialization

Restoration druids tend to the life-forms of the world, celebrating birth and growth in search of order and harmony. Where there is decay and devastation, these druids bring rejuvenation and regrowth. Life, in their view, needs not only protection, but also nourishment. To that end, they bond with living and growing things, seeking inspiration from the flower's bloom, the seed's sprout, the mushroom's spores, and the tree's towering growth.

Like nature itself, restoration druids persevere through patience, and they wield their nature-given power to mend wounds and protect their allies.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose XPHB for your Primal Order feature. Choose the following cantrips: Druidcraft, Produce Flame, and Spare the Dying. Prepare the following level 1 spells to start: Cure Wounds, Entangle, Goodberry, and Healing Word.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Chef, Healer, Inspiring Leader, and War Caster.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Restoration Druid Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 2 | Aid, Lesser Restoration, Protection from Poison |
| 3 | Aura of Vitality, Regrowth, Revivify |
| 4 | Divination, Freedom of Movement |
| 5 | Greater Restoration, Mass Cure Wounds |
| 6 | Heal, Tranquility |
| 7 | Regenerate |
| 8 | Tree of Life |
| 9 | Foresight |

> > Like a dragon's visage, a druid's beast form reflects something of their true nature.
> > — Chromie
> 

#### Druid Spells

The following new spells are added to the Druid spell list for *World of Warcraft* druids. In the Special column, *C* means the spell requires Concentration.

**Druid Spells**

| Level | Spell | Special |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cantrip | Wrath | — |
| 1 | Moonfire | — |
| 2 | Feral Prowl | — |
| 2 | Roar of Ursoc | — |
| 3 | Regrowth | C |
| 4 | Starfall | — |
| 5 | Innervate | — |
| 6 | Tranquility | C |
| 8 | Tree of Life | — |

##### Feral Prowl

*Level:* 2

*School:* T

You shape-shift as if you had used XPHB, but you don't expend a use of your XPHB. You have the Invisible condition until the spell ends. The spell ends early immediately after you make an attack roll, deal damage, cast a spell, or revert to your natural form.

##### Innervate

*Level:* 5

*School:* T

A creature of your choice within range gains a level 4 spell slot. The spell slot vanishes after it is expended or the target finishes a Long Rest. It can use this spell slot to cast any spell it has prepared.

In addition, you regain one expended use of XPHB.

##### Moonfire

*Level:* 1

*School:* V

A beam of lunar light burns a creature you can see within range. The target makes a Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, it takes 2d8 Radiant damage, sheds Dim Light in a 10-foot radius, and can't benefit from the Invisible condition until the end of its next turn. At the end of its next turn, it takes an extra 1d8 Radiant damage, and the spell ends. On a successful save, it takes half the initial damage only.

##### Regrowth

*Level:* 3

*School:* A

One creature you can see within range regains 3d8 Hit Points. For the duration, the target regains 1d8 Hit Points at the start of each of your turns. The spell ends early once it restores at least 24 Hit Points.

##### Roar of Ursoc

*Level:* 2

*School:* T

You shape-shift as if you had used XPHB, but you don't expend a use of your XPHB. Until the start of your next turn, any creature that starts its turn within 5 feet of you has Disadvantage on attack rolls against any creature other than you.

##### Starfall

*Level:* 4

*School:* V

You call down falling stars on creatures in a 30-foot-radius, 40-foot-high Cylinder within range. Each creature in the Cylinder makes a Dexterity saving throw, taking 7d8 Radiant damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

Until the start of your next turn, a creature that enters the Cylinder for the first time on a turn or ends its turn there takes Radiant damage equal to your Druid level. A creature takes this damage only once per turn.

*[Image: Bella Wilder, a Druid of the Cenarion Circle (book/WoW/011-01-007.druid-of-the-cenarion-circle.webp)]*

##### Tranquility

*Level:* 6

*School:* A

Nature's power flows around you in a 60-foot Emanation for the duration. Creatures of your choice in the Emanation have Resistance to Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing damage. As a Bonus Action, you can cause each ally in the Emanation to regain Hit Points equal to 2d8 plus your spellcasting ability modifier.

##### Tree of Life

*Level:* 8

*School:* T

You shape-shift into a Tree of Life form that resembles a treant. In this form, you gain the following benefits.

###### Tree Form

You become Large. Your Speed becomes 10 feet and can't be reduced, and Difficult Terrain doesn't cost you extra movement.

###### Bark Skin

You gain a +2 bonus to Armor Class and 10 Temporary Hit Points.

###### Condition Immunity

You gain Immunity to the Paralyzed and Restrained conditions. You can't be shape-shifted against your will.

###### Source of Life

When a spell you cast with a spell slot restores Hit Points to a creature, that creature regains an additional 1d8 Hit Points on the turn you cast the spell.

###### Spells Enhanced

The spell save DC of your Druid spells increases by 1, and you have Advantage on the attack rolls of Druid spells you cast.

##### Wrath

*Level:* 0

*School:* V

You hurl a ball of energy at a target within range. Make a ranged spell attack against the target. On a hit, the target takes 1d8 Lightning damage, and its Speed is reduced by 10 feet until the end of your next turn.

### Evoker

*[Image: image (classes/WoW/Evoker Crest.webp)]*

*[Image: Dracthyr Evoker (classes/WoW/Evoker.webp)]*

#### Evoker

#### Playing an Evoker

Evoker is a hero class, which means you typically start play at level 3 or higher (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter). To play an evoker, choose the Sorcerer class from the Player's Handbook and the WoW at level 3. Instead of the XPHB subclass feature, you gain the Evoker Spells feature, described below. You can use one of three specializations to guide your choice of class features, spells, Metamagic options, and feats: augmentation, devastation, or preservation.

All evokers in *World of Warcraft* are dracthyr, so check with your DM before playing an evoker of another species.

##### Augmentation Specialization

Evokers who specialize in augmentation imbue their companions with the might of the black dragonflight, amplifying those allies' abilities. They can also bend time and fate to their advantage using the magic of the bronze dragonflight.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW, and see "Evoker Spells" above. Choose the following cantrips: Elementalism, Light, Sorcerous Burst, and Thunderclap. Choose the Augmentation spells from the Evoker Spells table and the following additional prepared spells: Arcane Vigor, Enhance Ability, Flaming Sphere, Ice Knife, Living Flame, and Shield. Choose Empowered Spell and Extended Spell for your Metamagic options.
- **Level 6.** Level 6 Choose Fire for your XPHB.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Elemental Adept (Fire), Speedy, Spell Sniper, and War Caster.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Augmentation Evoker Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 3 | Fireball, Haste, Slow |
| 4 | Fire Shield, Stoneskin |
| 5 | Hold Monster, Wall of Stone |
| 6 | Sunbeam |
| 7 | Reverse Gravity |
| 8 | Earthquake |
| 9 | Time Stop |

##### Devastation Specialization

The devastation specialization uses the chaotic flames of the red dragonflight and the precise arcane power of the blue dragons to bathe the battlefield in destruction. Devastation evokers can rain fire down from the sky on their foes and maneuver quickly around the battlefield.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW, and see "Evoker Spells" above. Choose the following cantrips: Elementalism, Light, Ray of Frost, and Sorcerous Burst. Choose the Devastation spells from the Evoker Spells table and the following additional prepared spells: Arcane Vigor, Burning Hands, Cloud of Daggers, Flame Blade, Ice Knife, and Living Flame. Choose Empowered Spell and Transmuted Spell for your Metamagic options.
- **Level 6.** Level 6 Choose Cold or Fire for your XPHB.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Elemental Adept (Cold and Fire), Spell Sniper, and War Caster.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Devastation Evoker Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 3 | Counterspell, Fly, Sleet Storm |
| 4 | Ice Storm, Wall of Fire |
| 5 | Cone of Cold, Telekinesis |
| 6 | Azure Sweep, Otiluke's Freezing Sphere |
| 7 | Fire Storm, Lay Waste |
| 8 | Incendiary Cloud |
| 9 | Meteor Swarm |

##### Preservation Specialization

Evokers who specialize in preservation wield the power of the Emerald Dream—the fey realm of verdant life tended by the green dragonflight—to rejuvenate injured allies. They also use bronze dragons' magic to manipulate the sands of time, preventing harm and rewinding injury.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW, and see "Evoker Spells" above. Choose the following cantrips: Blade Ward, Elementalism, Light, and Sorcerous Burst. Choose the Preservation spells from the Evoker Spells table and the following additional prepared spells: Alter Self, Arcane Vigor, False Life, Hold Person, Ray of Sickness, and Witch Bolt. Choose Heightened Spell and Twinned Spell for your Metamagic options.
- **Level 6.** Level 6 Choose Lightning or Poison for your XPHB.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Elemental Adept (Lightning and Poison), Spell Sniper, and War Caster.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Preservation Evoker Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 3 | Dispel Magic, Haste, Lightning Bolt |
| 4 | Polymorph, Vitriolic Sphere |
| 5 | Cloudkill, Hold Monster, Rescue |
| 6 | Globe of Invulnerability |
| 7 | Prismatic Spray |
| 8 | Dominate Monster |
| 9 | Time Stop |

##### Azure Strike

*Level:* 1

*School:* V

You fire rays of arcane and frigid energy at up to two targets within range that are within 30 feet of each other. Make a ranged spell attack for each target. On a hit, the target takes 2d6 Force damage, and its Speed is reduced by 10 feet until the end of its next turn.

##### Azure Sweep

*Level:* 6

*School:* V

Pure magic sweeps out from you in a 30-foot Emanation. Creatures of your choice in the Emanation make a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 7d8 Force damage, and its Speed is reduced by 20 feet for the duration. On a successful save, the target takes half as much damage only.

##### Blistering Scales

*Level:* 3

*School:* T

Choose a willing creature you can see within range. Five explosive scales form on the target, granting it a +1 bonus to AC for the duration. If a creature hits the target with a melee attack roll before the spell ends, a scale explodes, and the attacking creature takes 1d8 Fire damage. The spell ends early if the target has no explosive scales.

##### Breath of Eons

*Level:* 4

*School:* A

You draw on the magic of the bronze dragonflight. For the duration, you gain the following benefits.

###### Temporal Wounds

When you move within 5 feet of a creature, you can curse it until the start of your next turn. While a creature is cursed, the next time it takes damage, it takes an extra 1d10 Force damage, and the curse ends. A creature can be cursed this way only once per turn.

###### Wings

You sprout spectral wings from your back if you don't already have wings. You have a Fly Speed of 60 feet and can hover. In addition, your movement doesn't provoke Opportunity Attacks.

##### Dream Breath

*Level:* 2

*School:* A

You exhale verdant healing magic. Each creature of your choice in a 15-foot Cone regains 2d6 Hit Points.

##### Dream Flight

*Level:* 5

*School:* A

You draw on the magic of the green dragonflight. For the duration, you gain the following benefits.

###### Healing Presence

When you move within 5 feet of a creature, you can restore 5d4 Hit Points to that creature. A creature can regain these Hit Points only once per turn.

###### Wings

You sprout spectral wings from your back if you don't already have wings. You have a Fly Speed of 60 feet and can hover. In addition, your movement doesn't provoke Opportunity Attacks.

##### Ebon Might

*Level:* 1

*School:* T

As you invoke the black dragonflight, draconic might erupts from you. Each creature of your choice in a 10-foot Emanation originating from you is empowered until the end of your next turn. While a creature is empowered, when it hits a target with an attack roll, the attack deals an extra 1d4 Force damage.

##### Emberstorm

*Level:* 4

*School:* V

White-hot embers fill a 20-foot-radius Sphere centered on a point you choose within range. Each creature in the Sphere makes a Constitution saving throw, taking 6d8 Fire damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

Creatures and flammable objects in the Sphere that aren't being worn or carried start burning, taking 1d8 Fire damage at the start of each of their turns instead of the normal burning damage.

##### Emerald Blossom

*Level:* 3

*School:* C

You conjure a spectral bulb that sprouts from the ground in an unoccupied space that you can see within range. The bulb lasts for the duration.

The bulb emits healing magic in a 10-foot Emanation at the end of each of your turns. Allies of your choice in the Emanation regain 5 Hit Points. The bulb vanishes when it restores a total of 20 Hit Points.

##### Eruption

*Level:* 2

*School:* V

The ground in a 20-foot-radius Sphere centered on a point within range erupts with flames. Each creature in the Sphere makes a Dexterity saving throw, taking 3d8 Fire damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

The cracks turn the ground in the Sphere into Difficult Terrain until the end of your next turn, at which point they close.

##### Lay Waste

*Level:* 7

*School:* V

You draw a deep breath and spew molten cinders along a 120-foot-long, 10-foot-wide Line originating from you in a direction you choose. Each creature in the Line makes a Dexterity saving throw, taking 12d6 Fire damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

You then teleport to an unoccupied space within 5 feet of the end of the Line.

##### Living Flame

*Level:* 1

*School:* V

Flickering flames hurtle toward a creature you choose within range. Choose one of these options:

- **Healing Flames.** The target regains 2d6 Hit Points.
- **Searing Flames.** Make a ranged attack roll against the target. On a hit, the target takes 2d8 Fire damage.

*[Image: image (book/WoW/014-01-009.noble-evoker.webp)]*

##### Rescue

*Level:* 5

*School:* C

You teleport to an unoccupied space you can see within range. One willing creature within 60 feet of the space you left also teleports to an unoccupied space within 5 feet of your destination space.

##### Shattering Star

*Level:* 5

*School:* V

You exhale a bolt of concentrated power at a creature within range. Make a ranged spell attack against the target. On a hit, the target takes 10d8 Force damage, and it takes a -2 penalty to AC until the start of your next turn as clinging frost makes the target's exterior brittle.

##### Time Dilation

*Level:* 4

*School:* A

The target reduces the damage taken by 4d8. At the end of each of the target's turns, the target loses 1d8 Hit Points until it has lost a total of 4d8 Hit Points (roll these 4d8 separately from the damage reduction).

> > Isn't time funny? Wait, did I say that already?
> > — Chromie
> 

##### Upheaval

*Level:* 4

*School:* V

Fiery stones erupt, filling a 20-foot-radius, 10-foot tall Cylinder originating from a point on the ground you can see within range. Each creature in the Cylinder makes a Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 1d12 Bludgeoning damage plus 3d8 Fire damage and is pushed vertically to the top of the cylinder, at which point the creature falls. On a successful save, a creature takes half as much damage only.

Smoldering stones turn the ground in the Cylinder into Difficult Terrain until the end of your next turn.

### Hunter

*[Image: image (classes/WoW/Hunter Crest.webp)]*

*[Image: Dwarf Hunter with a Bear Companion (classes/WoW/Hunter.webp)]*

#### Hunter

#### Playing a Hunter

To play a hunter, choose the Ranger class from the Player's Handbook. You can choose one of three specializations to guide your choice of subclass, spells, and feats: beast mastery, marksmanship, or survival.

##### Beast Mastery Specialization

From childhood, many hunters have formed profound bonds with the beasts of the wild. These hunters specialize in beast mastery. They are drawn to the primal world and invigorated by the dangerous wilds. Wilderness landscapes become their homes, and ferocious predators become their kin. To aid them in hunting and in battling foes, these hunters summon and command a wide array of vicious animals to fight at their side.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose the following prepared spells: Ensnaring Strike and Speak with Animals. Choose any two of Heavy Crossbows, Longbows, or Muskets for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 2.** Level 2 Choose Archery for a Fighting Style feat.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW. See "Hunter Pets" below.
- **Level 6.** Level 6 Instead of the XPHB class feature, you gain the Pet Specialization feature (see below).
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Crossbow Expert, Medium Armor Master, Piercer, Sharpshooter, and Weapon Master.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Beast Mastery Hunter Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Freezing Trap |
| 2 | Beast Sense, Summon Beast |
| 3 | Conjure Animals, Serpent Sting |
| 4 | Dominate Beast |
| 5 | Conjure Volley |

##### Marksmanship Specialization

Some hunters forgo the companionship of a wild beast, preferring to blend into the natural environment as they wield ranged weapons with deadly skill. Specializing in marksmanship, these hunters learn techniques for stalking and ambushing their prey by observing the deadliest predators of the wilds. They prefer to remain in hiding as they unleash their shots with deadly precision.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose the following prepared spells: Cure Wounds and Ensnaring Strike. Choose any two of Heavy Crossbows, Longbows, or Muskets for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 2.** Level 2 Choose Archery for a Fighting Style feat.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Crossbow Expert, Medium Armor Master, Piercer, and Sharpshooter.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Marksmanship Hunter Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Freezing Trap |
| 2 | Cordon of Arrows, Magic Weapon |
| 3 | Conjure Barrage, Lightning Arrow, Serpent Sting |
| 4 | Conjure Woodland Beings, Grasping Vine |
| 5 | Conjure Volley, Swift Quiver |

##### Survival Specialization

Some hunters are drawn to the most brutal aspects of nature: the unrelenting ferocity of predatory animals and the merciless bloodshed that means survival for the strongest. These hunters prefer close-quarters combat, wielding spears and polearms to bring down their prey. They call on the aid of beast companions, coat their weapons with venom, and lay deceptive traps.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose the following prepared spells: Cure Wounds and Ensnaring Strike. Choose any two of Glaives, Halberds, or Pikes for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 2.** Level 2 Choose Great Weapon Fighting for a Fighting Style feat.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW. See "Hunter Pets" below.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Great Weapon Master, Poisoner, Polearm Master, and Sentinel.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Survival Hunter Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Freezing Trap |
| 2 | Cordon of Arrows, Spike Growth |
| 3 | Serpent Sting |
| 4 | Grasping Vine, Stoneskin |
| 5 | Explosive Trap, Steel Wind Strike |

#### Hunter Pets

As a Beast Master (whether you choose the beast mastery or survival specialization), when you use your XPHB feature to summon a primal beast, you choose the form it takes from among the pet families shown on the table below. Each family has three characteristics:

- **Stat Block.** Stat Block Your primal beast's family determines which one of the stat blocks in the Player's Handbook it uses: Beast of the Land, Beast of the Sea, or Beast of the Sky.
- **Specialization (Spec.).** Specialization (Spec.) Each pet family has a specialization—Cunning (C), Ferocity (F), or Tenacity (T)—that comes into play only if you have the WoW feature.
- **Mastery Property (Mastery).** Mastery Property (Mastery) When you gain the Primal Companion feature and whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can choose your pet's family as one of the weapons for your Weapon Mastery feature. Then its mastery property applies to your primal companion's Beast's Strike attack.

#### Hunter Spells

The following new spells are added to the Ranger spell list for *World of Warcraft* hunters. In the Special column, *C* means the spell requires Concentration.

**Hunter Spells**

| Level | Spell | Special |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Freezing Trap | C |
| 3 | Serpent Sting | — |
| 5 | Explosive Trap | C |

##### Explosive Trap

*Level:* 5

*School:* V

An Invisible, intangible trap appears on the ground at a point you choose within range. The first time a creature comes within 5 feet of the trap, the trap explodes in a fiery burst that fills a 20-foot-radius Sphere centered on the trap. The spell ends, and each creature within the Sphere makes a Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 8d6 Fire damage and, if it is Huge or smaller, is pushed 20 feet straight away from the trap's location.

##### Freezing Trap

*Level:* 1

*School:* V

An Invisible, intangible trap appears on the ground at a point you choose within range. The first time a creature comes within 5 feet of the trap, the trap explodes in a burst of frost, the spell ends, and the triggering creature makes a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the target has the Restrained condition for 1 minute. If the creature takes any damage, the Restrained condition from the trap ends.

##### Serpent Sting

*Level:* 3

*School:* N

The target takes an extra 4d6 Poison damage from the attack, and the target has the Poisoned condition until the spell ends. At the end of each of its turns, the Poisoned target makes a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the creature takes 2d6 Poison damage. On a successful save, the spell ends.

### Mage

#### Mage

#### Playing a Mage

To play a mage, choose the Wizard class from the Player's Handbook. You can choose one of three specializations to guide your choice of spells and feats: arcane, fire, or frost.

Adopting the Wizard class, either at character creation or through multiclassing, increases a character's Renown Score with the Kirin Tor by 3 (see x).

##### Arcane Specialization

Arcane mages manipulate raw arcane magic, destroying enemies with overwhelming power.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose the following cantrips: Light, Mage Hand, and Thunderclap. Choose these spells for your spellbook: Detect Magic, Feather Fall, Mage Armor, Magic Missile, Shield, and Thunderwave.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Clearcasting, Spell Sniper, and War Caster.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you add spells to your spellbook.

**Arcane Mage Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Chromatic Orb, Comprehend Languages, Disguise Self, Find Familiar, Frost Nova |
| 2 | Enhance Ability, Invisibility, Mirror Image, Misty Step, Shatter |
| 3 | Blink, Counterspell, Create Food and Water, Dispel Magic, Haste, Remove Curse, Slow, Tongues |
| 4 | Conjure Minor Elementals, Fire Shield, Greater Invisibility, Otiluke's Resilient Sphere, Polymorph |
| 5 | Bigby's Hand, Teleportation Circle, Wall of Force |

| 7 | Forcecage, Mass Polymorph, Mordenkainen's Sword, Prismatic Spray, Teleport |
| 8 | Befuddlement, Maze |
| 9 | Prismatic Wall, Time Stop |

##### Fire Specialization

Fire mages focus the essence of fire magic, assaulting enemies with combustive flames.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose the following cantrips: Light, Mage Hand, and Fire Bolt. Choose the following spells for your spellbook: Burning Hands, Detect Magic, Feather Fall, Mage Armor, Shield, and Thunderwave.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Elemental Adept, Hot Streak, Spell Sniper, and War Caster.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you add spells to your spellbook.

| 3 | Counterspell, Create Food and Water, Dispel Magic, Fireball, Haste, Remove Curse, Slow, Tongues |
| 4 | Conjure Minor Elementals, Fire Shield, Greater Invisibility, Polymorph, Wall of Fire |

##### Frost Specialization

Frost mages freeze enemies in their tracks and shatter them with frost magic.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose the following cantrips: Light, Mage Hand, and Ray of Frost. Choose the following spells for your spellbook: Detect Magic, Feather Fall, Frost Nova, Ice Knife, Mage Armor, and Shield.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Fingers of Frost, Spell Sniper, and War Caster.

#### Mage Feats

Clearcasting, Fingers of Frost, and Hot Streak are new General feats intended primarily for arcane mages, frost mages, and fire mages, respectively.

##### Clearcasting

*Ability Score Increase:* Intelligence +1

*Prerequisite:* {'level': 4}

You gain the following benefits.

###### Clearcasting

When you score a Critical Hit with a spell's attack roll, you can take a Reaction to cast a cast time=action.

###### Force Adept

When you roll damage for a spell you cast that deals Force damage, you can treat any 1 on a damage die as a 2.

##### Fingers of Frost

*Ability Score Increase:* Intelligence +1

*Prerequisite:* {'level': 4}

You gain the following benefits.

###### Cold Mastery

Spells you cast ignore Resistance to Cold damage.

###### Frostbite

When you deal Cold damage to a creature that has the Restrained condition, the creature takes extra Cold damage. To determine the extra damage, roll a number of d4s equal to your Proficiency Bonus and add them together.

##### Hot Streak

*Ability Score Increase:* Intelligence +1

*Prerequisite:* {'level': 4}

You gain the following benefits.

###### Fire Mastery

Spells you cast ignore Resistance to Fire damage.

###### Ignite

When a spell you cast deals Fire damage to a creature, the target starts burning. When you deal Fire damage to a creature that is already burning, the flames become more severe, increasing the burning damage by 1d4.

#### Mage Spells

The following spells from the Player's Handbook and new spells (marked with an asterisk) are added to the Wizard spell list for *World of Warcraft* mages. In the Special column, *C* means the spell requires Concentration and *M* means it requires a specific Material component.

**Mage Spells**

| Level | Spell | Special |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Frost Nova* | — |
| 3 | Create Food and Water | — |
| 3 | Flurry* | — |
| 5 | Flame Strike | — |
| 6 | Combustion* | — |
| 6 | Heroes' Feast | M |
