##### Combustion

*Level:* 6

*School:* V

You engulf yourself in flames for the duration, shedding Bright Light in a 20-foot radius and Dim Light for an additional 10 feet. These flames grant you Immunity to Fire damage. While they last, you have Advantage on spell attack rolls.

At the end of each of your turns, each creature of your choice in a 5-foot Emanation originating from you takes 3d8 Fire damage.

##### Flurry

*Level:* 3

*School:* V

You hurl three shards of ice at one target within range or at several. Make a ranged spell attack for each shard. On a hit, the target takes 3d6 Cold damage. On a Critical Hit, the target has the Restrained condition until the end of your next turn.

##### Frost Nova

*Level:* 1

*School:* V

You unleash a burst of frost, creating a 15-foot Emanation originating from you. Each creature of your choice in the Emanation makes a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 2d6 Cold damage, and it has the Restrained condition until the end of your next turn. On a successful save, the creature takes half as much damage only.

##### Mass Polymorph

*Level:* 9

*School:* T

You attempt to transform any number of creatures in a 30-foot Emanation originating from you into identical Beasts. Each creature in the Emanation must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw or shapeshift into a Beast form for the duration. A creature that isn't a Beast, a Giant, a Humanoid, or a Monstrosity automatically succeeds on its saving throw.

The new form can be any miscellaneous=!swarm you choose that has a Challenge Rating of 0 (Goat is recommended). Each target's game statistics are replaced by the stat block of the chosen Beast, but the target retains its alignment, personality, creature type, Hit Points, and Hit Point Dice.

Each target gains a number of Temporary Hit Points equal to the Hit Points of the Beast form. The spell ends early on the target if it has no Temporary Hit Points left.

The targets are limited in the actions they can perform by the anatomy of their new form, and they can't speak or cast spells.

Each target's gear melds into the new form. The creature can't use or otherwise benefit from any of that equipment.

### Monk

*[Image: image (classes/WoW/Monk Crest.webp)]*

*[Image: Chen Stormstout, a Pandaren Monk (classes/WoW/Monk.webp)]*

#### Monk

#### Playing a Monk

To play a monk, choose the Monk class from the Player's Handbook. You can choose one of three specializations to guide your choice of subclass and feats: brewmaster, mistweaver, or windwalker.

##### Brewmaster Specialization

Brewmaster monks inhabit the murky space between intoxication and the appearance of intoxication. They use a combination of powerful concoctions and unpredictable, staggering movements to enhance their own attacks and defenses and keep enemies off guard. Deceived by a brewmaster's apparent intoxication, enemies barely have time to process the monk's unpredictable behavior before being laid low by careful fist strikes.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the Warrior of Mystic Brews subclass.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Chef, Crusher, Elusive Brawler, Grappler, Sentinel, and Stagger and Sway.

##### Mistweaver Specialization

Monks originated in Pandaria, where some of them learned from a Wild God called Yu'lon the Jade Serpent how to draw power from the magical mists that shrouded the continent for millennia. By weaving the mists, these monks wield the power of life's essence to heal wounds and sap their foes' life energy.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Enveloping Mist, Observant, Poisoner, Shadow Touched, and Skulker.

##### Windwalker Specialization

Windwalker monks are the quintessential martial artists, fighting with mastery and grace. Through a combination of intense physical training and mystical discipline, they can unleash a dizzying flurry of punches and kicks to devastate their enemies.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Charger, Crusher, Fists of Fury, and Speedy.

#### Warrior of Mystic Brews (Monk Subclass)

> **Monastic Orders of Pandaria**
> Monks undergo rigorous physical and spiritual training within monasteries. Even those who learn a martial art from traveling masters can trace their art's origin back to a monastic order. The most notable monastic orders are the following.
> ###### Crane Wing Order
> Under the guidance of the August Celestial Chi-Ji (one of the Wild Gods who watch over Pandaria), monks practice their art in the Temple of the Red Crane in Pandaria's Krasarang Wilds. Chi-Ji's teachings emphasize physical and emotional adaptability.
> ###### Niuzao Sentinels
> A martial order serving the August Celestial Niuzao maintains a temple in Pandaria's Townlong Steppes. The stern sentinels of this order undergo rigorous emotional and physical training to ward against fear as they keep vigil over the Sha of Fear imprisoned in the catacombs beneath the temple.
> ###### Order of the Broken Temple
> When the Burning Legion destroyed the Peak of Serenity in Pandaria, the survivors formed the Order of the Broken Temple on the mysterious Wandering Isle. They welcome monks of all kinds in an effort to hold strong against the Burning Legion.
> ###### Order of the Cloud Serpent
> Some monks bond with Pandaria's flying cloud serpents to defend the Jade Forest. By cultivating hatchling cloud serpents and shepherding their growth to adulthood, these monks learn patience to temper their ambitions. The windwalker specialization represents these monks' bond with the skies.
> ###### Shado-Pan
> The Watchers on the Wall are elite monks who protect Pandaria's peaceful way of life from internal and external threats. They operate across their realm from the Shado-Pan Monastery in Kun-Lai Summit and move through the shadows to strike suddenly against any foe.

#### Monk Feats

Elusive Brawler, Enveloping Mist, Fists of Fury, and Stagger and Sway are new General feats intended primarily for monks.

##### Elusive Brawler

*Ability Score Increase:* +1 to 1 of your choice from: Dexterity, Wisdom

*Prerequisite:* {'level': 4, 'ability': [{'dex': 13, 'wis': 13}]}

You gain the following benefits.

###### Reactive Dodge

When you take damage, you can take a Reaction to take the Dodge action.

##### Enveloping Mist

*Ability Score Increase:* +1 to 1 of your choice from: Dexterity, Wisdom

*Prerequisite:* {'level': 4, 'ability': [{'dex': 13, 'wis': 13}]}

You gain the following benefits.

###### Enveloping Mist

When you cast a spell or use a feature to restore Hit Points to a creature, the creature is wrapped in a healing mist. Roll one of the dice from the feature that healed it, and note that number as the creature's Life Cocoon value.

If the creature is hit by an attack roll before the start of its next turn, you can take a Reaction to reduce the damage it takes from that attack by its Life Cocoon value. The mist then disappears.

At the start of the creature's next turn, if the mist persists, the creature gains Temporary Hit Points equal to its Life Cocoon value.

##### Fists of Fury

*Ability Score Increase:* +1 to 1 of your choice from: Dexterity, Wisdom

*Prerequisite:* {'level': 4, 'ability': [{'dex': 13, 'wis': 13}]}

You gain the following benefits.

###### Clean Hits

When you roll damage for an attack you make with an Unarmed Strike or a weapon that has the Light property, you can treat any 1 on a damage die as a 2.

###### Combo Strikes

When you deal damage to a creature with an Unarmed Strike, you gain a +1 bonus to the damage roll for each time you previously hit that creature with a melee attack roll in the same turn.

##### Stagger and Sway

*Ability Score Increase:* +1 to 1 of your choice from: Dexterity, Wisdom

*Prerequisite:* {'level': 4, 'ability': [{'dex': 13, 'wis': 13}]}

You gain the following benefits.

###### Defer Damage

When you take damage, reduce the damage you take by your Dexterity modifier (minimum of 1). Then add a d6 to your Delayed Reaction pool. At the end of your turn, remove all the dice from your Delayed Reaction pool and roll them. You take Force damage equal to the total rolled.

###### Purify

As a Bonus Action, you can remove a number of dice equal to your Wisdom modifier from your Delayed Reaction pool.

### Paladin

*[Image: image (classes/WoW/Paladin Crest.webp)]*

*[Image: Blood Elf Paladin (classes/WoW/Paladin.webp)]*

#### Paladin

#### Playing a Paladin

To play a paladin, choose the Paladin class from the Player's Handbook. You can choose one of three specializations to guide your choice of subclass, spells, and feats: holy, protection, or retribution.

##### Holy Specialization

Holy paladins spend much of their lives in hallowed halls studying divine doctrine, learning to stand as beacons of the Light for their allies. They believe that evil must be eradicated from the world so that the Light can prevail, and they serve all who are engaged in this holy struggle. The Light, in turn, imbues holy paladins with the power to heal and revitalize their comrades.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Prepare the following level 1 spells to start: Cure Wounds and Shield of Faith. Choose at least one of Flails, Longswords, Morningstars, or Warhammers for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 2.** Level 2 Choose Blessed Warrior instead of a Fighting Style feat, and choose Sacred Flame and Spare the Dying as your cantrips.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the Oath of the Holy subclass.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Durable, Healer, Inspiring Leader, Shield Master, and War Caster.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Holy Paladin Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Bless, Heroism, Searing Smite |
| 2 | Aid, Warding Bond |
| 3 | Crusader's Mantle, Revivify |
| 4 | Aura of Purity, Divine Shield |
| 5 | Circle of Power, Raise Dead |

> **War Clerics**
> Although D&D's Paladin class is the best match for a paladin in *World of Warcraft*, if you want to play your paladin as a dedicated healer, you could also play a Cleric. Choose the WoW, and otherwise follow the guidance for building a holy paladin as a Paladin.

##### Protection Specialization

Stalwart and steadfast, paladins who specialize in protection wield a weapon of righteous judgment in one hand and a shield of holy protection in the other. These paladins are ardent defenders of the Light and all that it touches, and the radiance of the Light invigorates and heals them in turn.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Prepare the following level 1 spells to start: Compelled Duel and Searing Smite. Choose at least one of Battleaxes, Flails, Longswords, Morningstars, or Warhammers for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 2.** Level 2 Choose Interception or Protection as your Fighting Style feat.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Durable, Heavy Armor Master, Sentinel, Shield Master, and Tough.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

##### Retribution Specialization

The most dedicated paladins can become fanatical in their devotion, acting as instruments of retribution against those who dare to defy the Light. These paladins are vengeful guardians of the weak and crusaders who judge and punish the wicked. Their resolute conviction in the divine order of all things assures them that the Light's victory is inevitable, and so they fight to their last breath to ensure that the Light will prevail.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Prepare the following level 1 spells to start: Heroism and Wrathful Smite. Choose at least one of Greataxes, Greatswords, or Mauls for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 2.** Level 2 Choose Great Weapon Fighting as your Fighting Style feat.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Crusher, Great Weapon Master, and Slasher.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

| 1 | Divine Favor, Thunderous Smite |
| 2 | Consecration, Shining Smite |
| 3 | Crusader's Mantle, Elemental Weapon |
| 4 | Staggering Smite |
| 5 | Destructive Wave |

### Priest

*[Image: image (classes/WoW/Priest Crest.webp)]*

*[Image: Prophet Velen, a Draenei Priest (classes/WoW/Priest.webp)]*

#### Priest

#### Playing a Priest

To play a priest, choose the Cleric class from the Player's Handbook. You can choose one of three specializations to guide your choice of subclass, spells, and other features: discipline, holy, or shadow. These specializations reflect varying degrees of devotion to the cosmic forces of Light and Shadow, with holy priests focusing on the Light, shadow priests drawing primarily on Shadow, and discipline priests balancing the two.

##### Discipline Specialization

Some priests pride themselves on pragmatism. They understand that light casts a shadow, that darkness is defined by light, and that true discipline stems from one's ability to balance these opposing powers in service of a greater cause. While these priests possess many holy virtues to aid their allies, they also dabble in the dark arts to debilitate their enemies—exercising immense discipline to prevent themselves from delving too far into the shadows. Many discipline priests believe the ends justify the means; they maximize their effectiveness by studying scriptures both virtuous and vile.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Thaumaturge for your XPHB. Choose the following cantrips: Guidance, Resistance, Thaumaturgy, and Word of Radiance. Prepare the following spells to start: Cure Wounds, Guiding Bolt, Sanctuary, and Shield of Faith.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the Discipline Domain subclass.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Durable, Inspiring Leader, Mage Slayer, and War Caster.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider these spells as you gain levels.

**Discipline Priest Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Bless, Protection from Evil and Good, Shadow Word: Pain |
| 2 | Aid, Blur, Levitate, Spiritual Weapon |
| 3 | Aura of Vitality, Dispel Magic, Spirit Guardians |
| 4 | Aura of Purity, Holy Nova |
| 5 | Flame Strike, Greater Restoration, Summon Celestial |
| 6 | Blade Barrier, Sunbeam |
| 7 | Fire Storm, Power Word Fortify |
| 8 | Sunburst |
| 9 | Gate |

##### Holy Specialization

After spending much of their lives in temples studying ancient doctrine, preaching the tenets of their faith, and pledging their devotion to the divine powers they follow, the most adept priests leave their houses of worship to serve on the battlefield. There, they use their holy powers to bless allies and mend wounds. And while most stay behind the front lines to aid their comrades, these holy champions can also smite foes and carry out sacred justice. Holy priests are morally opposed to shadow magic. They are exemplars of the incredible grace and power of the divine and the Light.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Thaumaturge for your XPHB. Choose the following cantrips: Guidance, Light, Sacred Flame, and Spare the Dying. Prepare the following spells to start: Bless, Cure Wounds, Healing Word, and Purify Food and Drink.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Chef, Healer, Inspiring Leader, and War Caster.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Holy Priest Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Detect Evil and Good, Protection from Evil and Good, Shadow Word: Pain |
| 2 | Blur, Calm Emotions, Prayer of Healing, Protection from Poison |
| 3 | Beacon of Hope, Daylight, Prayer of Mending |
| 4 | Aura of Purity, Guardian of Faith, Holy Nova |
| 5 | Commune, Dispel Evil and Good, Raise Dead |
| 6 | Heal, Sunbeam |
| 7 | Conjure Celestial, Regenerate |
| 8 | Holy Aura |
| 9 | Apotheosis, Mass Heal |

##### Shadow Specialization

The Light in which many priests bathe is brilliant and effervescent, granting them immense divine power. But the brightest light casts the darkest shadow—and within this shadow, a rival power dwells. Shadow priests fully embrace this polarity, their resolute faith focused on shadowy magics and mental manipulation. They derive their power from the Void, straying dangerously close to the domain of the Old Gods and its ancient, corruptive influence. This is the state in which these dark priests thrive, feeding off of the minds of their opponents to reach terrifying new limits.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Thaumaturge for your XPHB. Choose the following cantrips: Mending, Resistance, Thaumaturgy, and Toll the Dead. Prepare the following spells to start: Bane, Command, Healing Word, and Shadow Word: Pain.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the Shadow Domain subclass.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Actor, Mage Slayer, Shadow Touched, Skulker, and Telepathic.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Shadow Priest Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Detect Poison and Disease |
| 2 | Blindness/Deafness, Blur, Hold Person, Silence |
| 3 | Bestow Curse, Dispel Magic, Speak with Dead |
| 4 | Banishment, Freedom of Movement, Locate Creature |
| 5 | Contagion, Geas, Insect Plague |
| 6 | Create Undead, Harm |
| 7 | Etherealness, Plane Shift |
| 8 | Antimagic Field |
| 9 | Astral Projection |

#### Priest Spells

The following spells from the Player's Handbook and new spells (marked with an asterisk) are added to the Cleric spell list for *World of Warcraft* priests. In the Special column, *C* means the spell requires Concentration and *M* means it requires a specific Material component.

**Priest Spells**

| Level | Spell | Special |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cantrip | Mind Sliver | — |

| 2 | Blur | C |

##### Apotheosis

*Level:* 9

*School:* T

You transform into a spectral, angelic form that grants you the following benefits.

###### Angelic Wings

You have a Fly Speed of 90 feet.

###### Spectral Form

You have Resistance to Bludgeoning, Piercing, Radiant, and Slashing damage.

###### Holy Empowerment

Whenever you cast a spell using a spell slot that restores Hit Points to one or more creatures or deals Radiant damage, determine the spell's effects as if you had used a spell slot 2 levels higher than the spell slot you used to cast it (maximum of level 9).

##### Holy Nova

*Level:* 4

*School:* V

An explosion of holy light surrounds you in a 30-foot Emanation, harming your enemies and healing your allies. Each creature of your choice in the Emanation makes a Constitution saving throw, taking 5d6 Radiant damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. Each other creature in the Emanation regains 3d6 Hit Points.

##### Prayer of Mending

*Level:* 3

*School:* A

You place a holy ward on one willing creature in range. The next time that creature takes damage, the ward activates, and the creature regains Hit Points equal to 1d8 plus your spellcasting ability modifier. The ward then jumps to another ally of your choice within 30 feet of the warded creature, with the same effect. If no ally is in range, the spell ends, and it ends after the ward has healed five times.

##### Psychic Horror

*Level:* 4

*School:* E

A creature within range is overwhelmed with mental visions of the Void and makes a Wisdom saving throw. On a failed save, the target takes 2d10 Psychic damage and has the Frightened condition for 1 minute. A Frightened creature also has the Stunned condition until the end of your next turn. At the end of each of its turns, the target repeats the save, ending the effect on itself on a success. On a successful save, the creature takes half as much damage only.

##### Shadow Word: Pain

*Level:* 1

*School:* N

Speaking a word of Void power, you cause one creature in range to suffer agonizing pain. The target makes a Wisdom saving throw. On a failed save, the target takes Psychic damage equal to 1d10 plus your spellcasting ability modifier and is cursed for the duration. On a successful save, it takes half as much damage only.

Until the curse ends, the target takes 1d10 Psychic damage at the start of each of its turns. It repeats the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the curse on a success.

##### Void Eruption

*Level:* 5

*School:* N

Shadowy Void energy erupts in a 20-foot-radius Sphere centered on a point you choose within range. Each creature in the Sphere makes a Constitution saving throw, taking 5d6 Necrotic damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. At the same time, you take on a form of Void that grants you the following benefits for the duration.

###### Void Bolt

As a Bonus Action on your turn, you can hurl a bolt of Void energy at a creature within 60 feet of yourself. Make a ranged spell attack; on a hit, the target takes Necrotic damage equal to 2d6 plus your spellcasting ability modifier.

###### Void Stealth

While you are in an area of Dim Light or Darkness, you have the Invisible condition.

> **Faiths of the Light and Shadow**
> Priests in Azeroth belong to several notable faiths related to the Light and Shadow.
> - **Holy Light.** Holy Light Many peoples across Azeroth revere the Holy Light as an abstract force or as a personified being. The Church of the Holy Light is the most prominent religious body in the world, devoted to the pure ideals of the Light as revealed in visions sent by the naaru. This faith is predominant among humans, dwarves, gnomes, high and blood elves, and draenei. A similar faith, the Church of the Sacred Flame, was brought east across the sea by the humans and high elves who founded the Empire of Arathor.
> - **Sun and Moon.** Sun and Moon Tauren revere the sun deity An'she as the right eye of the Earth Mother, with the moon (a being of Life), Mu'she, being her left eye. Night elves also revere a moon deity, Elune, who is associated with both the power of nature and the Holy Light.
> - **Old Gods.** Old Gods Most priests view Shadow as a dark mirror of the Light, but some revere it in the form of Old Gods or void lords. The Twilight's Hammer is the most widespread cult of the Old Gods, but less destructive faiths also draw on Shadow power. Most notable among these is the Cult of the Forgotten Shadow, founded by Forsaken priests who believed themselves abandoned or betrayed by the Light. Followers of the Forgotten Shadow wield Void power but believe it's important to understand the Light as well, balancing these opposed forces.
> - **Lok'osh.** Lok'osh Many orc priests practice Lok'osh, a path of healing and renewal that draws on "songs of life and shadow" found within each practitioner's heart and spirit. Priests within this tradition focus on Light and Void powers to varying degrees, but the path of the Lok'osh prevents priests from straying too far into the corruption of the Void.
> 

### Rogue

*[Image: image (classes/WoW/Rogue Crest.webp)]*

*[Image: Worgen Rogue (classes/WoW/Rogue.webp)]*

#### Rogue

#### Playing a Rogue

To play a rogue, choose the Rogue class from the Player's Handbook. You can choose one of three specializations to guide your choice of subclass and feats: assassination, outlaw, or subtlety.

##### Assassination Specialization

Rogues who specialize in assassination use poison extensively, often wielding a pair of daggers coated with deadly toxins.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Daggers and Shortbows for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Level 5.** Level 5 Frequently use the Poison option for your XPHB.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Actor, Alert, Defensive Duelist, Piercer, Poisoner, Skulker, and Speedy.

##### Outlaw Specialization

Rogues with the outlaw specialization are ruthless swashbucklers who use agility and guile to dominate in combat while standing toe-to-toe with their enemies. Many of these rogues are affiliated with outlaw bands like the Defias Brotherhood and the pirates of Booty Bay.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Daggers and Rapiers for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the Swashbuckler subclass.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Athlete, Defensive Duelist, Martial Weapon Training, Piercer, Speedy, and Tough.

##### Subtlety Specialization

Rogues who specialize in subtlety draw on the supernatural magic of the shadows to cloak themselves in darkness and form deadly blades. They strike from hiding, incapacitate their enemies with an overwhelming assault of rapid attacks, and disappear just as quickly back into the shadows.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Daggers and Maces for your Weapon Mastery.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Dual Wielder, Mage Slayer, Shadow Touched, and Skulker.

#### Swashbuckler (Rogue Subclass)

### Shaman

*[Image: image (classes/WoW/Shaman Crest.webp)]*

*[Image: Vol'jin, a Troll Shadow Hunter Who Wields Shamanic Magic (classes/WoW/Shaman.webp)]*

#### Shaman

#### Playing a Shaman

To play a shaman, choose the Barbarian class from the Player's Handbook. At level 3, you gain the Totems feature described below instead of the XPHB feature. You can choose one of three specializations to guide your choice of subclass, feats, and weapons: elemental, enhancement, or restoration.

Adopting the Barbarian class as a shaman, either at character creation or through multiclassing, increases a character's Renown Score with the Earthen Ring by 3 (see x).

##### Level 3: Totems

This feature replaces the Barbarian's XPHB feature.

You can coalesce elemental spirits into a totem that aids you and your allies. As a Bonus Action, you can plant one of the totems below into the ground in an unoccupied space you can see within 10 feet of yourself.

A totem is a Small object that has AC 10 and 1 Hit Point and lasts for 1 minute, until it drops to 0 Hit Points, or until you use this feature again. At the end of each of your turns, the totem pulses with elemental energy that fills a 30-foot Emanation.

###### Earthbind Totem

Each enemy in the Emanation has its Speed reduced by 10 feet until the end of your next turn.

###### Primal Knowledge Totem

Each ally in the Emanation is empowered with nature's focus until the end of your next turn. While empowered in this way, whenever the creature makes an ability check using one of the following skills, the creature can make it as a Strength check even if it normally uses a different ability: Acrobatics, Intimidation, Perception, Stealth, or Survival. After you plant this totem, you can't do so again until you finish a Long Rest. You can also restore your use of this totem by expending one use of your XPHB (no action required).

###### Tremor Totem

Each ally in the Emanation has Immunity to the Charmed and Frightened conditions until the end of your next turn. If the creature was already Charmed or Frightened, those conditions are suppressed until the end of its next turn.

###### Wind Rush Totem

Each ally in the Emanation has its Speed increased by 10 feet until the end of your next turn.

##### Elemental Specialization

Shaman with the elemental specialization dedicate themselves to forging deep bonds with the elements. Inspired by visions of an ancient past where manifestations of volatile energy once raged unimpeded across primordial Azeroth, these shaman devote themselves to channeling such power into destructive magical surges. The raw elemental energy of storms—lightning, fire, and cold—flow through their bodies as they summon spirits to their aid.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose any two of Battleaxes, Longswords, or Maces for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the Path of the Elements subclass. You also gain the Totems feature in place of the Barbarian's XPHB feature, as described above.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Athlete, Crusher, Durable, Medium Armor Master, Ritual Caster, and Slasher.

##### Enhancement Specialization

Shaman who specialize in enhancement empower their physical attacks with elemental energy as they wade into the thick of combat. They supplement their augmented weapon attacks with potent elemental strikes and totems.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose any two of Handaxes, Light Hammers, Scimitars, or Shortswords for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the Path of the Spiritual Guardian subclass. You also gain the Totems feature in place of the Barbarian's XPHB feature, as described above.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Athlete, Charger, Crusher, Dual Wielder, Durable, Medium Armor Master, and Slasher.

##### Restoration Specialization

Shaman who specialize in restoration find a serene affinity for the restorative properties of water. They feel a profound spiritual connection with the source from which all mortal life takes root, and they wield that power to restore vitality to their allies. These shaman balance this healing focus with a command of the other elements, finding harmony in nature and purifying their allies as a tidal surge washes across a sandy shore.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose any two of Flails, Maces, or Warhammers for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW. You also gain the Totems feature in place of the Barbarian's XPHB feature, as described above.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Chef, Crusher, Durable, Inspiring Leader, Medium Armor Master, Sentinel, and Shield Master.

### Warlock

#### Warlock

#### Playing a Warlock

To play a warlock, choose the Warlock class from the Player's Handbook. You can choose one of three specializations to guide your choice of subclass, spells, and Eldritch Invocations: affliction, demonology, or destruction.

##### Affliction Specialization

Affliction warlocks draw primarily on the power of the Void, a power often called shadow magic. Their spells cause intense pain, corrupting minds and agonizing souls, leaving their enemies in a state of ongoing torment.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization (Eldritch Invocations marked with an asterisk are described in "x" in this section):

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose the Pact of the Legion* Eldritch Invocation. Choose the following cantrips: Eldritch Blast and Poison Spray. Prepare the following spells to start: Hex and Witch Bolt.
- **Level 2.** Level 2 Choose these Eldritch Invocations: Agonizing Blast and Curse of Corruption.*
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Eldritch Invocations.** Eldritch Invocations Consider the following Eldritch Invocations as you reach level 5 and higher: Call of the Void,* Eldritch Mind, Gift of the Depths, Legion Summoning,* and Visions of Distant Realms.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

| Spell Level | Spells |

| 2 | Ray of Enfeeblement |

| 4 | Blight |
| 5 | Synaptic Static |
| 6 | Eyebite |
| 7 | Finger of Death |
| 8 | Befuddlement |
| 9 | Weird |

##### Demonology Specialization

Demonology warlocks harness the power of demons on the field of battle. They harvest soul energy from their foes and use it to pull abominations from the chaos of the Twisting Nether, maintaining absolute control over the summoned creatures.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization (Eldritch Invocations marked with an asterisk are described in "x" in this section):

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose the Pact of the Legion* Eldritch Invocation. Choose the following cantrips: Eldritch Blast and Toll the Dead. Prepare the following spells to start: Arms of Hadar and Hex.
- **Level 2.** Level 2 Choose these Eldritch Invocations: Health Funnel* and Repelling Blast.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Eldritch Invocations:.** Eldritch Invocations: Consider the following Eldritch Invocations as you reach level 5 and higher: Call of the Void,* Expendable Minions,* Gift of the Depths, Legion Summoning,* Master Demonologist,* and Visions of Distant Realms.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

> **Summon Fiend**
> When you cast Summon Fiend as a Warlock, the fiendish spirit resembles a demon of the Burning Legion: an infernal (choose the Devil option), a demonic tyrant (choose the Yugoloth option), or a vilefiend (choose the Demon option).

##### Destruction Specialization

Destruction warlocks favor magic of pure chaos and aggression in battle. They wield shadow, fire, fel, and raw chaos magic with equal ease, rattling the souls and immolating the flesh of their foes. They revel in the sheer destruction they cause, thrilling at the opportunity to watch the world burn.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization (Eldritch Invocations marked with an asterisk are described in "x" in this section):

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose the Pact of the Legion* Eldritch Invocation. Choose the following cantrips: Eldritch Blast and Thunderclap. Prepare the following spells to start: Hellish Rebuke and Hex.
- **Level 2.** Level 2 Choose these Eldritch Invocations: Agonizing Blast and Repelling Blast.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Eldritch Invocations:.** Eldritch Invocations: Consider the following Eldritch Invocations as you reach level 5 and higher: Call of the Void,* Gift of the Depths, Legion Summoning,* and Visions of Distant Realms.
- **Spells.** Spells Consider the spells on the table below as you gain levels.

**Destruction Warlock Spells**

| Spell Level | Spells |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Arms of Hadar |
| 2 | Cloud of Daggers |
| 3 | Rain of Fire |
| 4 | Blight |
| 5 | Jallarzi's Storm of Radiance |
| 6 | Circle of Death |
| 7 | Finger of Death |
| 8 | Power Word Stun |
| 9 | Power Word Kill |

#### Warlock Spells

The following new spells are added to the Warlock spell list for *World of Warcraft* warlocks. In the Special column, *C* means the spell requires Concentration.

**Warlock Spells**

| Level | Spell | Special |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2 | Curse of Agony | — |
| 3 | Hand of Gul'dan | C |
| 3 | Rain of Fire | C |

##### Curse of Agony

*Level:* 2

*School:* N

You curse one creature within range with excruciating pain. The target makes a Constitution saving throw; the spell's damage starts at 1d4 Psychic damage. On a failed save, the target takes the spell's damage, and the spell's damage die increases: from 1d4 to 1d6, 1d6 to 1d8, 1d8 to 1d10, or 1d10 to 1d12 (the maximum). On a successful save, the target takes the spell's damage, and the effect ends. The target repeats the save at the end of each of its turns.

##### Hand of Gul'dan

*Level:* 3

*School:* C

A demonic meteor hurtles from you to a point you choose within range and then erupts. Each creature in a 20-foot-radius Sphere centered on that point makes a Dexterity saving throw, taking 2d8 Fire damage plus 2d8 Necrotic damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

Demonic spirits resembling fel imps flit about the Sphere for the duration. A creature that enters the Sphere or ends its turn there makes a Dexterity saving throw, taking 1d8 Fire damage plus 1d8 Necrotic damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. A creature can be affected by this spell only once per turn.

##### Rain of Fire

*Level:* 3

*School:* V

Hellfire rains down in a 20-foot-radius, 40-foot-high Cylinder centered on a point within range. Until the spell ends, you can take a Magic action on later turns to move the Cylinder up to 60 feet.

When the Cylinder appears, each creature in it makes a Dexterity saving throw, taking 3d8 Fire damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. A creature also makes this save when the spell's area moves into its space and when it enters the spell's area or ends its turn there. A creature makes this save only once per turn.

You have Advantage on spell attack rolls against a creature in the Cylinder, and you deal an extra 1d6 Necrotic damage to the target if you hit.

#### Eldritch Invocations

Several new Eldritch Invocations are available to Warlocks.

##### Call of the Void

*Prerequisite:* {'level': {'level': 5, 'class': {'name': 'Warlock', 'source': 'XPHB', 'visibleStats': True}}, 'optionalfeature': ['pact of the legion|wow']}

When you cast Find Familiar using Pact of the Legion, you can choose to summon a Fel Imp familiar or a Voidwalker. In addition, you gain the following benefits.

###### Quick Attack

As a Bonus Action, you can command the familiar to take the Attack action.

###### Your Spell Attack

The familiar uses your spell attack bonus for its attacks.

###### Shadow Shield

When your Voidwalker familiar takes damage, you can take a Reaction to grant it Resistance against that damage.

##### Curse of Corruption

*Prerequisite:* {'level': {'level': 2, 'class': {'name': 'Warlock', 'source': 'XPHB', 'visibleStats': True}}}

When an enemy starts its turn cursed by a Warlock spell you cast (such as Hex), it must succeed on a Charisma saving throw or take Necrotic damage equal to your Charisma modifier.

##### Expendable Minions

*Prerequisite:* {'level': {'level': 9, 'class': {'name': 'Warlock', 'source': 'XPHB', 'visibleStats': True}}}

When your familiar or a summoned creature you control dies, you or another creature within 60 feet of you regains Hit Points equal to your Warlock level plus your Charisma modifier.

##### Health Funnel

*Prerequisite:* {'level': {'level': 2, 'class': {'name': 'Warlock', 'source': 'XPHB', 'visibleStats': True}}}

As a Bonus Action, you can expend any number of your unexpended Hit Dice to roll those dice, add your Charisma modifier to each die, and total the rolls. Your familiar or another Fiend you command gains that many Temporary Hit Points.

##### Legion Summoning

*Prerequisite:* {'level': {'level': 9, 'class': {'name': 'Warlock', 'source': 'XPHB', 'visibleStats': True}}, 'optionalfeature': ['pact of the legion|wow']}

You can cast Planar Binding without expending a spell slot. When you do, you summon a Felguard, a Felhound, an Incubus, or a Succubus, and that Fiend becomes the target of the spell. Once you cast the spell in this way, you can't do so again until you finish a Long Rest.

##### Master Demonologist

*Prerequisite:* {'level': {'level': 5, 'class': {'name': 'Warlock', 'source': 'XPHB', 'visibleStats': True}}}

Any Fiends you command also deal extra damage to the target of your Hex spell as if they were you.

##### Pact of the Legion

You learn the Find Familiar spell and can cast it as a Magic action without expending a spell slot. When you cast the spell, you summon a Fel Imp familiar.

Additionally, when you take the Attack action, you can forgo one of your own attacks to allow your familiar to make one attack with its Reaction.

### Warrior

*[Image: image (classes/WoW/Warrior Crest.webp)]*

*[Image: An Orc Warrior (classes/WoW/Warrior.webp)]*

#### Warrior

#### Playing a Warrior

To play a warrior, choose the Fighter class from the Player's Handbook. You can choose one of three specializations to guide your choice of subclass, spells, and feats: arms, fury, or protection.

##### Arms Specialization

Warriors' choices of weapons and fighting style determine their role on the battlefield. Arms warriors favor two-handed weapons that allow them to deliver devastating blows. These warriors combine these overpowering attacks with heightened mobility to strike where their foes are least defended.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Great Weapon Fighting for a Fighting Style feat. Choose at least one of Greataxes, Greatswords, or Mauls for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW. Consider the following maneuvers: Battle Shout,* Disarming Attack, Distracting Strike, Mortal Strike,* Pushing Attack, Sweeping Attack, and Trip Attack. Maneuvers marked with an asterisk are described below.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Charger, Crusher, Great Weapon Master, Piercer, Speedy, and Slasher.

##### Fury Specialization

Through a lifetime of training and sparring, fury warriors become masters of carnage, wielding a weapon in each hand to maximize the destruction they cause. Though they forgo the protection of a shield, they surround themselves with whirling blades that leave little opportunity for their opponents to strike without suffering grievous wounds in return. Fury warriors dive into the fray in a breathtaking display of brute force, savoring the frenzy of battle.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Two-Weapon Fighting for a Fighting Style feat. Choose Scimitars among your other weapons for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Charger, Crusher, Dual Wielder, Piercer, and Slasher.

##### Protection Specialization

Protection warriors favor a more measured approach to battle than other warriors, countering incoming attacks and using their shields to protect their allies as well as themselves. For the protection warrior, being the toughest soldier on the battlefield means nothing if allies are left vulnerable. These warriors stand as an unbreakable wall protecting their friends from their foes.

The following choices will help you focus on this specialization:

- **Level 1.** Level 1 Choose Interception or Protection for a Fighting Style feat. Choose at least one of Battleaxes, Longswords, or Morningstars for your Weapon Mastery feature.
- **Level 3.** Level 3 Choose the WoW. Consider the following maneuvers: Bait and Switch, Battle Shout,* Demoralizing Shout,* Goading Attack, Maneuvering Attack, Parry, and Warding Maneuver.* Maneuvers marked with an asterisk are described below.
- **Feats.** Feats Consider the following feats at higher levels: Crusher, Durable, Heavy Armor Master, Piercer, Sentinel, Shield Master, and Slasher.

> **Berserker Barbarians**
> Although D&D's Fighter class is the best match for a warrior in *World of Warcraft*, you could also play a Barbarian. Choose the WoW, and otherwise follow the guidance for building a fury warrior as a Fighter.

#### Warrior Maneuvers

Additional maneuver options for Battle Master Fighters are presented here in alphabetical order.

##### Battle Shout

As a Bonus Action, you can expend one Superiority Die to shout encouragement to allies within 30 feet of you that can hear you. Until the start of your next turn, each affected ally adds the Superiority Die to the damage roll the next time it hits with a melee attack.

##### Demoralizing Shout

As a Bonus Action, you can expend one Superiority Die to shout, demoralizing enemies within 30 feet of you that can hear you. Until the end of your next turn, each affected enemy subtracts the Superiority Die from the damage roll the next time it hits a target other than you with a melee attack.

##### Heroic Leap

As a Bonus Action, you can expend one Superiority Die to jump a number of feet up to your Strength score. Creatures within 5 feet of you when you land make a Strength saving throw, taking Bludgeoning damage equal to the Superiority Die roll on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one.

##### Mortal Strike

When you hit a creature with an attack roll, you can expend one Superiority Die to prevent the target from healing. Add the Superiority Die to the attack's damage roll. The target can't regain Hit Points until the end of your next turn.

##### Warding Maneuver

When you or a creature you can see within 5 feet of you is hit by an attack roll, you can take a Reaction and expend one Superiority Die if you're wielding a Melee weapon or a Shield. Add the Superiority Die to the target's AC against the triggering attack. If the attack still hits, the target has Resistance against the attack's damage.

## Chapter 2: Character Origins

*[Image: A gnome welcomes the adventurous of all species to the Darkmoon Faire. (book/WoW/036-02-001.darkmoon-faire-splash.webp)]*

Many different peoples call Azeroth home. Some are native to its lands, while others have settled there from far-flung worlds. Most of these peoples have aligned themselves with either the Alliance or the Horde.

When you make a character to adventure on Azeroth, choose a background from the Player's Handbook, then choose one of the species described in this chapter. Your choice of species might also give you a starting Renown Score with one of Azeroth's factions (detailed in 4).

### Languages

When choosing languages for a character in a *World of Warcraft* campaign, use the tables here instead of the ones in the Player's Handbook. Your character knows either Common (if they belong to the Alliance) or Orcish (if they belong to the Horde), plus two languages you roll or choose from the appropriate table—Alliance Languages or Horde Languages. If the origin you choose isn't affiliated with either faction, you can choose your three starting languages from either table. (At least one of the common languages of the Alliance and the Horde—Common or Orcish—is recommended.)

**Alliance Languages**

| 1d12 | Language | Origin |
| --- | --- | --- |
| — | Common | Humans |
|  | Common Sign Language | Humans |
|  | Darnassian | Night elves |
|  | Draconic | Dragons |
|  | Draenei | Draenei |
|  | Dwarven | Dwarves |
|  | Gnomish | Gnomes |
|  | Hara'ni | Haranir |
|  | Pandaren | Pandaren |
|  | Thalassian | High Elves |

**Horde Languages**

| 1d12 | Language | Origin |
| --- | --- | --- |
| — | Orcish | Orcs |
|  | Orcish Sign Language | Orcs |
|  | Draconic | Dragons |
|  | Goblin | Goblins |
|  | Forsaken | Forsaken |
|  | Hara'ni | Haranir |
|  | Pandaren | Pandaren |
|  | Shalassian | Nightborne |
|  | Taur-ahe | Tauren |
|  | Thalassian | High Elves |
|  | Vulpera | Vulpera |
|  | Zandali | Trolls |

The Rare Languages table lists languages that are either secret or derived from other planes of existence and thus less widespread on Azeroth. Some features let a character learn a rare language.

The First Ones, the enigmatic architects of the entire cosmos, left fragments of language that are akin to mathematics and largely incomprehensible to mortals. Kalimag, the language of elementals, is equivalent to Primordial in D&D, and Shath'Yar, the language of the Old Gods, is equivalent to Deep Speech.

**Rare Languages**

| Language | Origin |
| --- | --- |
| Cyphers of the First Ones | First Ones |
| Druidic | Druidic circles |
| Eredun | Demons of the Burning Legion |
| Furbolg | Furbolgs |

### Dracthyr

*[Image: Dracthyr shift freely between natural and visage forms. (races/WoW/Dracthyr.webp)]*

#### Dracthyr

*Size:* Medium

*Speed:* 30 ft.

##### Darkvision

You have Darkvision with a range of 60 feet.

##### Gliding Wings

While you're in your natural form and don't have the Incapacitated condition, when you fall, you subtract up to 100 feet from the distance fallen when calculating the fall's damage, and you can move up to 2 feet horizontally for every 1 foot you descend.

##### Natural Weaponry

While you're in your natural form, your wings and tail are powerful weapons. As a Bonus Action, you can use your wings or tail to make an Unarmed Strike to Shove a creature.

##### Visage

As a Magic action, you can shape-shift into a humanoid form called your visage, or back to your natural form. Your visage has a specific appearance that you choose when you select this species, including your coloration, height and weight, hair length, and sex. Changing shape with this trait doesn't change your clothing, equipment, or game statistics.

##### Draconic Flight

When you reach character level 5, you can use your natural form's wings to fly short distances. As a Bonus Action, you leap into the air and give yourself a Fly Speed equal to your Speed that lasts for 10 minutes. Once you use this trait, you can't use it again until you finish a Long Rest.

### Draenei

*[Image: Many draenei cherish the lore collected during their long sojourn from their home world. (races/WoW/Draenei.webp)]*

#### Draenei

*Size:* Medium

*Speed:* 30 ft.

##### Darkvision

You have Darkvision with a range of 60 feet.

##### Eredar Lineage

You are part of a lineage related to your ancestors' embrace of the Light—or of the power of the Burning Legion. This lineage grants you supernatural abilities. Choose a lineage from the Eredar Lineages table: Draenei, Krokul, Lightforged, or Man'ari. You gain the level 1 benefit of that lineage.

When you reach character levels 3 and 5, you learn a higher-level spell, as shown on the table. You always have that spell prepared. You can cast it once without a spell slot, and you regain the ability to cast it in that way when you finish a Long Rest. You can also cast it using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level.

Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability for the spells you cast with this trait (choose when you select the lineage).

**Eredar Lineages**

| Lineage | Level 1 | Level 3 | Level 5 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Draenei | You have Resistance to Necrotic damage. You also know the Spare the Dying cantrip. | Healing Word | Prayer of Healing |
| Krokul | You have Resistance to Poison damage. You also know the Poison Spray cantrip. | Ray of Sickness | Hold Person |
| Lightforged | You have Resistance to Radiant damage. You also know the Sacred Flame cantrip. | Guiding Bolt | Aid |
| Man'ari | You have Resistance to Fire damage. You also know the Fire Bolt cantrip. | Hellish Rebuke | Darkness |

##### Heroic Presence

When you cast a spell granted by your Eredar Lineage, if you don't have Heroic Inspiration, you gain it. You can gain Heroic Inspiration in this way once, and you regain the ability to do so when you finish a Short or Long Rest.

##### Azerite Surge

When you take the Attack action on your turn, you can replace one of the attacks with a blast of Azerite energy in a 15-foot Cone. Each creature of your choice in the Cone regains 1d10 Hit Points. Each other creature in the Cone makes a Dexterity saving throw (DC 8 plus your Constitution modifier and your Proficiency Bonus), taking 1d10 Radiant damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

Both the damage and the healing increase by 1d10 when you reach character levels 5 (2d10), 11 (3d10), and 17 (4d10).

You can use this ability a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

##### Darkvision

You have Darkvision with a range of 120 feet.

You have Resistance to Poison damage. You also have Advantage on saving throws you make to avoid or end the Poisoned condition.

You don't gain Exhaustion levels from malnutrition.

When you spend Hit Point Dice when you finish a Short Rest, you regain 1 additional Hit Point per Hit Point Die you spend.

*Level:* 0

You create a pulse of arcane energy in a 10-foot Emanation originating from yourself. Each creature of your choice within this area makes a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, it takes 1d8 Force damage, and its Speed is reduced by 10 feet (to a minimum of 0 feet) until the end of your next turn.

*Level:* 1

You are empowered by the essence of the Void for the duration, becoming swathed in shadow and taking on a dark-blue or purple appearance. While this effect lasts, you gain the following benefits.

*[Image: For many of the Forsaken, life continues much as it did before their transformation. (races/WoW/Forsaken.webp)]*

#### Forsaken

*Size:* Small or Medium

*Speed:* 30 ft.

##### Darkvision

You have Darkvision with a range of 60 feet.

##### Deathly Endurance

You have Resistance to Necrotic damage.

##### Everlasting

You don't gain Exhaustion levels from dehydration, malnutrition, or suffocation. You don't need to sleep, and magic can't put you to sleep. You can finish a Long Rest in 4 hours if you spend those hours in an inactive, motionless state, during which you retain consciousness.

##### Touch of the Grave

When you deal damage to a creature, you can roll a number of d4s equal to your Proficiency Bonus. You deal extra Necrotic damage to the creature equal to the total rolled, and you regain a number of Hit Points equal to the extra damage dealt.

Once you use this benefit, you can't use it again until you finish a Long Rest.

##### Will of the Forsaken

You have Advantage on saving throws to avoid or end the Charmed or Frightened condition.

### Gnome

*[Image: Technological advancement is both a means of survival and a source of joy for many gnomes. (races/WoW/Gnome.webp)]*

#### Gnome

#### Mechagnome

*Size:* Small

*Speed:* 30 ft.

##### Built for Success

You can add 1d4 to one D20 Test you make, and you can do so after rolling the die but before resolving the effects of the roll. You can use this trait a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

##### Emergency Fail-Safe

When you become Bloodied, you can take a Reaction to spend one Hit Point Die, roll it, and regain a number of Hit Points equal to the roll plus your Constitution modifier (minimum of 1 Hit Point). Once you use this trait, you can't use it again until you finish a Short or Long Rest.

##### Mechanical Nature

You have Resistance to Poison damage, and you have Advantage on saving throws to avoid or end the Paralyzed or Poisoned condition. You don't gain Exhaustion levels from dehydration, malnutrition, or suffocation.

##### Sentry's Rest

You don't need to sleep. You can finish a Long Rest in 6 hours if you spend those hours in an inactive, motionless state, during which you retain consciousness.

##### Specialized Design

You gain proficiency with two type=tool;artisan's tools;instrument;gaming set of your choice.

When you damage a creature with an attack or a spell and the creature's size is larger than yours, you can cause the attack or spell to deal extra damage to the creature. The extra damage equals your Proficiency Bonus and is the same damage type as the other damage dealt.

You can use this trait a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, regaining all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest, and you can use it no more than once per turn.

You can take the Disengage or Hide action as a Bonus Action on each of your turns.

You can cast it once without a spell slot, and you regain the ability to cast it in that way when you finish a Long Rest. You can also cast the spell using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level.

Your horns are powerful natural weapons. When you hit with your Unarmed Strike and deal damage, you can deal Piercing damage equal to 1d6 plus your Strength modifier instead of the normal damage of an Unarmed Strike.

Immediately after you take the Dash action on your turn and move at least 20 feet, you can make one Unarmed Strike as a Bonus Action.

When you shove a target in this way, you can push the target up to 10 feet away instead of 5 feet.

##### Tauren Lineage

You are part of a lineage that grants you supernatural qualities. If your lineage grants you an ability that allows a saving throw, the DC equals 8 plus your Strength, Constitution, or Wisdom modifier (choose when you select the lineage) and Proficiency Bonus. Choose one of the following options:

- **Mulgore Tauren.** When you take the Attack action on your turn, you can replace one of the attacks with a magically empowered stomp on the ground. You can't use this feature if you have the Prone condition or if you are flying, climbing, or swimming. When you use your war stomp, each creature of your choice within a 5-foot Emanation originating from you makes a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, a creature has the Stunned condition until the end of your current turn.
- **Highmountain Tauren.** As an action, you can move up to your Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks and can move through the spaces of other creatures. Each Medium or smaller creature whose space you enter makes a Strength saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes damage equal to your Proficiency Bonus plus your Strength modifier and has the Prone condition. A creature can take this damage only once per turn.
- **Taunka.** Your shaggy hide gives you a +1 bonus to Armor Class and Resistance to Cold damage.

You are part of a lineage that grants you supernatural qualities. Choose one of the following options:

- **Amani Troll.** Whenever you spend Heroic Inspiration, you regain 1 expended Hit Point Die.
- **Darkspear Troll.** When you take the Dash action, Difficult Terrain composed of ice, rubble, snow, or undergrowth doesn't cost you extra movement until the end of your turn.

#### Worgen

As a Bonus Action, you can shape-shift to assume a more bestial appearance. This transformation lasts for 1 minute or until you revert to your normal appearance as a Bonus Action. When you enter your bestial form, you gain Temporary Hit Points equal to 2 times your Proficiency Bonus. You can enter this form a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

In your bestial form, you gain the following benefits:

- **Increased Speed.** Your Speed increases by 10 feet.
- **Reactive Movement.** You can take a Reaction to move up to 10 feet when a creature ends its turn within 5 feet of you. This reactive movement doesn't provoke Opportunity Attacks.
- **Necrotic Resistance.** You have Resistance to Necrotic damage.

##### Darkvision

You have Darkvision with a range of 60 feet.

##### Instinctual Shift
