## Chapter 3: Professions and Gear

*[Image: Adventurers bid on precious treasures in the Stormwind auction house. (book/WoW/052-03-001.stormwind-auction-house-splash.webp)]*

Beyond choosing class and origin, you can customize your character in a *World of Warcraft* campaign through their professions and equipment—especially mounts, pets, and magic items. This chapter covers those options.

### Professions and Crafting

Characters in Azeroth can use their tool proficiencies to practice professions, allowing them to gather resources and craft them into equipment and magic items. A character can practice any profession as long as the character has the prerequisite proficiency.

The rules in this chapter build on the Crafting Equipment in the Player's Handbook and Dungeon Master's Guide. With these rules, characters can use gathering professions (such as fishing, mining, and skinning) to collect abstract resources that are measured in GP value. For example, a character might use the herbalism profession to collect 50 GP worth of herbalism resources. The character can then use any combination of those resources and the character's own money to craft items using their production professions (such as blacksmithing, cooking, and leatherworking). Having gathered 50 GP worth of herbalism resources, a character might spend those resources, along with 50 GP of coins or other monetary resources, to craft a Potion of Water Breathing.

Optionally, a DM can make the resources less abstract, but this approach requires the DM to invent or research recipes for magic items characters wish to create and name the resources characters gather. A character who wants to craft a *Potion of Water Breathing* might need to gather 50 GP worth of stranglekelp, not just abstract herbalism resources, and also acquire 50 GP worth of blackmouth oil (or use fishing to catch oily blackmouth fish). Listing specific resources and recipes is beyond the scope of this book.

#### Proficiency Levels

A character's Proficiency Bonus is the measure of how skilled the character is at a profession. A character with a +2 Proficiency Bonus is an apprentice in a profession, a +3 bonus makes the character a journeyman, and so on, as shown on the Profession Levels table below. Each recipe used in a crafting profession requires a minimum Proficiency Bonus, which is determined by the rarity of the magic item the recipe creates. (Most nonmagical equipment requires apprentice recipes.)

**Profession Levels**

| Proficiency Bonus | Proficiency Level | Magic Item Crafting |
| --- | --- | --- |
| +2 | Apprentice | Common |
| +3 | Journeyman | Uncommon |
| +4 | Expert | Rare |
| +5 | Artisan | Very Rare |
| +6 | Master | Legendary |

This chapter assumes characters work with trainers to improve their professions between adventures. If your campaign uses the Variant: Training to Gain Levels in the *Dungeon Master's Guide* that characters must spend time and money training to gain levels, characters must also spend time and money training with profession trainers to improve their skill in the profession. This training requires a number of days equal to the character's new Proficiency Bonus and costs 2 GP per day. So when a character reaches level 5 and a Proficiency Bonus of +3, the character must spend 3 days and 6 GP training to achieve a journeyman proficiency level in each profession they practice.

#### Gathering Professions

Gathering professions allow characters to collect resources, which can be sold or used for crafting.

Most gathering professions work like foraging during travel, as Foraging in the *Dungeon Master's Guide*. You can make a Wisdom (Survival) check using a gathering profession once per journey stage (or once per day if a stage is shorter than a day). The DC is set by the DM based on the general level range of the region where you're traveling, as described in 5. (Archaeology and skinning work differently, as described below.)

You find nothing on a failed check. On a successful check, you find one resource per day of the journey stage. If you beat the DC by 5 or more, one of the resources you find on that journey stage is a rare (more valuable) resource. The DM decides what resources you find.

**Gathering Professions**

| 1–4 | 10 | 10 GP | 25 GP |
| 5–10 | 12 | 50 GP | 100 GP |
| 11–16 | 15 | 250 GP | 500 GP |
| 17–20 | 17 | 1,000 GP | 2,500 GP |
| Endgame | 20 | 5,000 GP | 7,500 GP |

##### Archaeology

*Requires Proficiency in History*

You can use the archaeology profession to gather archaeology fragments from the sites of ancient ruins. Unlike the resources gathered with other gathering professions, these resources aren't used in crafting. Rather, you can piece these archaeology fragments together into items of historical interest.

###### Gathering Fragments

To gather archaeology fragments, you must visit a dig site. The DM determines whether an area is a dig site and where the relics originate from. The Dig Sites shows the regions where relics of different origins can be found.

After spending 1 hour exploring a dig site (which can be concurrent with a Short Rest), you make an Intelligence (History) check. The DC of the check depends on the region level, as shown on the x. On a successful check, you unearth 1d6 archaeology fragments. If you beat the DC by 5 or more, you unearth a complete relic with the value of a rare resource for that region, as shown on the Gathering Professions table. On a natural 20, you instead find one magic fragment.

###### Assembling a Relic

You can assemble six archaeology fragments with the same origin into a completed relic by succeeding on a DC 15 Intelligence (History) check. The value of the assembled relic equals the value of a common resource for your level (rather than the region level), as shown on the x table. On a failed check, you can't try again until you've collected at least one additional archaeology fragment with the same origin.

###### Assembling a Magic Item

You can assemble six magic fragments with the same origin into an item of the DM's choice whose rarity corresponds with your Proficiency Bonus, as shown on the Profession Levels table. For example, if you collect six magic fragments from dwarf dig sites, and you are a master archaeologist (Proficiency Bonus +6), you can assemble the pieces into a Staff of the Magi that was wielded by Sorcerer-Thane Thaurissan.

**Dig Sites**

| Origin | Regions |
| --- | --- |
| Demon | x |
| Draenei | Outland |
| Drust | x |
| Dwarf | Central x |
| Fossil | x, x |
| Highborne | x |
| Highmountain tauren | x |
| Mantid | x |
| Mogu | x |
| Nerubian | x |
| Night elf | x |
| Orc | Outland |
| Pandaren | x |
| Tol'vir | x (x) |
| Troll | Northern and southern x, x |
| Vrykul | x |
| Zandalari | x |

*[Image: Fishing can yield unexpected treasures. (book/WoW/053-03-002.unexpected-treasure.webp)]*

##### Fishing

*Requires No Proficiency*

The primary use for fishing is Foraging, which uses the rules in the *Dungeon Master's Guide*. If you roll a natural 20 on a Wisdom (Survival) check to forage for food using fishing, you might pull up a chest, crate, or lockbox containing other resources—such as ores, fabrics, or leather—at the DM's discretion. The value of such a find equals the value of a rare resource for the region's level.

If you are searching for particular fish to use in a recipe, you can make a Wisdom (Survival) check once per journey stage to see whether you can catch the desired fish, using a DC chosen from the x.

*[Image: A Forsaken herbalist collects a ghost mushroom. (book/WoW/054-03-003.herbalist.webp)]*

##### Herbalism

*Requires Proficiency with Herbalism Kit*

You can use the herbalism profession to gather herbs, flowers, and mushrooms. These resources are primarily useful in crafting items with the x and x professions.

Using herbalism follows the general rules described in "x." In unusual circumstances, you can use herbalism to gather rare resources from Plant creatures. Use the rules for x as a guideline to determine the DC for gathering such resources and their value.

##### Mining

*Requires Proficiency with Mason's Tools*

You can use the mining profession to gather metal ores, stones, and gems. These resources are primarily useful in crafting items with the x, x, and x professions.

###### Smelting

If you are gathering specific resources to use in detailed recipes, metal mining resources might need to be smelted into bars before they can be used in crafting. You need access to a smith's forge or a refinery to smelt ore, and the time required is 1 hour for every 10 GP of the metal resource's value. So copper and tin (10 GP mining resources) can be refined into bars in 1 hour, while creating an empyrium bar (a 500 GP resource) takes 50 hours.

You can use the same rules to combine metal resources to create alloys, which can be important in blacksmithing.

##### Skinning

*Requires Proficiency with Leatherworker's Tools*

You can use the skinning profession to gather leather or hide. These resources are primarily useful in crafting items with the leatherworking profession.

You use skinning on dead creatures rather than as part of travel, and the quality of leather or hide gathered depends on the Challenge Rating of the creature rather than the level of the region.

Skinning a Beast or an animallike Monstrosity yields leather or hide. You must be in the same space as the corpse and spend 10 minutes skinning the creature, and then you make a Wisdom (Survival) check using Leatherworker's Tools. The check DC depends on the Challenge Rating of the creature, using the Region Level column of the x and treating a CR above 20 as Endgame. If you make a successful check, the effort yields a leather resource worth the value of a common resource. If you beat the DC by 5 or more, the effort yields hide instead, which has the value of a rare resource.

You can also skin certain Fiends and Dragons. Skinning an animal-like Fiend, such as a felhound, requires journeyman-level skinning (a Proficiency Bonus of +3 or higher). Skinning a Dragon requires artisan-level skinning (a Proficiency Bonus of +5 or higher). A successful check in either case yields hide with the value of a rare resource.

#### Production Professions

Production professions allow characters to use resources—whether gathered using gathering professions, found as treasure, or purchased from merchants—to craft nonmagical and magical items.

Production professions use the Crafting Equipment in the *Player's Handbook* and the Crafting Magic Items in the *Dungeon Master's Guide*, with one key change: Ingredients provide the magic. Characters can craft magic items even if they lack proficiency in the Arcana skill. They also don't need to have spells prepared in order to craft magic items that produce spells. Instead, they need to know specific recipes and use ingredients—many of which are infused with magic—to create items. Characters learn recipes as described below and in the profession descriptions that follow.

##### Crafting Recipes

"Recipe" is a general term that encompasses recipes for alchemy and cooking, plans for blacksmithing, formulas for enchanting, schematics for engineering, techniques for inscription, designs for jewelcrafting, and patterns for leatherworking and tailoring. As characters advance their crafting skills, they learn basic recipes automatically. Basic recipes let a character create mundane items and Common, Uncommon, and Rare magic Armor, Potions, Scrolls, Wands, and Weapons. Other recipes can be purchased from specialist trainers, found as treasure, or granted as renown rewards (see 4). Artisan and master recipes can be acquired only by extraordinary means, as described below.

The specific recipes mentioned here are examples only, inspired by crafting recipes in the *World of Warcraft* game. The DM is free to create unique recipes or to ignore the specifics and require only some combination of abstract resources and money.

###### Apprentice Recipes

Apprentice recipes require some combination of two or three different resources. For example, using alchemy to craft a Common potion costs 25 GP. The DM might look up alchemy recipes from *World of Warcraft* and decide that this requires 20 GP of herbalism resources plus 5 GP of miscellaneous ingredients. Crafting a Potion of Healing might require one peacebloom and one silverleaf, while a Potion of Mana might require a mageroyal and a silverleaf. All these are common resources found in level 1–4 regions (worth 10 GP each). Similarly, using blacksmithing to craft a suit of Plate Armor in *World of Warcraft* requires iron bars and heavy stones, so the DM might decide that the 750 GP cost to craft the armor consists of twelve iron bars and three heavy stones, which are all common resources found in level 5–10 regions (50 GP each).

###### Journeyman Recipes

Journeyman recipes sometimes involve more complex steps than simply combining two or three resources of the same type. For example, crafting an elixir of water breathing in *World of Warcraft* requires the combination of stranglekelp (gathered with herbalism) and blackmouth oil (made with alchemy from oily blackmouth fish). So the DM might create a recipe for a Potion of Water Breathing (an Uncommon potion that costs 100 GP to make) that involves catching two rare fish resources in a level 1–4 region (25 GP each), then combining the oil from them with stranglekelp, a common herb resource from a level 5–10 region (50 GP). Similarly, using blacksmithing to craft a +1 Greatsword (which costs 225 GP) might involve both mining and skinning resources: ten copper bars (10 GP each), two malachite stones (10 GP each), and two medium leather (50 GP each), plus 5 GP extra.

###### Expert Recipes

In addition to requiring more expensive resources, expert recipes are often quite complex and sometimes require unusual ingredients—which often give them minor properties or quirks (see "Magic Item Special Features" in the Dungeon Master's Guide). For example, characters in *World of Warcraft* can use blacksmithing to create barbaric iron plate using iron bars, heavy stones, and fangs and claws from predatory animals. The DM might decide that this armor is +1 Plate Armor with the Guardian minor property, granting the wearer a +2 bonus to Initiative rolls. Such an item costs 2,750 GP to craft, so the recipe might require forty-eight iron bars (50 GP each), five heavy stones (50 GP each), and 100 GP of fangs and claws.

###### Artisan Recipes

Befitting their high price, artisan recipes require rare and expensive ingredients. Learning the recipes required for these items often requires completing special quests or earning a Renown Score of 10+ with a faction that guards the secrets of creating them (see 4). For example, an alchemy recipe called "elixir of giants"—which teaches a character to make a Potion of Giant Strength (cloud)—isn't commonly known in modern times but can be found in lockboxes and other treasure hoards across Azeroth. (It might require five Gromsblood and five sungrass, both 1,000 GP herbalism resources.)

Artisan recipes sometimes require numerous resources that must first be processed with other professions. For example, making a Frost Brand (Longsword) with blacksmithing could require eighteen arcanite bars, which are thorium bars (250 GP mining resources) that have been combined via alchemy with arcane crystals (100 GP gems). The hilt requires four enchanted leather, which are thick leather (250 GP leather resources) enhanced via enchanting using 1,200 GP of magical residue from disenchanting items. The cold magic in the blade comes from four water elemental essences (available for 100 GP each from specialized vendors). Then these items are combined with four blue sapphires (1,000 GP gems), four Azerothian diamonds (1,000 GP gems), and a dense grinding stone (125 GP mining resource).

###### Master Recipes

Master recipes can be acquired only as treasure or renown rewards. They often require rare resources or resources found on other worlds or planes of existence. For example, crafting a Potion of Giant Strength (storm) with alchemy might require twenty rising glory—a 5,000 GP herbalism resource that grows only in the Shadowlands realm of Bastion. Similarly, crafting Plate Armor of Etherealness with blacksmithing requires a combination of six different minerals found only in the Shadowlands: five laestrite ore (5,000 GP mining resource) and two each of elethium, solenium, phaedrum, sinvyr, and oxxein ore (7,500 GP mining resources), along with 750 GP worth of luminous flux (purchased from a vendor) to aid the mixture of metals.

##### Alchemy

*Requires Proficiency with Alchemist's Supplies or Herbalism Kit*

Using Alchemist's Supplies or an Herbalism Kit and resources mostly gathered from herbalism and following particular recipes, you can craft potions, alchemical equipment, dusts, perfumes, and the like. You can learn to make these items as your skill increases:

- **Apprentice (+2) Recipes:.** Apprentice (+2) Recipes: Acid, Alchemist's Fire, Component Pouch, Fuel (see "x" in this chapter), Oil, Paper, Perfume, Common Potions, Perfume of Bewitching
- **Journeyman (+3) Recipes:.** Journeyman (+3) Recipes: Uncommon Potions, Alchemy Jug, Dust of Disappearance, Dust of Dryness, Keoghtom's Ointment
- **Expert (+4) Recipes:.** Expert (+4) Recipes: Rare Potions
- **Artisan (+5) Recipes:.** Artisan (+5) Recipes: Very Rare Potions, Philosopher's Stone
- **Master (+6) Recipes:.** Master (+6) Recipes: Legendary Potions, Sovereign Glue, Universal Solvent

##### Blacksmithing

*Requires Proficiency with Mason's Tools or Smith's Tools*

Using Mason's Tools or Smith's Tools and resources gathered from mining (including metals, gems, and stones), and following particular plans, you can craft items from metal and stone. Some plans might require cloth or leather resources as well. You can learn to make these items as your skill increases:

- **Apprentice (+2) Plans:.** Apprentice (+2) Plans: Any Melee weapon (except Club, Greatclub, Quarterstaff, and Whip); Medium armor (except Hide); Heavy armor; Shield; Ball Bearings; Block and Tackle; Bucket; Caltrops; Chain; Crowbar; Firearm Bullets; Grappling Hook; Iron Pot; Iron Spikes; Sling Bullets; Common magic ammunition, Armor, and Weapons in the above categories; Dark Shard Amulet; Dread Helm
- **Journeyman (+3) Plans:.** Journeyman (+3) Plans: Uncommon magic ammunition, Armor, and Weapons in the above categories; Figurine of Wondrous Power (silver raven); Gauntlets of Ogre Power; Helm of Comprehending Languages; Helm of Telepathy; Sending Stones; Stone of Good Luck (Luckstone)
- **Expert (+4) Plans:.** Expert (+4) Plans: Rare magic ammunition, Armor, and Weapons in the above categories; Belt of Giant Strength (hill); Bracers of Defense; Brazier of Commanding Fire Elementals; Dimensional Shackles; Figurine of Wondrous Power (bronze griffon, golden lions, marble elephant); Helm of Teleportation; Horseshoes of Speed; Iron Bands of Bilarro; Stone of Controlling Earth Elementals
- **Artisan (+5) Plans:.** Artisan (+5) Plans: Very Rare magic ammunition, Armor, and Weapons in the above categories; Belt of Giant Strength (fire, frost, or stone); Helm of Brilliance; Horseshoes of a Zephyr
- **Master (+6) Plans:.** Master (+6) Plans: Legendary magic ammunition, Armor, and Weapons in the above categories; Belt of Giant Strength (cloud or storm); Iron Flask

##### Cooking

*Requires Proficiency with Brewer's Supplies or Cook's Utensils*

Using Brewer's Supplies or Cook's Utensils and ingredients (typically gathered from fishing, herbalism, and hunting game), and following particular recipes, you can craft mundane and magical edible items:

- **Apprentice (+2) Recipes:.** Apprentice (+2) Recipes: Antitoxin, Rations, Bead of Nourishment, Bead of Refreshment, Heward's Handy Spice Pouch, Tankard of Sobriety

Higher levels of skill in cooking are best reflected with the Chef feat. Additionally, a meal prepared by an artisan (+5) cook, given an hour of preparation time and 1,000 GP of resources (which can include fish or other meat), can duplicate the effects of the Heroes' Feast spell.

##### Enchanting

*Requires Proficiency with Carpenter's Tools, Potter's Tools, or Woodcarver's Tools*

Using Carpenter's Tools, Potter's Tools, or Woodcarver's Tools, along with reagents (primarily magical residue from disenchanting items, as described below, as well as herbs, gems, and other resources), and following particular formulas, you can craft various items. You can learn to make these items as your skill increases:

- **Apprentice (+2) Formulas:.** Apprentice (+2) Formulas: Wooden weapons (Club, Greatclub, Quarterstaff), Ranged weapons (except Musket, Pistol, and Sling), ammunition (Arrows, Bolts, Needles), Arcane Focus, Barrel, Chest, Druidic Focus, Ink Pen, Jug, Ladder, Lamp, Pole, Portable Ram, Torch, Common magic ammunition in the above categories, Common Wands, Charlatan's Die, Pot of Awakening
- **Journeyman (+3) Formulas:.** Journeyman (+3) Formulas: Uncommon magic ammunition and Weapons in the above categories; Uncommon Rods and Wands; Uncommon Enspelled Armor, Enspelled Staff, or Enspelled Weapon; Broom of Flying; Decanter of Endless Water; Pipes of Haunting; Pipes of the Sewers
- **Expert (+4) Formulas:.** Expert (+4) Formulas: Rare magic ammunition and Weapons in the above categories; Rare Rods and Wands; Rare Enspelled Armor, Enspelled Staff, or Enspelled Weapon; Bowl of Commanding Water Elementals; Figurine of Wondrous Power (ebony fly)
- **Artisan (+5) Formulas:.** Artisan (+5) Formulas: Very Rare magic ammunition and Weapons in the above categories; Very Rare Rods and Wands; Very Rare Enspelled Armor, Enspelled Staff, or Enspelled Weapon
- **Master (+6) Formulas:.** Master (+6) Formulas: Legendary magic ammunition and Weapons in the above categories; Legendary Rods and Wands; Legendary Enspelled Armor, Enspelled Staff, or Enspelled Weapon

###### Disenchanting

When you reach journeyman enchanting skill, you learn to disenchant magic items. If you spend 1 hour (which can be concurrent with a Short Rest) with a permanent magic item that isn't attuned to someone else, you can remove its magic and store it in the residue, which takes the form of a magical dust or shard. The item's rarity determines the enchanting skill required to disenchant it and the value of the residue it leaves, as shown on the table below. The magical residue can be used as a crafting resource for any enchanting formula. Legendary magic items and Artifacts can't be disenchanted in this way.

| Item Rarity | Skill Required | Residue Value |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Common | Journeyman (+3) | 25 GP |
| Uncommon | Expert (+4) | 100 GP |
| Rare | Artisan (+5) | 1,000 GP |
| Very Rare | Master (+6) | 10,000 GP |

##### Engineering

*Requires Proficiency with Tinker's Tools*

Using Tinker's Tools and components (typically including resources gathered from mining as well as leather or cloth), and following particular schematics, you can craft gadgets.

Magic items created with engineering typically have a mechanical appearance and seem to utilize some combination of mundane science and magic. The traditions of gnome engineers tend toward elaborate mechanical contraptions, while goblin engineering traditions are more explosive.

Part of every engineering schematic is the creation of mechanical parts and explosive powders used in crafting other items, such as transforming mined and smelted copper bars into copper bolts and mined stone into blasting powder. Crafting these components is included in the time and money required for crafting a finished item. You can learn to make these items as your skill increases:

- **Apprentice (+2) Schematics:.** Apprentice (+2) Schematics: Musket, Pistol, Bell, Bullseye Lantern, Flask, Hooded Lantern, Hunting Trap, Lock, Manacles, Mirror, Shovel, Signal Whistle, Tinderbox, Clockwork Amulet, Ear Horn of Hearing, Ersatz Eye, Horn of Silent Alarm, Instrument of Illusions, Instrument of Scribing, Lock of Trickery, Mystery Key, Orb of Direction, Orb of Time, Pipe of Smoke Monsters, Pole of Angling, Pole of Collapsing, Prosthetic Limb, Rival Coin, Veteran's Cane
- **Journeyman (+3) Schematics:.** Journeyman (+3) Schematics: Goblin Trike. Uncommon magic Muskets and Pistols, Baba Yaga's Dancing Broom, Driftglobe, Eversmoking Bottle, Goblin Jumper Cables, Goggles of Night, Lantern of Revealing, Wind Fan
- **Expert (+4) Schematics:.** Expert (+4) Schematics: Flying Machine, Mechano-Hog, or Mekgineer's Chopper; Rare magic Muskets and Pistols; Chime of Opening; Cube of Summoning; Daern's Instant Fortress; Experimental Goblin Jetpack; Horn of Blasting; Horn of Valhalla (brass or silver); Portable Hole
- **Artisan (+5) Schematics:.** Artisan (+5) Schematics: Very Rare magic Muskets and Pistols, Cauldron of Rebirth, Horn of Valhalla (bronze)
- **Master (+6) Schematics:.** Master (+6) Schematics: Legendary magic Muskets and Pistols, Apparatus of Kwalish, Cubic Gate, Horn of Valhalla (iron), Sphere of Annihilation, Talisman of the Sphere, Well of Many Worlds

##### Inscription

*Requires Proficiency with Calligrapher's Supplies, Cartographer's Tools, or Painter's Supplies*

Using Calligrapher's Supplies, Cartographer's Tools, or Painter's Supplies, along with pigments and inks made from herbalism resources (gathered with herbalism or purchased), and using particular techniques, you can craft inks, pigments, and writings.

Part of every inscription technique is the process of milling herbs to create pigments and inks. Crafting these components is included in the time and money required for crafting a finished item. You can learn to make these items as your skill increases:

- **Apprentice (+2) Techniques:.** Apprentice (+2) Techniques: Druidic Focus, Holy Symbol, Ink, Map, Common Scrolls and Staffs, Enduring Spellbook
- **Journeyman (+3) Techniques:.** Journeyman (+3) Techniques: Uncommon Scrolls and Staffs, Deck of Illusions
- **Expert (+4) Techniques:.** Expert (+4) Techniques: Rare Scrolls and Staffs
- **Artisan (+5) Techniques:.** Artisan (+5) Techniques: Very Rare Scrolls and Staffs, Manual of Bodily Health, Manual of Gainful Exercise, Manual of Golems (any), Manual of Quickness of Action, Nolzur's Marvelous Pigments, Spirit Board, Tome of Clear Thought, Tome of Leadership and Influence, Tome of Understanding
- **Master (+6) Techniques:.** Master (+6) Techniques: Legendary Scrolls and Staffs, Tome of the Stilled Tongue

###### Inscription Adept

A character can improve their inscription by taking the Inscription Adept feat.

##### Inscription Adept

*Ability Score Increase:* +1 to 1 of your choice from: Intelligence, Wisdom, Charisma

*Prerequisite:* {'level': 4, 'proficiency': [{'tool': ["calligrapher's supplies"]}, {'tool': ["cartographer's tools"]}, {'tool': ["painter's supplies"]}]}

Your mastery of inscription allows you to duplicate the effects of certain spells with your written magic. You gain the following benefits.

###### Inscription Magic

You learn the Illusory Script spell and always have it prepared. You can cast it once without a spell slot, and you regain the ability to cast it in that way when you finish a Long Rest. You can also cast the spell using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level. You can use herbalism resources of the appropriate value in place of the Material component specified for that spell. The spell's spellcasting ability is the ability increased by this feat.

When you reach higher levels of proficiency in inscription, you learn higher-level spells and can cast them in the same way, as shown on the table.

| Proficiency | Spell |
| --- | --- |
| Journeyman (+3) | Glyph of Warding |
| Artisan (+5) | Symbol |

##### Jewelcrafting

*Requires Proficiency with Glassblower's Tools or Jeweler's Tools*

Using Glassblower's Tools or Jeweler's Tools, along with metals and gems (gathered from mining or purchased), and using particular designs, you can craft jewelry and similar items. You can learn to make these items as your skill increases:

- **Apprentice (+2) Designs:.** Apprentice (+2) Designs: Arcane Focus, Glass Bottle, Holy Symbol, Magnifying Glass, Spyglass, Vial, Ruby of the War Mage
- **Journeyman (+3) Designs:.** Journeyman (+3) Designs: Uncommon magic Rings, Amulet of Proof against Detection and Location, Brooch of Shielding, Circlet of Blasting, Elemental Gem, Eyes of Charming, Eyes of Minute Seeing, Gem of Brightness, Headband of Intellect, Medallion of Thoughts, Necklace of Adaptation, Pearl of Power, Periapt of Health, Periapt of Wound Closure, Uncommon Quaal's Feather Tokens
- **Expert (+4) Designs:.** Expert (+4) Designs: Rare magic Rings, Amulet of Health, Bead of Force, Censer of Controlling Air Elementals, Cube of Force, Rare Figurines of Wondrous Power (including the jade panther described in this chapter), Gem of Seeing, Rare Ioun Stones, Necklace of Fireballs, Necklace of Prayer Beads, Periapt of Proof against Poison, Rare Quaal's Feather Tokens

- **Master (+6) Designs:.** Master (+6) Designs: Legendary magic Rings, Crystal Ball of Mind Reading, Crystal Ball of True Seeing, Legendary Ioun Stones, Scarab of Protection

##### Leatherworking

*Requires Proficiency with Cobbler's Tools or Leatherworker's Tools*

Using Cobbler's Tools or Leatherworker's Tools, along with reagents (mostly gathered from skinning), and using particular patterns, you can craft mundane and magical leather armor and similar goods. You can learn to make these items as your skill increases:

- **Apprentice (+2) Patterns:.** Apprentice (+2) Patterns: Sling, Whip, Hide Armor, Leather Armor, Studded Leather Armor, Backpack, Climber's Kit, Crossbow Bolt Case, Map or Scroll Case, Parchment, Pouch, Quiver, Waterskin, Common magic Armor and Weapons in the above categories, Boots of False Tracks

##### Tailoring

*[Image: image (book/WoW/055-03-004.leatherworker.webp)]*

*Requires Proficiency with Weaver's Tools*

Using Weaver's Tools along with reagents (primarily bolts of cloth purchased or found as treasure), and using particular patterns, you can craft cloth goods. You can learn to make these items as your skill increases:

- **Apprentice (+2) Patterns:.** Apprentice (+2) Patterns: Padded Armor, Basket, Bedroll, Blanket, Fine Clothes, Net, Robe, Rope, Sack, String, Tent, Traveler's Clothes, Common magic Padded Armor; Cloak of Billowing, Cloak of Many Fashions, Clothes of Mending, Hat of Vermin, Hat of Wizardry, Rope of Mending, Talking Doll

- **Master (+6) Designs:.** Master (+6) Designs: Legendary magic Padded Armor, Cloak of Invisibility, Robe of the Archmagi

###### Cloth Resources

Cloth resources have the same range of values as resources collected with gathering professions, from 10 GP linen and 25 GP wool to 7,500 GP dawnweave or duskweave, found only in Khaz Algar. Cloth is sometimes found as treasure in the hands of Humanoids who recognize its value as a trade good.

*[Image: Pets and mounts can show off a character's accomplishments. (book/WoW/056-03-005.pets-and-mounts.webp)]*

### Mounts and Vehicles

Mounts are an important tool for every adventurer, not only carrying people and goods across Azeroth but also helping adventurers express their personalities and accomplishments.

#### Mounts for Purchase

The Mounts for Purchase table shows mounts that characters can purchase. Many of the mounts listed there can't be purchased unless a character has achieved a certain level of renown with a particular faction (see 4). These mounts follow the Mounts and Vehicles in the *Player's Handbook*.

#### Crafted Mounts

Characters can use the professions described earlier in this chapter to craft magic items that serve as mounts. Among the best-known such mounts are the Carpet of Flying (crafted with artisan x), the jeweled panther Figurine of Wondrous Power (crafted with expert x and described later in this chapter), and vehicles made with engineering.

##### Powered Vehicles

Powered vehicles are similar to the Large Vehicles described in the *Player's Handbook*. They include the following:

- **Flying Machine:.** Flying Machine: A small gyrocopter with a rotor on top, two side-mounted engines, and skis as landing gear.
- **Goblin Trike:.** Goblin Trike: A three-wheeled motor vehicle with luxurious wood paneling.
- **Mechano-Hog and Mekgineer's Chopper:.** Mechano-Hog and Mekgineer's Chopper: Two powerful motorcycles with optional sidecars, one made by the Horde, the other by the Alliance.

Characters traveling in these vehicles use the vehicle's speed in miles per hour (as shown on the x) to determine their rate of travel, and they don't choose a travel pace. These vehicles require Fuel, which weighs 10 pounds and costs 1 GP for each 8 hours of travel. (Characters can use the alchemy profession to craft Fuel.)

#### Special Mounts

Other mounts can be acquired by characters as adventure rewards, treasure, or renown perks. These include magical mounts that can be summoned and dismissed by means of a command word, as well as more unusual creatures beyond those described above. Here are two examples:

- A character with a Renown Score of 25+ with the Valdrakken Accord (see x) gains a Blessing allowing them to summon a draconic steed.
- A character who uses archaeology to collect six magic fragments from Highmountain tauren dig sites and who is an artisan archaeologist (see "x" in this chapter) can assemble the fragments into a Very Rare Enspelled Staff with the Find Steed spell (level 4 version). The staff summons the spirit of Eche'ro, the famed moose mount of Huln Highmountain.

### Companion Creatures

Characters can acquire companion creatures by a variety of means. Some can be purchased from merchants for a few Gold Pieces, sometimes with a minimum renown threshold. Others are found as treasure, given as rewards for completing an adventure, or even found while using gathering professions. (For example, mining elementium in level 17+ zones can sometimes release an elementium geode, a kind of elemental companion.)

Companion creatures are too small to serve as mounts, too weak to participate meaningfully in combat, and generally incapable of useful independent action. Much like mounts, though, they can serve as badges of honor, marking a character's achievements or the breadth of their travels.

#### Calling a Companion

Companion creatures are magically bound to a character. Some appear when summoned and disappear to some other plane when dismissed. Others are natural creatures bound to magical items (often collars or leashes) that allow a character to summon and dismiss them in the same way. Still others are mechanical or magical constructs that can be deactivated, rather than dismissed, and stored with the character's gear. In any case, summoning or dismissing a companion requires the character to take the Magic action, and a character can have only one companion summoned in this way at a time. Summoning a new companion instantly dismisses a companion that is already present. While summoned, a companion stays within 5 feet of the character by floating or flying nearby, perching on the character's shoulder, or following on foot.

A companion creature can't take actions and can't be targeted by attacks. If it takes any damage (for example, if the character is caught in a Fireball spell's area), it is instantly dismissed or deactivated.

#### Companion Loyalty

A companion creature has a Loyalty Score, using the Loyalty in the *Dungeon Master's Guide*, but its loyalty is to an individual and not to the party. When a character acquires a companion, the companion's Loyalty Score equals the character's Charisma modifier (minimum of 1). The companion's Loyalty Score can't be higher than the character's Charisma.

A companion creature's Loyalty Score increases by 1d4 if the companion is treated particularly well, which might include buying special food for it, pampering it in other ways, or rescuing it from danger. Its Loyalty Score decreases by 1 if it is dismissed or deactivated by taking damage.

If a companion creature's Loyalty Score reaches 10 or higher, the companion gives the character a small supernatural gift while the companion is present. The nature of this gift depends on the companion's category, as described below.

#### Types of Companions

The following sections detail different types of companions characters might discover.

##### Aquatic Companions

Aquatic companion creatures include fish, crabs, frogs, octopi, water birds, turtles, otters, and other water-dwelling creatures. Sometimes they float in a bubble of water near their owners.

###### Blessing of the Aquatic Companion

*Type:* Blessing

While you have an aquatic companion whose Loyalty Score is 10 or higher, you have the Blessing of the Aquatic Companion, which gives you a Swim Speed equal to your Speed.

##### Beast Companions

Beast companion creatures are natural animals that neither fly nor swim. They include Tiny and Small beasts, as well as the young of larger creatures. (These beasts don't age while kept as a companion.)

###### Blessing of the Beast Companion

*Type:* Blessing

While you have a beast companion whose Loyalty Score is 10 or higher, you have the Blessing of the Beast Companion, which gives you proficiency and Expertise in Animal Handling and Survival.

##### Dragonkin Companions

Dragonkin companion creatures include dragon whelplings—younger and smaller than the whelps described in 7—as well as draconic animals native to the x.

###### Blessing of the Dragonkin Companion

*Type:* Blessing

While you have a dragonkin companion whose Loyalty Score is 10 or higher, you have the Blessing of the Dragonkin Companion, which gives you Advantage on saving throws to avoid or end the Frightened condition.

##### Elemental Companions

Elemental companion creatures include animate plants and remnants of the x power as well as living motes of elemental energy.

###### Blessing of the Elemental Companion

*Type:* Blessing

While you have an elemental companion whose Loyalty Score is 10 or higher, you have the Blessing of the Elemental Companion, which gives you Advantage on saving throws to avoid or end the Exhaustion condition.

##### Flying Companions

Flying companions include flying creatures—ordinary birds, bats, moths, bumblebees, and flying snakes—and magical versions of these creatures.

###### Blessing of the Flying Companion

*Type:* Blessing

While you have a flying companion whose Loyalty Score is 10 or higher, you have the Blessing of the Flying Companion, which increases your Speed by 5 feet.

##### Humanoid Companions

Humanoid companions include imps, kobolds, baby murlocs, and a range of other beings that can help you in ways that don't involve combat. The DM is encouraged to develop any humanoid companion as an NPC using the NPCs as Party Members in the *Dungeon Master's Guide*.

###### Blessing of the Humanoid Companion

*Type:* Blessing

While you have a humanoid companion whose Loyalty Score is 10 or higher, you have the Blessing of the Humanoid Companion, which gives you proficiency and Expertise in the Persuasion skill.

##### Magic Companions

Magic companions encompass a variety of beings that are fundamentally magical, including tiny fragments of x or other powers, stray eyes or tentacles of otherworldly origin, oozes, creatures of the Void, ghostly spirits, and animated magical tomes.

###### Blessing of the Magic Companion

*Type:* Blessing

While you have a magic companion whose Loyalty Score is 10 or higher, you have the Blessing of the Magic Companion, which gives you proficiency and Expertise in the Arcana skill.

##### Mechanical Companions

Mechanical companions are clockwork or otherwise engineered creatures, ranging from mechanical chickens to diminutive replicas of the x terrifying fel reaver constructs.

###### Blessing of the Mechanical Companion

*Type:* Blessing

While you have a mechanical companion whose Loyalty Score is 10 or higher, you have the Blessing of the Mechanical Companion, which allows you to roll 1d4 whenever you take Psychic damage and reduce the damage taken by the number rolled.

##### Undead Companions

Undead companions include skeletal or ghostly animals and similar creatures.

###### Blessing of the Undead Companion

*Type:* Blessing

While you have an undead companion whose Loyalty Score is 10 or higher, you have the Blessing of the Undead Companion, which gives you Advantage on Death Saving Throws.

### Magic Items

Many magic items are available to characters in a *World of Warcraft* campaign. The items described here cover the gamut from renowned weapons to simple Potions that characters skilled in alchemy can craft. They follow the Magic Items in the *Dungeon Master's Guide*.

*[Image: Ashbringer (items/WoW/Ashbringer.webp)]*

#### Ashbringer

*Type:* M|XPHB

*Rarity:* artifact

Infused with the Holy Light and forged by King Magni Bronzebeard on the Great Anvil of Ironforge, Ashbringer was once the pride and joy of the knightly Mograine family. But when the traitorous Renault Mograine used the sword to kill his father, it became corrupted. The sword was claimed by the Scourge until Renault's brother, Darion Mograine, betrayed the Lich King and delivered the weapon to Tirion Fordring—an act of heroism that cleansed Ashbringer of its corruption and restored the sword to its rightful form.

##### Magic Weapon

You gain a +3 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. It deals an extra 1d10 Fire damage on a hit, or an extra 3d10 Fire damage if the target is Undead.

##### Changing States

When first encountered, Ashbringer is in either its True State or its Corrupted State. At the DM's discretion, Ashbringer changes into its Corrupted State if its wielder uses it to commit an act of legendary wickedness; it changes into its True State if its wielder uses it to commit an act of legendary heroism. Ashbringer's current state grants you one of the following benefits:

##### Steal Life (Corrupted State Only)

Whenever you use Ashbringer to reduce a creature to 0 Hit Points, you gain 20 Temporary Hit Points.

##### Wake of Ashes (True State Only)

Whenever you use Ashbringer to reduce a creature to 0 Hit Points, you can make its corpse explode into a cloud of smoldering ash. Each creature of your choice in a 10-foot Emanation originating from the target must succeed on a DC 18 Constitution saving throw or have the Blinded condition until the end of its next turn.

##### Sentience

Ashbringer is a sentient weapon with an Intelligence of 14, a Wisdom of 17, and a Charisma of 17. It is Lawful Good in its True State or Lawful Evil in its Corrupted State. It has hearing and Darkvision with a range of 120 feet.

The weapon communicates telepathically with its wielder and speaks Common and Dwarven.

##### Personality

Ashbringer's voice sounds like faint, intermittent whispers. In its True State, Ashbringer seeks to serve the forces of the Light and reduce the unholy to ash. In its Corrupted State, Ashbringer wants to undermine faith in the Light and destroy the righteous. Conflict arises if the wielder doesn't share the goals of Ashbringer's current state.

##### Random Properties

Ashbringer has the following random properties:

- 1 minor beneficial property
- 1 major beneficial property
- 1 minor detrimental property

##### Destroying Ashbringer

Ashbringer can be destroyed only by its creator, Magni Bronzebeard. The weapon will shatter when he strikes it against the Great Anvil of Ironforge.

#### Aviana's Feather

*Rarity:* legendary

While attuned to this golden feather, you can take a Magic action to sprout a pair of glowing, golden wings from your back. The wings give you a Fly Speed of 80 feet, and any creature that makes an Opportunity Attack against you when you fly out of its reach has Disadvantage on the attack roll. If you fall while you have the wings, you descend at a rate of 60 feet per round until you land.

The wings last until you end the effect early as a Magic action or until your Attunement ends. If you are aloft when the wings disappear, you fall.

*[Image: Crown of the Ogre King (items/WoW/Crown of the Ogre King.webp)]*

#### Crown of the Ogre King

*Rarity:* very rare

While wearing this crown, you can take a Magic action to release a powerful belch that can be heard up to 300 feet away. Each creature in a 20-foot Cone originating from you makes a DC 17 Strength saving throw. On a failed save, a creature is pushed up to 20 feet away from you and has the Prone condition.

##### Cursed

This item is cursed. Attuning to it curses you until you are targeted by a Remove Curse spell or similar magic. Removing the crown fails to end the curse on you. While cursed, you are far hungrier than normal. You lose all your table manners, and your daily food requirement triples.

#### Defias Leather

*Type:* LA|XPHB

*Rarity:* rare

Elite members of the Defias Brotherhood wear this armor to honor the faction's stonemason founders. While wearing the armor, you gain the following benefits.

##### Mason's Camouflage

You have Advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks made to hide behind stone objects and structures.

##### Meld into Stone

You can cast Meld into Stone from the armor. Once the armor has been used to cast this spell, it can't be used to cast it again until the next dawn.

*[Image: Doomhammer (items/WoW/Doomhammer.webp)]*

#### Doomhammer

*Type:* M|XPHB

*Rarity:* artifact

Long ago on the world of Draenor, an orc named Gelnar set out to fashion a weapon that would empower his people against their ogre foes. He forged a fearsome hammer in the heat of an elemental pool of lava. Dubbed Doomhammer, this weapon was passed down through generations of Blackrock orcs. An ancient prophecy foretold that possession of the hammer would someday leave the Blackrock lineage; this prophecy came true when Thrall, an orc shaman, proved himself the weapon's worthy wielder.

##### Magic Weapon

You gain a +3 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. It deals an extra 1d8 Lightning damage on a hit.

##### Gelnar's Gifts

While holding Doomhammer, you gain the following benefits:

- **Doom Winds.** Your fury carries the unbridled momentum of a windstorm. While your XPHB is active, your Speed is doubled, and you can take an extra Attack action (one attack only) on each of your turns.
- **Lava Stride.** You take no damage from molten lava and can walk across it as if it were solid ground.
- **Lightning Rod.** When a creature you can see within 60 feet of you would take Lightning damage, you can take a Reaction to reduce the damage to 0.
- **Storm's Passage.** You can cast Wind Walk from this weapon. Once the weapon has been used to cast this spell, it can't be used to cast it again until the next dawn.

##### Random Properties

Doomhammer has the following random properties:

- 2 minor beneficial properties
- 1 major beneficial property
- 1 minor detrimental property

##### Destroying Doomhammer

To destroy Doomhammer, a descendant of Gelnar must hurl the weapon into the eye of a storm in Outland, the remnants of the destroyed world of Draenor.

#### Experimental Goblin Jetpack

*Rarity:* rare

While wearing this pack, you can take a Magic action to fire up its jets. The jets remain active for 1 hour or until you end the effect early as a Magic action. The jets give you a Fly Speed of 60 feet. If you are aloft when the jets become inactive, you fall. When the jets stop, you can't use them again for 1d12 hours.

#### Figurine of Wondrous Power (Jeweled Panther)

*Rarity:* rare

A Figurine of Wondrous Power is a statuette small enough to fit in a pocket. If you take a Magic action to throw the figurine to a point on the ground within 60 feet of yourself, the figurine becomes a living creature specified in the figurine's description below. If the space where the creature would appear is occupied by other creatures or objects, or if there isn't enough space for the creature, the figurine doesn't become a creature.

The creature is Friendly to you and your allies. It understands your languages, obeys your commands, and takes its turn immediately after you on your Initiative count. If you issue no commands, the creature defends itself but takes no other actions.

The creature exists for a duration specific to each figurine. At the end of the duration, the creature reverts to its figurine form. It reverts to a figurine early if its creature form drops to 0 Hit Points or if you take a Magic action while touching the creature to make it revert to figurine form. When the creature becomes a figurine again, its property can't be used again until a certain amount of time has passed, as specified below.

##### Jeweled Panther (Rare)

This statuette is of a panther made from jade, onyx, ruby, sapphire, or sunstone. It can become a Large Panther with a Fly Speed of 30 feet for up to 12 hours and can be ridden as a mount. Once it has been used, it can't be used again until 2 days have passed.

#### Flask of the Titans

*Type:* P|XPHB

*Rarity:* very rare

When you drink this potion, your Hit Point maximum and current Hit Points increase by 30 for 1 hour.

The golden liquid in this flask tastes like honey and gleams with the light of dawn.

*[Image: Frostmourne (book/WoW/060-03-009.frostmourne.webp)]*

#### Frostmourne

*Type:* M|XPHB

*Rarity:* artifact

Runes of domination adorn this icy mourneblade. The Jailer—one of the rulers of the Shadowlands—originally sought to use Frostmourne to subjugate Azeroth, and that cruel intention forever lingers in the form of a prophecy: "Whosoever takes up this blade shall wield power eternal. Just as the blade rends flesh, so must power scar the spirit." Arthas Menethil was the first to discover the prophecy's true meaning when he lost his soul by wielding the sword, ultimately becoming the Lich King.

##### Magic Weapon

You gain a +3 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. It deals an extra 2d12 Cold damage on a hit. Whenever you use Frostmourne to reduce a creature to 0 Hit Points, the creature dies, and its soul is trapped in the sword. The target can't be raised from the dead until Frostmourne is destroyed.

##### Pawn of the Lich King

While you're attuned to Frostmourne, the Lich King can see through your eyes, hear through your ears, and communicate with you telepathically over any distance, even across planes.

##### Prophecy of Frostmourne

When you attune to Frostmourne, your creature type becomes Undead, and your alignment becomes Neutral Evil. These changes remain even if your Attunement ends.

##### Twisted Aura

If you are a Paladin, you also gain the following benefits while attuned to Frostmourne:

- **Marshal Undead.** Undead creatures of your choice (excluding you) in your XPHB have Advantage on attack rolls and saving throws.
- **Numb to Cold.** Allies in your XPHB have Immunity to Cold damage.

##### Random Properties

Frostmourne has the following random properties:

- 1 major beneficial property
- 1 minor detrimental property
- 1 major detrimental property

##### Destroying Frostmourne

Frostmourne shatters if its wielder is slain by the legendary sword Ashbringer. Only the Runecarver, who forged the weapon at the Jailer's command, can reforge Frostmourne from the resulting fragments.

#### Goblin Jumper Cables

*Rarity:* uncommon

As a Magic action, you can attach these cables to a dead creature and attempt to return the creature to life. Make a DC 15 Intelligence (Arcana) check using Tinker's Tools. On a successful check, the creature revives with 1 Hit Point as long as the creature has been dead no longer than 1 minute and didn't die of old age. On a failed check, you take 2d6 Lightning damage.

Once used, a set of Goblin Jumper Cables can't be used again until the next dawn.

#### Hearthstone

*Rarity:* uncommon

A Hearthstone teleports you to a predetermined location—usually an inn. Activating the stone takes 1 minute and a Magic action. You appear in an unoccupied space within 30 feet of the destination you previously set.

While you are at an inn (or other locations at the DM's discretion), you can take a Magic action to set that location as the destination for your Hearthstone's teleportation. That location becomes your destination until you change it.

One Hearthstone doesn't count against the number of magic items you can have Attunement to.

#### Helm of Domination

*Rarity:* artifact

This ornate helm is infused with the power of domination—a form of runic magic intended to utterly suppress the will of another being. The helm (along with its companion Artifact, Frostmourne) allowed its wearers—three successive Lich Kings—to rule the Scourge.

##### Attuning to the Helm

When you attune to the helm, make a DC 18 Charisma saving throw. On a successful save, you take 10d6 Psychic damage. On a failed save, your soul merges with the Lich King, who then takes control of your body like a puppet, and you become an NPC under the DM's control.

While attuned to the helm, you are unwilling to part with the helm, wearing it at all times, and you can't voluntarily end your Attunement to the helm.

##### Properties of the Helm

While attuned to the helm, your alignment is Neutral Evil, and you gain the following benefits:

- **Command the Scourge.** As a Magic action, you can send a psychic command to each Undead that is part of the Scourge. Make an ability check using your spellcasting ability (DC 20). On a successful check, you can issue a general command to the Undead Scourge, such as "attack any creature you come across" or "guard my stronghold," or you can issue a command to a specific member of the Scourge, such as "deliver a plague to Stormwind." Whether or not you succeed, this property of the helm can't be used again until the next dawn.
- **Truesight.** You have Truesight with a range of 120 feet.
- **Spellcasting.** The helm has 8 charges and regains 1d4 + 4 expended charges daily at dawn. While wearing the helm, you can cast one of the spells on the following table from it (save DC 18). The table indicates how many charges you must expend to cast the spell.
  | Spell | Charge Cost |
  | --- | --- |
  | Animate Dead | 1 |
  | Clairvoyance | 2 |
  | Dream | 4 |
  | Speak with Dead | 1 |
  | Telepathy | 5 |
  | Word of Recall | 5 |
  

##### Random Properties

The Helm of Domination has the following random properties:

- 1 major beneficial property
- 1 minor detrimental property
- 1 major detrimental property

##### Destroying the Helm of Domination

The helm is shattered if struck by a blade forged in the Shadowlands. Only the Runecarver, who originally forged the helm, can reforge it from the fragments.

#### Judgment Armor

*Type:* HA|XPHB

*Rarity:* legendary

While wearing this armor, you are under the effects of the Detect Evil and Good and Protection from Evil and Good spells (no Concentration required). If you are a Paladin, the effects of Protection from Evil and Good extend to creatures of your choice in your XPHB.

*[Image: Life-Staff of the Web Lair (items/WoW/Life-Staff of the Web Lair.webp)]*

#### Life-Staff of the Web Lair

*Type:* M|XPHB

*Rarity:* legendary

This staff has 8 charges.

##### Arachnid's Grace

While attuned to the staff, you can move up, down, and across vertical surfaces and along ceilings without using your hands. You also gain a Climb Speed equal to your Speed. You ignore movement restrictions caused by webs, and you know the location of any other creature in contact with the same web.

##### Web

While holding the staff, you can expend 1 charge to cast Web (save DC 18) from it.

##### Vampiric Silk

While holding the staff, you can take a Bonus Action and expend 1 charge to have a creature in the area of your Web spell make a DC 18 Constitution saving throw. (The creature makes the save with Disadvantage if it has the Restrained condition from the webbing.) The creature takes 4d10 Necrotic damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. Either way, you regain Hit Points equal to half the damage dealt.

##### Regaining Charges

The staff regains 1d6 + 2 expended charges daily at dawn. If you expend the last charge, roll 1d20. On a 1, the staff dissolves into a swarm of harmless spiders and is destroyed.

#### Noggenfogger Elixir

*Type:* P|XPHB

*Rarity:* rare

Noggenfogger Elixir is an unpredictable potion invented by Marin Noggenfogger, the trade prince of the Steamwheedle Cartel. It is highly coveted by adventurers (particularly goblins), despite or perhaps because of its unpredictable effects. When you drink this potion, roll 1d6: On a 1-2, you take on a skeletal appearance for 1d4 hours, during which time you don't need to breathe. (Not breathing doesn't give you Exhaustion levels.) On a 3-4, you gain the Reduce effect of the Enlarge/Reduce spell for 1d4 hours (no Concentration required). On a 5-6, your rate of descent while falling slows to 60 feet per round for 10 minutes. During that time, you take no damage from falling.

Drinking multiple Noggenfogger Elixirs doesn't require you to roll on the Potion Miscibility table in the Dungeon Master's Guide.

This green potion is oily in the mouth.

#### Philosopher's Stone

*Rarity:* very rare

A Philosopher's Stone is a powerful reagent used in alchemy, and it can be effectively used only by the alchemist who created it. While you carry it, you gain the following benefits.

##### Spellcasting Focus

You can use your Philosopher's Stone as a Spellcasting Focus for your spells.

##### Transmute Resources

If you spend 1 hour (which can be concurrent with a Short Rest) with your Philosopher's Stone and crafting resources of a single kind (fish, gems, herbs, ores or stones, leather or hide, or cloth), you can magically transmute the resources into resources of a different kind but the same value. Thus, you can convert 100 GP worth of herbs into 100 GP worth of ore or vice versa, or change 500 GP of mithril (a common ore) into 500 GP of truesilver (a rare ore).

##### Mixology

When you roll a die to determine the effect of a potion you drink (including the result of mixing potions), you can roll the die twice and choose either result. If a potion you drink has a duration, that duration is doubled.

*[Could not resolve item 'Potions of Mana' (WoW)]*

*[Could not resolve item 'Potions of Rejuvenation' (WoW)]*

#### Rod of the Sleepwalker

*Type:* RD|XDMG

*Rarity:* very rare

This rod puts creatures into a suggestible, sleepwalking state. You can cast Dominate Monster (save DC 17) from this rod, with the following modifications:

##### Duration

The spell's duration is Concentration, up to 8 hours.

##### Immunity

Creatures that don't sleep, such as elves, or that have Immunity to the Exhaustion condition automatically succeed on the save.

##### Memory

An affected creature remembers nothing from the spell's duration once the spell ends.

Once the rod has been used to cast the spell in this way, it can't be used to do so again until the next dawn.

#### Shadowfang

*Type:* M|XPHB

*Rarity:* rare

You gain a +1 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon.

##### Mark of the Worgen

In most circumstances, the weapon appears unremarkable. But under the night sky, ragged fur and sharp fangs sprout around the sword's cross guard. While transformed in this way, Shadowfang deals an extra 2d6 Necrotic damage on a hit. The weapon returns to normal at dawn or if it is brought indoors.

#### Shalamayne

*Type:* M|XPHB

*Rarity:* artifact

When dragon magic split King Varian Wrynn into two versions of himself, one of his selves wielded the night elven blade Shalla'tor, while the other wielded the night elven blade Ellemayne. Varian eventually merged his twin selves back into one whole. In doing so, he inadvertently fused the blades into a new weapon called Shalamayne.

The Jailer, one of the rulers of the Shadowlands, once stole and reforged Shalamayne into a mourneblade called Kingsmourne. But Varian's son, Anduin Wrynn, later restored Shalamayne to its rightful form. Anduin is the sword's last known wielder.

##### Magic Weapon

You gain a +3 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. It deals an extra 2d12 Force damage on a hit.

##### Dual Mastery

If you have the Weapon Mastery feature and choose Greatsword as one of your mastered weapons, you can use both the Graze and Sap mastery properties at once while wielding Shalamayne.

##### Splitting and Reuniting the Sword

While holding Shalamayne, you can take a Magic action to end your Attunement to the weapon and split it into two magic swords: Shalla'tor and Ellemayne. Each is a +3 Longsword with the Finesse and Light properties. A creature who holds both swords can take a Magic action to rejoin them into Shalamayne, attuning to the weapon instantly.

##### Random Properties

Shalamayne has the following random properties:

- 2 minor beneficial properties
- 1 major beneficial property

##### Destroying Shalamayne

One can destroy Shalamayne by splitting it into Shalla'tor and Ellemayne, then burying one or both swords at the roots of a World Tree in the Emerald Dream. After one year, the buried swords dissolve into the soil.

*[Image: Staff of Light and Shadow—Anathema form (items/WoW/Staff of Light and Shadow (Anathema).webp)]*

#### Staff of Light and Shadow

*Type:* M|XPHB

*Rarity:* legendary

This staff has two forms: a shadowy state known as Anathema and a holy state known as Benediction. While attuned to the staff, you can switch the staff from one state to the other at dawn.

##### Anathema

As Anathema, the staff grants the following benefits:

- **Necrotic Resistance.** While attuned to the staff, you have Resistance to Necrotic damage.
- **Shadow Mana.** While you're holding the staff, when you cast a school=N from the Necromancy school that would expend a spell slot, you can roll 1d6. If the number on the die exceeds the spell's level, the spell slot isn't expended.

##### Benediction

As Benediction, the staff grants the following benefits:

- **Radiant Resistance.** While attuned to the staff, you have Resistance to Radiant Damage.
- **Light-Blessed Healing.** While you're holding the staff, when you cast a spell that would restore Hit Points to a creature, you can treat the spell as if it had been cast using a spell slot of 1 level higher.

*[Image: Sulfuras, Hand of Ragnaros (items/WoW/Sulfuras, Hand of Ragnaros.webp)]*

#### Sulfuras, Hand of Ragnaros

*Type:* M|XPHB

*Rarity:* artifact

The elementium Maul Sulfuras is the weapon of Ragnaros, the Elemental Lord of Fire. In the beginning, the four elemental lords warred against one another for dominance over the nascent Azeroth. While the other elemental lords fought with mastery of their own elements, Ragnaros used Sulfuras to turn their elements against them, boiling seas, choking the skies with smoke and embers, and turning mountains into erupting volcanoes.

##### Magic Weapon

You gain a +3 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. A creature hit by this weapon takes an extra 1d10 Fire damage and starts burning, taking 1d10 Fire damage at the start of each of its turns instead of the normal burning damage.

##### Ignite the Elements

While holding this weapon, you can take a Magic action to produce one of the following effects. Once an effect has been used, that effect can't be used again until the next dawn:

- **Boiling Point.** You cause any container or body of water within 1 mile of you to boil for 1 minute. A creature that enters the boiling water or starts its turn there takes 2d10 Fire damage.
- **Embersmoke.** You cast Incendiary Cloud (save DC 18) from the weapon.
- **Eruption.** You make lava erupt in a 10-foot-radius, 60-foot-high Cylinder centered on a point on the ground you can see within 120 feet. Each creature in that area makes a DC 18 Dexterity saving throw, taking 9d10 Fire damage plus 9d10 Bludgeoning damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

##### Random Properties

Sulfuras has the following random properties:

- 1 minor beneficial property
- 1 major beneficial property
- 1 minor detrimental property
- 1 major detrimental property

##### Destroying Sulfuras

In an underwater tunnel network deep beneath Vashj'ir lies an abandoned, heavily trapped temple to Neptulon, the Elemental Lord of Water. Placing Sulfuras on the temple's central altar quenches its fire permanently, rendering it nonmagical and destructible.

*[Could not resolve item 'Troll's Blood Elixirs' (WoW)]*

#### Twin Blades of Azzinoth

*Type:* M|XPHB

*Rarity:* artifact

This pair of demonic warglaives once belonged to Azzinoth, an ancient doomguard commander of the Burning Legion. Illidan Stormrage slew Azzinoth millennia ago during the War of the Ancients and reforged the blades for himself, allowing him to use fel magic against the demons of the Legion.

##### Magic Weapons

You gain a +3 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with these magic weapons. Each deals an extra 2d8 Psychic damage if the target is a Fiend; this Psychic damage ignores Resistance and Immunity.

##### Fel Synchronization

If you hit the same creature with both weapons on a turn, that creature makes a DC 18 Wisdom saving throw immediately after the second weapon hits. On a failed save, the creature is racked by fel magic and has the Stunned condition until the end of its next turn.

##### Flames of Azzinoth

As a Magic action, you can throw one of the weapons to an unoccupied space on the ground you can see within 30 feet of you. The blade transforms into a fiery green fiend as if you had cast the Summon Fiend spell (choose the Devil option). The spell lasts for 1 minute, and when the summoned fiend disappears, the weapon reappears in your hand. You can't turn that weapon into a Fiend again until 7 days have passed.

##### Random Properties

The Twin Blades of Azzinoth have the following random properties:

- 1 minor beneficial property
- 1 major beneficial property
- 1 minor detrimental property

##### Destroying the Blades

Reciting an ancient poem called "Gift of the Light" over the blades causes them to dissolve into glittering dust. This poem has largely been lost to time, but a few rare naaru remember it.

#### Vithrak, Gaze of the Deadman

*Type:* RG|XDMG

*Rarity:* very rare

This ring has 3 charges and regains 1d3 expended charges daily at dawn. While wearing the ring, you can take a Magic action and expend 1 charge to learn the Resistances, Vulnerabilities, and Immunities (if any) of a creature you can see within 120 feet of yourself. When you use this effect, your eyes briefly become glassy, gray husks.

## Chapter 4: Factions and Renown

*[Image: Sylvanas Windrunner and Anduin Wrynn battle at the forefront of the Horde and the Alliance. (book/WoW/064-04-001.sylvanas-windrunner-splash.webp)]*

Azeroth is defined by its factions—nations, organizations, cartels, and pacts that claim members from around the world. As characters interact with these factions, they can build a reputation and earn tangible and intangible rewards.

This chapter builds on the Renown found in the *Dungeon Master's Guide* to track characters' standing with the major factions of Azeroth. In addition, characters might gain renown with smaller groups to achieve short-term goals. *World of Warcraft* offers abundant examples that can be adapted into a tabletop campaign. For example, characters seeking a route from Felwood into Winterspring might work to gain renown with the furbolgs of Timbermaw Hold, who will allow passage through their tunnels once at least one member of the party has achieved a Renown Score of 10+.

### Starting Renown

Most characters created using the origins in chapter 2 have a starting Renown Score of 3 with one of the subfactions associated with either the Alliance or the Horde. A few species and class options give starting Renown Scores with other factions.

### General Renown Perks

The perks listed here apply to all the factions described in this chapter:

- **Friendly (Renown 3+).** Friendly (Renown 3+) Other members of the faction are Friendly to you by default.
- **Honored (Renown 10+).** Honored (Renown 10+) You can purchase equipment found in the Player's Handbook, as well as Common or Uncommon magic items, from merchants of the faction at a 10% discount.
- **Revered (Renown 25+).** Revered (Renown 25+) Your discount increases to 15% and extends to Rare magic items.
- **Exalted (Renown 50+).** Exalted (Renown 50+) Your discount increases to 25% and extends to Very Rare magic items.

Certain mounts (see 3) and magic items are available only from merchants associated with a particular faction and might require a minimum Renown Score with that faction to purchase.

### The Alliance

*[Image: image (book/WoW/065-04-002.alliance-emblem.webp)]*

The peoples of the Alliance are bound together by proud traditions of nobility, honor, faith, justice, and sacrifice. The disparate cultures that make up this faction contribute technical, arcane, and spiritual wisdom toward the goal of a peaceful and just world. The highest ideals of the Alliance are tradition, justice, and duty, matching closely to the Lawful Good alignment.

The Alliance is a military coalition whose leadership is dependent on the unified will of its member peoples. By default, the high king (or other leader) of Stormwind has served as commander of the Alliance, but the commander has authority over only the military forces that other Alliance leaders commit to the Alliance's cause. Thus, in practice, the human king leads as part of a council made up of all the Alliance leaders.

#### Alliance Renown Perks

The Alliance consists of many subfactions. Instead of having a Renown Score with the Alliance, characters gain renown perks based on their Renown Scores with the subfactions of the Alliance, as described below. The perks listed in "x" apply to specific subfactions, not to the Alliance as a whole.

##### Alliance: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with Any Alliance Subfaction*

Creatures associated with the Alliance are Friendly to you by default.

##### Alliance: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with Any Alliance Subfaction*

If your 10 is in territory controlled by the Alliance, when you issue the Maintain order to your Bastion, the DM rolls twice on the Bastion Events table, and you pick which event occurs.

##### Ambassador of the Alliance

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with Darnassus, the**Exodar**, Gilneas, Gnomeregan, Ironforge, and Stormwind*

You have the title Ambassador of the Alliance. You can gain an audience with the leader of any Alliance subfaction at any time, and you can summon the leaders of the Alliance forces into council, given 1 week's notice. For a worthy cause, any or all of these leaders will commit troops to aid you, at the DM's discretion. You can also call on Alleria Windrunner, Anduin Wrynn, or Jaina Proudmoore to assist you on one adventure.

> **Training**
> Several renown perks give you the opportunity to work with a trainer to learn a language or gain a proficiency. The days required needn't be consecutive, but you must spend 8 consecutive hours with the trainer for a day to count. You can gain only one tool proficiency from each renown perk.

#### Alliance Subfactions

*[Image: Leaders of the Alliance (Left to Right): Shandris Feathermoon, Anduin Wrynn, Muradin Bronzebeard, Aysa Cloudsinger, Gelbin Mekkatorque, Prophet Velen, and Tess Greymane (book/WoW/066-04-003.leaders-of-the-alliance.webp)]*

The Alliance is made up of members of many different species, and the most prominent among those species are the subfactions of the Alliance. Most of these subfactions are named for their capital city, as shown on the list below. The species descriptions in 2 provide more information about these subfactions.

##### Alliance Subfactions

- x (night elves)
- x (draenei)
- x (worgen)
- x (gnomes)
- x (dwarves)
- x (dracthyr)
- x (humans)
- x (pandaren)

If your species gives you a starting Renown Score with an Alliance subfaction, gaining renown with different Alliance subfactions also increases your Renown Score with your starting faction as described below:

- **Friendly.** Friendly The first time your Renown Score with each Alliance faction other than your starting faction reaches 3, increase your Renown Score with your starting faction by 1.
- **Honored.** Honored The first time your Renown Score with each Alliance faction other than your starting faction reaches 10, increase your Renown Score with your starting faction by 2.
- **Revered.** Revered The first time your Renown Score with each Alliance faction other than your starting faction reaches 25, increase your Renown Score with your starting faction by 3.
- **Exalted.** Exalted The first time your Renown Score with each Alliance faction other than your starting faction reaches 50, increase your Renown Score with your starting faction by 5.

#### Darnassus Renown Perks

The Darnassus faction represents the night elves of Azeroth, wherever they actually reside, who acknowledge the leadership of Shandris Feathermoon (in the present age) or her parents, High Priestess Tyrande Whisperwind and Archdruid Malfurion Stormrage. The faction also encompasses the void elves led by Alleria Windrunner, based in Telogrus Rift.

While your 10 is in Darnassus or Teldrassil (or in Bel'ameth or Amirdrassil after the destruction of Teldrassil), your Renown Score with Darnassus increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with Darnassus gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Darnassus: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with Darnassus*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Darnassian or Thalassian or to gain proficiency with the Herbalism Kit (see the "x" sidebar).

##### Darnassus: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with Darnassus*

When you succeed on a check to gather herbs using herbalism (see x), you gain one extra resource, and you find a rare resource if you beat the check DC by 4 or more.

##### Darnassus: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with Darnassus*

You can request a Blessing from the leaders of Darnassus before you undertake a quest on their behalf. You receive one of the Blessings described in the *Dungeon Master's Guide* (your choice), and it lasts until you complete or abandon the quest. Once you request this Blessing, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Darnassus increases.

##### Darnassus: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with Darnassus*

You can call on a Warden (Questing Knight), a leading Sentinel (Warrior Commander) with three Sentinel scouts (Scout Captains), or a leading priestess of Elune (Archpriest) to assist you for a single adventure. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Darnassus increases.

#### Exodar Renown Perks

The *Exodar* faction represents the draenei who came to Azeroth aboard that vessel under the leadership of Prophet Velen, as well as the Lightforged draenei of the *Vindicaar*.

While your 10 is within the *Exodar*, within the *Vindicaar*, or on the Azuremyst Isles, your Renown Score with the *Exodar* increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with the *Exodar* gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Exodar: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the**Exodar*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn the Draenei language or gain proficiency with Glassblower's Tools or Jeweler's Tools (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Exodar: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the**Exodar*

When you use jewelcrafting to craft an item (see x), the time required to craft it is halved.

##### Exodar: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the**Exodar*

You can request a Charms from the leaders of the *Exodar* before you undertake a quest on their behalf. You receive a Charm of Heroism, Charm of Restoration, or Charm of Vitality (your choice; see the *Dungeon Master's Guide*), which lasts until you expend it or you complete or abandon the quest. Once you request a Charm, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with the *Exodar* increases.

##### Exodar: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the**Exodar*

You can call on fifty draenei soldiers (Warrior Infantry) led by three sergeants (Warrior Veterans) and one commander (Warrior Commander) to assist you for a single adventure. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with the *Exodar* increases.

#### Gilneas Renown Perks

The Gilneas faction represents the nation of Gilneas and its people, including its worgen. Its leader is Queen Tess Greymane since the abdication of her father, Genn Greymane.

While your 10 is anywhere in Gilneas, your Renown Score with Gilneas increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with Gilneas gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Gilneas: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with Gilneas*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Common or Common Sign Language or gain proficiency with Leatherworker's Tools (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Gilneas: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with Gilneas*

You gain a +2 bonus to ability checks you make to skin a creature (see x).

##### Gilneas: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with Gilneas*

You can request a Charm of Animal Conjuring from the leaders of Gilneas before you undertake a quest on their behalf, which lasts until you expend it or you complete or abandon the quest. When you use the Charm, the conjured animal spirits appear as worgen spirits from the Emerald Dream. Once you request a Charm, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Gilneas increases.

##### Gilneas: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with Gilneas*

You can call on a pack of six worgen skirmishers (Berserkers) with one leader (Berserker Commander) to assist you for a single adventure. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Gilneas increases.

#### Gnomeregan Renown Perks

The Gnomeregan faction encompasses gnomes and mechagnomes who acknowledge the leadership of King Gelbin Mekkatorque. (Before the reclamation of Gnomeregan, the faction was known as Gnomeregan Exiles.) Most of them live in Tinkertown (a neighborhood of Ironforge) or the settlement of New Tinkertown.

While your 10 is in Gnomeregan, in New Tinkertown, or on Mechagon Island, your Renown Score with Gnomeregan increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with Gnomeregan gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Gnomeregan: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with Gnomeregan*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Gnomish or gain proficiency with Tinker's Tools (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Gnomeregan: Honored

*[Image: image (book/WoW/067-04-004.gnomeregan.webp)]*

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with Gnomeregan*

When you use engineering to craft an item (see x), the time required to craft it is halved.

##### Gnomeregan: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with Gnomeregan*

You can request a complicated engineering device from the leaders of Gnomeregan before you undertake a quest on their behalf. You receive a mechanical device that functions as a Wand of Wonder, but it can't regain charges, and it explodes dramatically but harmlessly when its last charge is expended. The device doesn't require Attunement, but it functions only for you.

##### Gnomeregan: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with Gnomeregan*

In recognition of your service to gnomekind, you are given a Shield Guardian that serves you. If your Renown Score with Gnomeregan falls below 50, the guardian turns on you.
