#### Ironforge Renown Perks

The Ironforge faction encompasses dwarves who acknowledge the leadership of the Council of Three Hammers, the government formed by the union of the Bronzebeard, Dark Iron, and Wildhammer clans, which includes some Frostborn and other dwarves who are affiliated with the Alliance.

While your 10 is in Ironforge, elsewhere in Khaz Modan (except gnome settlements), in or near Aerie Peak, or in Shadowforge City, your Renown Score with Ironforge increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with Ironforge gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Ironforge: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with Ironforge*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Dwarven or gain proficiency with Mason's Tools or Smith's Tools (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Ironforge: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with Ironforge*

When you use blacksmithing to craft an item (see x), the time required to craft it is halved.

##### Ironforge: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with Ironforge*

You can request a dwarven relic from the leaders of Ironforge before you undertake a quest on their behalf. You receive a Belt of Dwarvenkind, Dwarven Plate, or a Dwarven Thrower (your choice). You must return the relic on the completion of the quest; failure to do so reduces your Renown Score with Ironforge by 1d8.

##### Ironforge: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with Ironforge*

You can call on fifty dwarf soldiers (Warrior Infantry) led by three sergeants (Warrior Veterans) and one commander (Warrior Commander) to assist you for a single adventure. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Ironforge increases.

#### Obsidian Warders Renown Perks

The Obsidian Warders is the dracthyr weyrn that has joined the Alliance, led by Scalecommander Azurathel.

Increasing your Renown Score with the Obsidian Warders gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Obsidian Warders: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Obsidian Warders*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Draconic or gain proficiency in History (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Obsidian Warders: Honored

###### Sight of the Earth-Warder

*Type:* Blessing

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Obsidian Warders*

You receive the Sight of the Earth-Warder. This Blessings gives you Advantage on Initiative rolls and Wisdom (Perception) checks. You lose the Blessing if your Renown Score with the Obsidian Warders falls below 10.

##### Obsidian Warders: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Obsidian Warders*

You can request a Blessing from the leaders of the Obsidian Warders before you undertake a quest on their behalf. You receive the Blessing of Protection (see the *Dungeon Master's Guide*), and it lasts until you complete or abandon the quest. Once you request this Blessing, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with the Obsidian Warders increases.

##### Obsidian Warders: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Obsidian Warders*

You can call on a Black Dragonflight Whelp to assist you for a single adventure. If the dragon is killed, your Renown Score with the Obsidian Warders is reduced by 2d6. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with the Obsidian Warders increases.

#### Stormwind Renown Perks

The Stormwind faction encompasses the remnants of most of the human kingdoms of Azeroth, including Kul Tiras, Dalaran (but see "x" later in this chapter), and Stromgarde but excluding Gilneas. Stormwind remains the strongest human nation on Azeroth, under the leadership of Anduin Wrynn (and the regent of Stormwind, Turalyon, in his absence). Lady Jaina Proudmoore of Kul Tiras and Danath Trollbane of the League of Arathor (in the rebuilt Stromgarde Keep) acknowledge Stormwind's place as the keystone of the Alliance.

While your 10 is in Elwynn Forest, Kul Tiras, Stormwind City, Stromgarde, or Westfall, your Renown Score with Stormwind increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with Stormwind gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Stormwind: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with Stormwind*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Common or Common Sign Language or gain proficiency with Weaver's Tools (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Stormwind: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with Stormwind*

When you use tailoring to craft an item (see x), the time required to craft it is halved.

##### Stormwind: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with Stormwind*

You can request a supernatural gift from the leaders of Stormwind before you undertake a quest on their behalf. You receive any Blessings or Charms from the *Dungeon Master's Guide*, and it lasts until you complete or abandon the quest or expend it. Once you request this gift, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Stormwind increases.

##### Stormwind: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with Stormwind*

You can call on Stormwind forces to assist you for a single adventure. You receive your choice of one of the following groups:

- Fifty human soldiers (Warrior Infantry) led by three sergeants (Warrior Veterans) and one commander (Warrior Commander)
- Three SI:7 agents (Scout Captains) and their leader (Spy Master)
- A champion of the Holy Light (Questing Knight)
- A Kul Tiran admiral (Pirate Admiral) with a Warship (you must hire the ship's crew)

Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Stormwind increases.

#### Tushui Renown Perks

The Tushui faction, led by Aysa Cloudsinger, represents those pandaren who have given their allegiance to the Alliance.

Increasing your Renown Score with the Tushui gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Tushui: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Tushui*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn the Pandaren language or gain proficiency with Brewer's Supplies or Cook's Utensils (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Tushui: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Tushui*

If you spend a Short Rest in silent meditation, you gain Heroic Inspiration when you finish the rest.

##### Tushui: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Tushui*

You receive the Blessing of Understanding (see the *Dungeon Master's Guide*). You lose the Blessing if your Renown Score with the Tushui falls below 25.

##### Tushui: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Tushui*

A Tushui monk establishes a Meditation Chamber special facility in your Bastion. This facility doesn't count toward the total number of special facilities in your Bastion. The monk departs and the facility becomes unusable if your Renown Score with the Tushui falls below 50.

### The Horde

*[Image: image (book/WoW/068-04-005.horde-emblem.webp)]*

The proud nations of the Horde are loosely joined in an alliance of convenience against a hostile world that would see them destroyed. Focused and ferocious, the Horde values strength and honor but struggles to keep its members' aggression in check. The Horde's ideals are unity, freedom, and hope, matching closely to the Chaotic Good alignment.

From the time of its founding until the end of the Fourth War, the Horde was led by a single warchief. The orc shaman Thrall held that position from before the Third War until he stepped down to join the x (described later in this chapter) during the Cataclysm. He was succeeded by the warmongering Garrosh Hellscream, who was in turn deposed by a combination of Alliance pressure and a rebellion of the Darkspear trolls. The troll chieftain Vol'jin then took up the mantle, becoming the first non-orc to hold the title, but Vol'jin died during the Burning Legion's third invasion of Azeroth. The undead high elf Sylvanas Windrunner then became warchief, but she ultimately abandoned the Horde to pursue her own agenda. Since her departure, a Horde Council made up of the leaders of the Horde's subfactions has governed the Horde.

#### Horde Renown Perks

The Horde is composed of subfactions. Instead of having a Renown Score with the Horde, characters gain renown perks based on their Renown Scores with the subfactions of the Horde, as described below. The perks listed in "x" apply to specific subfactions, not to the Horde as a whole.

##### Horde: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with Any Horde Subfaction*

Creatures associated with the Horde are Friendly to you by default.

##### Horde: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with Any Horde Subfaction*

If your 10 is in territory controlled by the Horde, when you issue the Maintain order to your Bastion, the DM rolls twice on the Bastion Events table, and you can pick which event occurs.

##### Ambassador of the Horde

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with Bilgewater Cartel, Darkspear Tribe, Orgrimmar, Silvermoon City, Thunder Bluff, and Undercity*

You carry the title Ambassador of the Horde. You can gain an audience with the leader of any Horde subfaction at any time, and you can summon the leaders of the Horde forces into council, given 1 week's notice. For a worthy cause, any or all of these leaders will commit troops to aid you, at the DM's discretion. You can also call on Sylvanas Windrunner or Thrall (both described in chapter 7) to assist you on one adventure.

#### Horde Subfactions

*[Image: Leaders of the Horde (Left to Right): Lilian Voss, Thrall, Monte Gazlowe, Lor'themar Theron, Baine Bloodhoof, Rokhan, and Ji Firepaw (book/WoW/069-04-006.leaders-of-the-horde.webp)]*

The Horde is made up of members of many different species, and the most prominent among those species are the subfactions of the Horde. These subfactions are generally identified by their capital city, as shown on the list below. The species descriptions in chapter 2 provide more information about these subfactions.

##### Horde Subfactions

- x (goblins)
- x (dracthyr)
- x (trolls)
- x (pandaren)
- x (orcs and vulpera)
- x (blood elves)
- x (tauren)
- x (Forsaken)

If your species gives you a starting Renown Score with a Horde subfaction, gaining renown with different Horde subfactions also increases your Renown Score with your starting faction as described below:

- **Friendly.** Friendly The first time your Renown Score with each Horde faction other than your starting faction reaches 3, increase your Renown Score with your starting faction by 1.
- **Honored.** Honored The first time your Renown Score with each Horde faction other than your starting faction reaches 10, increase your Renown Score with your starting faction by 2.
- **Revered.** Revered The first time your Renown Score with each Horde faction other than your starting faction reaches 25, increase your Renown Score with your starting faction by 3.
- **Exalted.** Exalted The first time your Renown Score with each Horde faction other than your starting faction reaches 50, increase your Renown Score with your starting faction by 5.

#### Bilgewater Cartel Renown Perks

The Bilgewater Cartel represents goblins aligned with the Horde. The cartel's capital is Bilgewater Harbor in Azshara, and it has significant holdings across Azeroth. The cartel's leader is Trade Prince Monte Gazlowe, who succeeded Jastor Gallywix in that role after Jastor abandoned the cartel. The cartel's business has been described as "the classical goblin arts: building things and blowing them up." Engineering, construction, infrastructure, and demolitions are all in the Bilgewater wheelhouse.

While your 10 is in Bilgewater Harbor or any other Bilgewater holding, your Renown Score with the Bilgewater Cartel increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with the Bilgewater Cartel gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Bilgewater Cartel: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Bilgewater Cartel*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn the Goblin language or gain proficiency with Alchemist's Supplies (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Bilgewater Cartel: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Bilgewater Cartel*

When you use alchemy to craft an item (see x), the time required to craft it is halved.

##### Bilgewater Cartel: Revered

###### Clockwerk Charm

*Type:* Charm

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Bilgewater Cartel*

You can request a Clockwerk Charm from the leaders of the Bilgewater Cartel before you undertake a quest on their behalf. This Charms allows you to cast the Summon Construct spell (Metal only), which lasts for 1 hour and doesn't require Concentration. The Construct Spirit appears as a mechanical goblin. When it drops to 0 Hit Points or the spell ends, the summoned spirit explodes in a 10-foot-radius Sphere of fire. Creatures in the Sphere make a DC 13 Dexterity saving throw, taking 2d6 Fire damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

The Charm lasts until you use it once or you complete or abandon the quest. Once you request this Charm, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with the Bilgewater Cartel increases.

##### Bilgewater Cartel: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Bilgewater Cartel*

You can call on a cartel captain (Pirate Admiral) with six crew members (Pirates) to assist you for a single adventure. The captain has a Sailing Ship, but you need to hire additional crew (at your own expense) to use the ship. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with the Bilgewater Cartel increases.

#### Dark Talons Renown Perks

The Dark Talons is the dracthyr weyrn that has joined the Horde, led by Scalecommander Cindrethresh.

Increasing your Renown Score with the Dark Talons gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Dark Talons: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Dark Talons*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Draconic or gain proficiency in History (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Dark Talons: Honored

###### Strike of the Earth-Warder

*Type:* Blessing

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Dark Talons*

You receive the Strike of the Earth-Warder. This Blessings improves your Unarmed Strikes, giving you a +1 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with them and increasing the saving throw DC of your Grapple and Shove options by 1. You lose the Blessing if your Renown Score with the Dark Talons falls below 10.

##### Dark Talons: Revered

###### Charm of Terror

*Type:* Charm

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Dark Talons*

You can request a Charms from the leaders of the Dark Talons before you undertake a quest on their behalf. You receive the Charm of Terror, and it lasts until you use it three times or you complete or abandon the quest. You can take a Magic action to release a wave of terror. Each creature of your choice within 30 feet of you must succeed on a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw or have the Frightened condition for 1 minute. While Frightened, a creature must spend its turns trying to move as far away from you as it can, and it can't make Opportunity Attacks. For its action, it can take only the Dash action or an action to try to escape from an effect that prevents it from moving. If it has nowhere it can move, the creature can take the Dodge action. At the end of each of its turns, a creature repeats the save, ending the effect on itself on a success.

Once you request this Charm, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with the Dark Talons increases.

##### Dark Talons: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Dark Talons*

You can call on a Black Dragonflight Whelp to assist you for a single adventure. If the dragon is killed, your Renown Score with the Dark Talons is reduced by 2d6. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with the Dark Talons increases.

#### Darkspear Tribe Renown Perks

The Darkspear tribe was the first group of trolls to join forces with the Horde. Trolls on the Echo Isles (off Durotar) are part of this faction, and the Zandalari Empire, on joining the Horde, aligned with the Darkspears to form a united troll faction. Chieftain Rokhan leads the Darkspear tribe, having succeeded Vol'jin, who died during the third invasion of the Burning Legion.

While your 10 is anywhere on the Echo Isles or Zandalar, your Renown Score with the Darkspear tribe increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with the Darkspear tribe gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Darkspear Tribe: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Darkspear Tribe*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Zandali or gain proficiency in Religion (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Darkspear Tribe: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Darkspear Tribe*

During a Short Rest, you can make offerings to the loa revered by trolls to gain a Charm. When you finish the rest, make a DC 15 Intelligence (Religion) check. On a successful check, you gain a Charms that allows you to gain the benefit of one of the following Potions as a Bonus Action (choose when you gain the Charm): Potion of Animal Friendship, Potion of Climbing, Potion of Comprehension, Potion of Fire Breath, Potion of Giant Strength (hill), Potion of Growth, Potion of Healing (greater), Potion of Mana (greater), Potion of Pugilism, Potion of Resistance (your choice of damage type), or Potion of Water Breathing.

The Charm vanishes from you after you use it, if you use this feature again, or if your Renown Score with the Darkspear tribe falls below 10. Once you receive a Charm in this way, you can't gain another until your Renown Score with the Darkspear tribe increases.

##### Darkspear Tribe: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Darkspear Tribe*

When you request a Charm from the loa, you can choose from the following additional Potion effects for your Charm: Potion of Clairvoyance, Potion of Diminution, Potion of Gaseous Form, Potion of Giant Strength (fire), Potion of Healing (superior), Potion of Mana (superior), Potion of Heroism, Potion of Invisibility, Potion of Invulnerability, or Potion of Mind Reading.

If you choose one of the options listed above, the Charm vanishes from you if your Renown Score with the Darkspear tribe falls below 25.

##### Darkspear Tribe: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Darkspear Tribe*

You can call on a shadow hunter (Cultist Hierophant) and six hexxers (Cultist Fanatics) to assist you for a single adventure. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with the Darkspear tribe increases.

#### Huojin Renown Perks

The Huojin faction, led by Ji Firepaw, represents those pandaren who side with the Horde.

Increasing your Renown Score with the Huojin gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Huojin: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Huojin*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn the Pandaren language or gain proficiency with Brewer's Supplies or Cook's Utensils (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Huojin: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Huojin*

You have Advantage on Initiative rolls as long as your Renown Score with the Huojin is 10+.

##### Huojin: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Huojin*

You receive the Blessing of Health (see the *Dungeon Master's Guide*). You lose the Blessing if your Renown Score with the Huojin falls below 25.

##### Huojin: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Huojin*

A group of Huojin monks establishes a Training Area special facility in your Bastion. This facility doesn't count toward the total number of special facilities in your Bastion. The monks depart and the facility becomes unusable if your Renown Score with the Huojin falls below 50.

#### Orgrimmar Renown Perks

Orgrimmar is the capital city of the orcs in Azeroth and is practically synonymous with the Horde. Most orcs across Azeroth consider themselves citizens of Orgrimmar, including the Mag'har orcs who recently joined the Horde. The orc shaman Thrall founded Orgrimmar and now leads the faction once again, after abdicating for a time in favor of Garrosh Hellscream and then Varok Saurfang.

While your 10 is in Orgrimmar or elsewhere in Durotar (except the Echo Isles), your Renown Score with Orgrimmar increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with Orgrimmar gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Orgrimmar: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with Orgrimmar*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Orcish, Orcish Sign Language, or Vulpera or to gain proficiency with Leatherworker's Tools (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Orgrimmar: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with Orgrimmar*

When you use leatherworking to craft an item (see x), the time required to craft it is halved.

##### Orgrimmar: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with Orgrimmar*

You can request a Blessings from the leaders of Orgrimmar before you undertake a quest on their behalf. You receive one of the Blessings described in the *Dungeon Master's Guide*, and it lasts until you complete or abandon the quest. Once you request a Blessing, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Orgrimmar increases.

##### Orgrimmar: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with Orgrimmar*

You can call on a warband leader (Berserker Commander), a shaman (Elemental Cultist), and three experienced warriors (Berserkers), all mounted on Dire Wolves, to assist you for a single adventure. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Orgrimmar increases.

#### Silvermoon City Renown Perks

As the capital of the kingdom of Quel'Thalas, Silvermoon City represents the entirety of the blood elf kingdom, as well as other blood elves who have aligned themselves with the Horde. Regent Lord Lor'themar Theron has ruled the kingdom since the fall of Prince Kael'thas Sunstrider, who made a traitorous pact with the Burning Legion. The nightborne elves of Suramar are also considered part of this faction.

While your 10 is in Silvermoon City, elsewhere in Quel'Thalas, or in Suramar, your Renown Score with Silvermoon City increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with Silvermoon City gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Silvermoon City: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with Silvermoon City*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Shalassian or Thalassian or to gain proficiency with Carpenter's Tools, Potter's Tools, or Woodcarver's Tools (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Silvermoon City: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with Silvermoon City*

When you use enchanting to craft an item (see x), the time required to craft it is halved.

##### Silvermoon City: Revered

###### Silvermoon City Charm

*Type:* Charm

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with Silvermoon City*

You can request a Charms from the leaders of Silvermoon City before you undertake a quest on their behalf. You receive a Charm that duplicates the effect of one of the following Wands: Wand of Binding, Wand of Enemy Detection, Wand of Fear, Wand of Fireballs, Wand of Lightning Bolts, Wand of Magic Missiles, Wand of Paralysis, Wand of Polymorph, or Wand of Web. The Charm doesn't regain charges. It vanishes from you if you spend all its charges, if you complete or abandon the quest, or if your Renown Score with Silvermoon City falls below 25. Once you request a Charm, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Silvermoon City increases.

##### Silvermoon City: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with Silvermoon City*

You can call on a single Magister (Archmage) or four Mages to assist you for a single adventure. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Silvermoon City increases.

#### Thunder Bluff Renown Perks

*[Image: image (book/WoW/070-04-007.thunder-bluff.webp)]*

The Thunder Bluff faction represents those united tauren tribes who have aligned themselves with the Horde, including the Highmountain tauren of the Broken Isles and some taunka of Northrend. The leader of the faction is High Chieftain Baine Bloodhoof, who succeeded his father, Cairne Bloodhoof, when Cairne died in an ill-fated duel against Garrosh Hellscream.

While your 10 is in Thunder Bluff or elsewhere in Mulgore or Highmountain, your Renown Score with Thunder Bluff increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with Thunder Bluff gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Thunder Bluff: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with Thunder Bluff*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Taur-ahe or gain proficiency with the Herbalism Kit (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Thunder Bluff: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with Thunder Bluff*

When you succeed on a check to gather herbs using herbalism (see x), you gain one extra resource, and you find a rare resource if you beat the check DC by 4 or more.

##### Thunder Bluff: Revered

###### Charm of An'she

*Type:* Charm

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with Thunder Bluff*

You can request the Charm of An'she from the leaders of Thunder Bluff before you undertake a quest on their behalf. This Charms allows you to cast Summon Beast (level 3 version). Once used three times, the Charm vanishes from you; it also vanishes if you complete or abandon the quest. Once you request a Charm, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Thunder Bluff increases.

##### Thunder Bluff: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with Thunder Bluff*

You can call on a tauren shaman (Elemental Cultist) and eight assistant shaman (Berserkers) to assist you for a single adventure. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Thunder Bluff increases.

#### Undercity Renown Perks

The Undercity faction encompasses the Forsaken aligned with the Horde. The faction was long led by Sylvanas Windrunner, who originally led the Forsaken who broke free from the control of the Lich King. When Sylvanas stepped into the leadership of the Horde as warchief, the Desolate Council was formed to govern the Forsaken in her place. The council, based now in the Ruins of Lordaeron, is made up of five leaders: Calia Menethil, Lilian Voss, Master Apothecary Faranell, Deathstalker Commander Belmont, and Dark Ranger Velonara.

While your 10 is in Undercity or the Ruins of Lordaeron, your Renown Score with Undercity increases by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with Undercity gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Undercity: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with Undercity*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn the Forsaken language or gain proficiency with Alchemist's Supplies (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Undercity: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with Undercity*

When you use alchemy to craft an item (see x), the time required to craft it is halved.

##### Undercity: Revered

###### Charm of the Banshee

*Type:* Charm

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with Undercity*

You can request the Charm of the Banshee from the leaders of Undercity before you undertake a quest on their behalf. This Charms allows you to cast Summon Undead. Once used three times, the Charm vanishes from you; it also vanishes if you complete or abandon the quest. Once you request a Charm, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Undercity increases.

##### Undercity: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with Undercity*

You can call on two Forsaken necromancers (Ghast Gravecallers) and five unliving soldiers (Wights) to assist you for a single adventure. Once you request this assistance, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with Undercity increases.

### Argent Dawn

The knightly Order of the Argent Dawn is dedicated to protecting Azeroth from the Burning Legion, the Scourge, and other forces that seek to destroy the world. Neutral in the continual conflict between the Alliance and the Horde (and generally Neutral Good in alignment), the Argent Dawn calls both factions allies in its higher purpose of resisting these forces of evil. The order's headquarters is Light's Hope Chapel, in the Eastern Plaguelands—a beacon of hope and comfort in a barren and twisted land—and the knights operate across the Plaguelands and anywhere evil rears its head.

In addition to pursuing quests assigned to them by the Argent Dawn, characters can increase their Renown Score by killing powerful Fiends and Undead and bringing evidence of the monsters' destruction to Argent Dawn agents.

#### Argent Dawn Renown Perks

Increasing your Renown Score with the Argent Dawn gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Argent Dawn: Friendly

###### Blessing of Argent Will

*Type:* Blessing

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Argent Dawn*

You receive the Blessing of Argent Will. This Blessings gives you Advantage on saving throws to avoid or end the Charmed condition caused by a Fiend or Undead. You lose the Blessing if your Renown Score with the Argent Dawn falls below 3.

##### Argent Dawn: Honored

###### Argent Dawn Charm

*Type:* Charm

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Argent Dawn*

If you spend a Long Rest in Light's Hope Chapel or another Argent Dawn sanctuary, you gain a Charms that lasts for 7 days or until you use it. The Charm allows you to cast Healing Word once.

##### Argent Dawn: Revered

###### Blessing of Argent Fortitude

*Type:* Blessing

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Argent Dawn*

You receive the Blessing of Argent Fortitude. This Blessing gives you Immunity to the Poisoned condition and Poison damage. You lose the Blessing if your Renown Score with the Argent Dawn falls below 25.

##### Argent Dawn: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Argent Dawn*

For a cost of 15,000 GP, the Argent Dawn will add a magical property to any Rare magic item you are attuned to. While attuned to the item, you have Resistance to one of the following damage types (chosen when the property is added): Acid, Cold, Fire, Lightning, Necrotic, Poison, Psychic, Radiant, or Thunder. The item's rarity then increases to Very Rare.

### Cenarion Circle

*[Image: Malfurion Stormrage, the First Druid (book/WoW/071-04-008.malfurion-stormrage.webp)]*

Named for Cenarius—the demigod who trained Malfurion Stormrage, the first druid—the Cenarion Circle is a worldwide order keeping watch over Azeroth's druids and protecting the natural balance. Since the end of the Third War, the circle has accepted druids from both the Alliance and the Horde, reflecting a generally Neutral alignment. In the druidic sanctuary of Moonglade, night elf and tauren druids live and work in harmony, and in recent years, druids from other peoples—Kul Tiran humans, worgen, trolls, and even haranir—have joined the ranks of the Cenarion Circle. The order is still led by Cenarius himself, with the involvement of Malfurion and the tauren druid Hamuul Runetotem.

The Cenarion Circle is most active in Kalimdor. Besides Moonglade, which is inaccessible to anyone but circle members, the order also has a base in Desolace (Karnum's Glade). It has launched expeditions into Silithus, the Plaguelands, and Outland, and its members are on the forefront of any effort to preserve, protect, or restore nature.

Adopting the Druid class, either at character creation or through multiclassing, increases a character's Renown Score with the Cenarion Circle by 3.

> > There's no time like the present to start saving the future!
> > — Chromie
> 

#### Cenarion Circle Renown Perks

Increasing your Renown Score with the Cenarion Circle gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Cenarion Circle: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Cenarion Circle*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Druidic or gain proficiency in Nature (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Cenarion Circle: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Cenarion Circle*

If you spend a Long Rest in a Cenarion Circle sanctuary, you gain the Charm of Restoration (see the *Dungeon Master's Guide*).

##### Cenarion Circle: Revered

###### Wildguard Blessing

*Type:* Blessing

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Cenarion Circle*

You receive the Wildguard Blessing. This Blessings gives you Advantage on saving throws against spells. You lose the Blessing if your Renown Score with the Cenarion Circle falls below 25.

##### Cenarion Circle: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Cenarion Circle*

You carry the title Guardian of Cenarius. For a cost of 2,000 GP, you can purchase a Cenarion war hippogryph mount (Hippogriff). If your Renown Score falls below 50, you lose the title, and your mount no longer obeys your commands.

### Earthen Ring

The Earthen Ring is a gathering of Azeroth's most powerful shaman, working together to preserve the balance among the elements. The members of the Earthen Ring oppose destructive forces such as the Twilight's Hammer cult, the rampaging black dragon Deathwing, and the Burning Legion, and they work tirelessly to heal the wounds inflicted on Azeroth by such forces. Insisting on a neutral stance in mortal conflicts (and a generally Neutral alignment), the Earthen Ring welcomes members from both the Alliance and the Horde, though the peoples of the Alliance—particularly draenei, Wildhammer dwarves, and humans of Kul Tiras—are recent additions to the organization. It also includes murlocs, the Krokul draenei, and other druids who haven't chosen a side in the Alliance–Horde conflict.

The Earthen Ring is governed by the Elder Council, which gathers at a shrine perched at the edge of the Maelstrom called the Heart of Azeroth. The order also maintains a ritual circle just outside Stormwind's walls (the Eastern Earthshrine) and another above Orgrimmar (the Western Earthshrine). Both circles include portals leading to the Elemental Plane and other areas of Earthen Ring activity, which Earthen Ring shaman allow only those who are at least Honored with the faction to use.

Adopting the Barbarian class (as a shaman; see chapter 1), either at character creation or through multiclassing, increases a character's Renown Score with the Earthen Ring by 3.

#### Earthen Ring Renown Perks

Increasing your Renown Score with the Earthen Ring gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Earthen Ring: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Earthen Ring*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn the Kalimag language (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter). Elementals that haven't been corrupted by the Old Gods are Friendly to you by default.

##### Earthen Ring: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Earthen Ring*

You can freely use the portals located in the Eastern and Western Earthshrine.

Additionally, if you spend a Long Rest in an Earthen Ring sanctuary, you gain the Charm of the Earthen Ring, which allows you to cast Summon Elemental. The Charms vanishes from you once used three times, after 7 days, or if your Renown Score with the Earthen Ring falls below 10.

##### Earthen Ring: Revered

###### Blessing of the Earthen Ring

*Type:* Blessing

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Earthen Ring*

You receive the Blessing of the Earthen Ring. The Blessings enables you to take a Magic action to summon an Air Elemental, an Earth Elemental, a Fire Elemental, or a Water Elemental. The elemental appears in an unoccupied space you choose within 30 feet of yourself, obeys your commands, and takes its turn immediately after you on your Initiative count. The elemental disappears after 1 hour, when it dies, or when you dismiss it as a Bonus Action. You can't use this Blessing again until the next dawn. You lose the Blessing if your Renown Score with the Earthen Ring falls below 25.

##### Earthen Ring: Exalted

###### Blessing of the Farseer

*Type:* Blessing

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Earthen Ring*

You receive the Blessing of the Farseer. The Blessing gives you the benefits of a Ring of Elemental Command linked to an Elemental Plane of your choice. When you finish a Long Rest, you can change the plane it's linked to. You lose the Blessing if your Renown Score with the Earthen Ring falls below 50.

### Explorers' League and Reliquary

Affiliated with the Alliance and the Horde respectively, the Explorers' League and the Reliquary are scholarly organizations dedicated to the practice of archaeology and the study of history, seeking to understand the origins of life and culture on Azeroth.

Magni Bronzebeard, the king of Ironforge, founded the Explorers' League by decree after dwarf-led expeditions uncovered surprising facts about the dwarves' connection to the ancient titans. Declaring that learning this history was more important than mining and engineering, Magni directed the Explorers' League to focus on archaeological work and plumbing the secrets of the ancient world. The league quickly expanded its scope to encompass natural history as well as the history of all Azeroth's peoples, and it soon accepted citizens of other Alliance nations as members. High Explorer Muninn Magellas is the leader of the Explorers' League, and the organization's headquarters remains in Ironforge, in the Hall of Explorers.

The Reliquary, in contrast, began as an effort to harness the power of ancient relics for the benefit of the blood elves and their allies in the Horde. The leader and founder of the Reliquary, Tae'thelan Bloodwatcher, believed such artifacts should be carefully studied, not feared. Tae'thelan's ultimate goal in founding the Reliquary was to free the blood elves from their magical addiction and reunite them. When the blood elves joined the Horde, the Reliquary opened its membership to all members of the Horde, but its headquarters (also called the Reliquary) remains in Silvermoon City.

#### Relic Rivals

Despite their different origins (and different alignments, corresponding to the difference between the Alliance and the Horde), the Explorers' League and the Reliquary use similar methods and often vie for access to the same sites. For most of recent history, the organizations were bitter rivals, with the tension between them often erupting into violent conflict. During this time, the factions are mutually hostile (see the "x" sidebar).

However, with the reemergence of the Dragon Isles and the subsequent discovery of Khaz Algar, the two organizations have set aside their old rivalry and formed joint expeditions. During this period, a character's Renown Score with either faction applies to both factions, so having a Renown Score of 3+ with either one makes members of both factions Friendly by default.

#### Explorers' League/Reliquary Renown Perks

Increasing your Renown Score with either the Explorers' League or the Reliquary gives you the following additional benefits. Characters can't increase their Renown Score with both factions; typically, characters affiliated with the Alliance work with the Explorers' League and Horde characters work with the Reliquary.

##### Explorers' League/Reliquary: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Faction*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn any language from the x or gain proficiency in History (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Explorers' League/Reliquary: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Faction*

When you gather archaeology fragments from a dig site (see x), you gain one additional fragment on a successful check, and you have Advantage on Intelligence (History) checks to assemble fragments into a complete relic.

##### Explorers' League/Reliquary: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Faction*

If you spend 7 days studying the relics in the Hall of Explorers in Ironforge or the Reliquary in Silvermoon City (or some other significant Explorers' League or Reliquary facility, at the DM's discretion) and then finish a Long Rest there, you gain a Charms that lasts for 7 days or until you use it. You can't gain another Charm from this feature while you still have one. The effect of the Charm depends on the origin of the relics you study; you can choose one of the following options:

- **Charm of Draenor.** Charm of Draenor While you have this Charm, you know Draenei and Orcish. You can expend the Charm to cast Conjure Minor Elementals.
- **Charm of Pandaria.** Charm of Pandaria While you have this Charm, you know the Pandaren language. You can expend the Charm to cast Gaseous Form, targeting yourself. The effect lasts for 1 hour or until you end the effect as a Bonus Action.
- **Charm of the Burning Legion.** Charm of the Burning Legion While you have this Charm, you know the Eredun language. You can expend the Charm to cast Rain of Fire (level 4 version).
- **Charm of the Highborne.** Charm of the Highborne While you have this Charm, you know Thalassian. You can expend the Charm to cast Charm Monster.
- **Charm of the Old Gods.** Charm of the Old Gods While you have this Charm, you know Shath'Yar. You can expend the Charm to cast Summon Aberration.
- **Charm of the Titan-Forged.** Charm of the Titan-Forged While you have this Charm, you know Titan. You can expend the Charm to gain Resistance to all damage for 1 minute as your flesh takes on a mineral appearance.
- **Charm of the Zandalari.** Charm of the Zandalari While you have this Charm, you know Zandali. You can expend the Charm to cast Summon Beast (level 4 version). The summoned Bestial Spirit resembles a loa.

##### Explorers' League/Reliquary: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Faction*

You can request the use of a powerful magic item stored in your organization's vaults to assist you for a single adventure that is relevant to your organization's interests and aims. It takes 7 days for top agents in your organization to retrieve the item, which can be any Legendary magic item of your choice (subject to the DM's discretion). If the item isn't consumable, you must return it at the conclusion of the adventure, or your Renown Score decreases by 2d12. Once you use this benefit, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with your organization increases.

> **Mutually Hostile Factions**
> *World of Warcraft* features many opposing factions that are so hostile to each other that any renown gain with one faction automatically results in a loss of renown with the other. The Explorers' League and the Reliquary were mutually hostile before they were forced to cooperate in exploring the Dragon Isles. Other examples include the Scryers and the Aldor of Shattrath City, as well as the Frenzyheart Tribe of wolvar and the gorlock Oracles of Sholazar Basin. In situations like these, a character's Renown Score with a faction can drop below 0, which means members of that faction are Hostile to the character by default.

### Kirin Tor

*[Image: Archmage Khadgar of the Kirin Tor (book/WoW/072-04-009.archmage-khadgar.webp)]*

The Kirin Tor began as a secretive mages' guild within the Empire of Arathor, the first great human civilization of the Eastern Kingdoms. Viewed with suspicion by much of the populace, the mages of Arathor secluded themselves in their own city, Dalaran, which became a beacon to mages from across Azeroth. With the fall of Arathor, Dalaran became a nation in its own right, one of Arathor's seven successor kingdoms, and the Council of Six who led the Kirin Tor became the rulers of this city-state. Razed during the Third War, Dalaran was rebuilt behind powerful warding spells and later transformed into a flying island that could be relocated to safety when needed. From its original site in the Hillsbrad Foothills, Dalaran moved first to Northrend's Crystalsong Forest, then above Karazhan in Deadwind Pass, then to the Broken Isles, then above the Isle of Dorn in Khaz Algar. There it met its doom at the hands of Xal'atath's forces of the Void, but the survivors of the Kirin Tor remain in Dornogal, coordinating the magical defense of Azeroth.

The Council of Six, the leadership of the Kirin Tor, is made up of the most senior members of the Kirin Tor. Its leaders have included Archmage Rhonin, who oversaw the rebuilding of Dalaran after the Third War, Lady Jaina Proudmoore, and Archmage Khadgar, who was imprisoned by Xal'atath just before Dalaran's destruction. The Kirin Tor is generally Lawful Neutral in alignment.

Adopting the Mage class, either at character creation or through multiclassing, increases a character's Renown Score with the Kirin Tor by 3.

#### Kirin Tor Renown Perks

Establishing your 10 in Dalaran increases your Renown Score with the Kirin Tor by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with the Kirin Tor gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Kirin Tor: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Kirin Tor*

You can enjoy a Lifestyle Expenses lifestyle in Dalaran at no cost. You can also request a favor from the archmages of the Kirin Tor, which can take one of the following forms:

- An archmage casts for you a spell of level 3 or lower at half the usual cost.
- An archmage provides you with a Common or Uncommon magic item from the Arcana Tables in the *Dungeon Master's Guide* at half the usual cost.
- An archmage spends 30 days teaching you, so you learn a language from the x or gain proficiency with a tool (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

You can request such a favor a number of times equal to your Renown Score with the Kirin Tor. If your Renown Score falls below the number of favors you have requested, a higher-ranking member of the Kirin Tor might call in a favor from you, sending you on a mission for the organization.

##### Kirin Tor: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Kirin Tor*

You can enjoy a Lifestyle Expenses lifestyle in Dalaran at no cost.

If your Bastion is in Dalaran, when you issue the Maintain order to your Bastion, the DM rolls twice on the Bastion Events table and you can pick which event occurs.

In addition, when you request a favor from the Kirin Tor (see "x"), it can take one of the following additional forms:

- An archmage casts for you a spell of level 4 or lower at half the usual cost.
- An archmage provides you with a Rare magic item from the Arcana Tables in the *Dungeon Master's Guide* at half the usual cost.
- Whenever you take a Bastion turn, you can issue the Research order to the Kirin Tor as if it were a Library facility in your Bastion.

##### Kirin Tor: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Kirin Tor*

You can enjoy a Lifestyle Expenses lifestyle in Dalaran at no cost.

In addition, when you request a favor from the Kirin Tor (see "x"), it can take one of the following additional forms:

- An archmage casts for you a spell of level 6 or lower at half the usual cost.
- An archmage provides you with a Very Rare magic item from the Arcana Tables in the *Dungeon Master's Guide* at half the usual cost.
- Whenever you take a Bastion turn, you can issue the Craft order to the Kirin Tor as if it were a Scriptorium facility in your Bastion.

##### Kirin Tor: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Kirin Tor*

You can enjoy an Lifestyle Expenses lifestyle in Dalaran at no cost.

In addition, when you request a favor from the Kirin Tor (see "x"), it can take one of the following additional forms:

- An archmage casts for you a spell of level 8 or lower at half the usual cost.
- An archmage provides you with a Legendary magic item from the Arcana Tables in the *Dungeon Master's Guide* at half the usual cost.
- When you craft a magic item, two Kirin Tor apprentices assist you, reducing the time required to craft the item accordingly.

### Steamwheedle Cartel

The Steamwheedle Cartel is one of the largest and richest goblin cartels, having maintained a careful neutrality in the conflict between the Alliance and the Horde since the Second War (and a generally Neutral alignment). From its headquarters in Gadgetzan (in Tanaris), the cartel oversees operations across Azeroth and even Outland. It also runs Booty Bay (in Stranglethorn), Ratchet (in the Northern Barrens), and Everlook (in Winterspring), as well as smaller outposts. The cartel's hands are in virtually every line of work, with a specialty in premium-grade alchemy (at least since the alchemist Marin Noggenfogger assumed the position of trade prince). Besides potions, the cartel also deals in fuels, poisons, and industrial chemicals—what some goblins describe as "liquid assets."

#### Steamwheedle Cartel Renown Perks

Establishing your 10 in Gadgetzan or another Steamwheedle Cartel territory increases your Renown Score with the Steamwheedle Cartel by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with the Steamwheedle Cartel gives you the following additional benefits. (Steamwheedle Cartel merchants appear in many locations across the world, making their discounts particularly notable.)

##### Steamwheedle Cartel: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Steamwheedle Cartel*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn the Goblin language or gain proficiency with Alchemist's Supplies or Tinker's Tools (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Steamwheedle Cartel: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Steamwheedle Cartel*

You can request a supply of 1d4 flasks of Noggenfogger Elixir from the leaders of the cartel before you undertake a quest on their behalf. Once you request these elixirs, you can't do so again until your Renown Score with the cartel increases.

##### Steamwheedle Cartel: Revered

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Steamwheedle Cartel*

You can purchase an Experimental Goblin Jetpack from the Steamwheedle Cartel for 2,000 GP (with no further discount).

##### Steamwheedle Cartel: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Steamwheedle Cartel*

You can purchase a Steamwheedle Apparatus (which functions as an Apparatus of Kwalish) from the Steamwheedle Cartel for 100,000 GP (with no further discount).

### Valdrakken Accord

The Valdrakken Accord is the unified might of all dragonkind, seeking to protect not only the Dragon Isles but all of Azeroth according to the ancient charge given to the five Dragon Aspects (see x). The accord has also expanded its mission to include dragons beyond the five flights—in particular those who follow Vyranoth, who has been empowered as the Aspect of Storms.

The mission of the Dragon Aspects means that what is good for dragonkind is generally beneficial for all of Azeroth. The dragons oppose the Burning Legion, the lingering forces of the Old Gods and elementals they have corrupted, the remnants of the Scourge, the powers of the Void, and any other force that might threaten Azeroth and its inhabitants. That said, working with the dragons means viewing the world's conflicts from their perspective (which is generally Neutral) and solving problems their way.

The leaders of the Valdrakken Accord are the six Dragon Aspects: Alexstrasza, Nozdormu, Kalecgos, Merithra, Ebyssian, and Vyranoth. Their base of operations is the city of Valdrakken in the Dragon Isles.

Before the Dragon Isles reappeared, the Wyrmrest Accord carried out a similar mission but without the black dragonflight.

#### Valdrakken Accord Renown Perks

Establishing your 10 in Valdrakken or elsewhere in the Dragon Isles increases your Renown Score with the Valdrakken Accord by 1.

Increasing your Renown Score with the Valdrakken Accord gives you the following additional benefits.

##### Valdrakken Accord: Friendly

*Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with the Valdrakken Accord*

You can spend 30 days with a trainer to learn Draconic (see the "x" sidebar earlier in this chapter).

##### Valdrakken Accord: Honored

*Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with the Valdrakken Accord*

You receive the Blessing of the Proto-Whelp. This Blessings allows you to cast Find Familiar with no Material components. When you cast the spell in this way, your familiar is a crimson proto-whelp (Pseudodragon). Once you cast the spell in this way, you can't do so again until you finish a Long Rest. You lose the Blessing and your proto-whelp familiar vanishes if your Renown Score with the Valdrakken Accord falls below 10.

Also, if you establish your Bastion in the Dragon Isles, it includes a Garden special facility. This facility doesn't count toward the total number of special facilities in your Bastion. The hireling departs and the facility becomes unusable if your Renown Score with Valdrakken Accord falls below 10.

##### Valdrakken Accord: Revered

###### Blessing of the Wylderdrake

*Type:* Blessing

*Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with the Valdrakken Accord*

You receive the Blessing of the Wylderdrake. This Blessings allows you to cast Find Steed (level 4 version). The Otherworldly Steed summoned by the spell is a cliffside wylderdrake, which has the Dragon type, deals Force damage with its Otherworldly Slam, and has the following trait instead of the Bonus Action options shown in the Player's Handbook:

###### Flyby

The steed doesn't provoke an Opportunity Attack when it flies out of an enemy's reach.

##### Valdrakken Accord: Exalted

*Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with the Valdrakken Accord*

The Valdrakken Accord grants you the title Ally of Dragons. Dragons are Friendly to you by default unless they are corrupted. You can petition one of the Dragon Aspects for a favor, which takes the form of a Wish spell. Once you are granted such a favor, you can't ask for another one, even if your Renown Score falls below 50 and later reaches 50 again.

## Chapter 5: The World of Warcraft

*[Image: Illidan Stormrage schemes before the Dark Portal. (book/WoW/073-05-001.illidan-stormrage-schemes-splash.webp)]*

The planet called Azeroth floats through the Material Plane—what its scholars call the Great Dark Beyond. This plane of existence, the physical universe, is an infinite living realm composed of innumerable stars, worlds, and mortal civilizations. Some worlds, like Azeroth, have souls, and the titans believe that these Worldsouls, properly nourished, will eventually develop into more titans.

The Twisting Nether is how sages of Azeroth refer to the Wildspace of the Astral Plane: a parallel dimension to the Great Dark Beyond, defined by perpetual strife between the cosmic forces of Light and Shadow. At times, the volatile magic that pervades the Twisting Nether intrudes on the physical universe, warping reality beyond measure.

### The Cosmic Forces

Beyond the Great Dark Beyond and the Twisting Nether, various cosmic forces inhabit their own extradimensional realms. The conflicts among these forces shape the nature of reality on the Material Plane. These forces include both elemental substance and spiritual energy—comparable to the influence of the The Planes described in the *Dungeon Master's Guide*—and they are best understood as pairs or groups of oppositional forces.

#### Light and Shadow

Light and Shadow are fundamental forces. Although contradictory by nature, they are bound together on a cosmic scale; neither can exist without the other. Pure Light and Shadow dwell in realms outside the borders of reality, but manifestations of their presence are found in the physical universe. Light manifests as holy magic, while Shadow (also referred to as "the Void") appears as shadow magic.

##### Naaru

The naaru are benevolent creatures of living holy energy. They are perhaps the purest expression of the Light that exists in the Great Dark Beyond. The naaru have vowed to bring peace and hope to all mortal civilizations and waylay the forces of the Void that seek to engulf creation.

##### Church of the Holy Light

In the wake of a devastating war against the trolls of the Amani Empire millennia ago, a group of humans received visions of strange angelic beings who introduced them to the Light. These beings were the naaru, and these encounters led to the foundation of the religion that commands the loyalty of most humans on Azeroth: the Church of the Holy Light. This faith was codified in a series of holy texts, teaching the three virtues of respect, tenacity, and compassion, based on the idea that the Light dwells in all living things.

Since its founding, the faith of the Holy Light has spread to the high elves of Quel'Thalas and the dwarves of Khaz Modan.

##### Void Lords

The void lords are monstrous entities composed of pure Shadow energy. These beings are cruel and merciless beyond mortal comprehension. Driven by an insatiable hunger, the void lords seek to devour all matter and energy in the physical universe.

In their natural state, the void lords exist outside reality. Only the most powerful of these entities can manifest in the physical universe, and only for limited amounts of time. To maintain their presence in reality, the void lords must consume untold amounts of matter and energy.

###### Old Gods

The Old Gods are physical manifestations of the Void. They are nightmares incarnate: mountains of blighted flesh and writhing tentacles that grow like cancers within the worlds of the Great Dark Beyond. These malignant entities serve the void lords by transforming the worlds they infest into places of despair and death. The Old Gods are detailed in x.

###### Twilight's Hammer

Twilight's Hammer is a cult that reveres the Old Gods and seeks to bring about the end of the world. It has been active since before the First War, but it grew to particular prominence amid the cataclysmic events surrounding the return of Deathwing.

#### Life and Death

The forces of Life and Death hold sway over every living thing in the physical universe. The energies of Life, known commonly as nature magic, promote growth and renewal. Death, in the form of necromantic magic, breeds despair in mortal hearts and pushes everything toward a state of entropic decay and eventual oblivion.

##### Elune

Elune, called the Mother Moon and the Night Warrior, is the primary deity of the night elves of Kalimdor. Tauren also revere her as Mu'sha, the left eye of the Earth Mother. Mu'sha's twin, An'she, is the right eye (the sun), a force of Light.

##### Wild Gods

Wild Gods are primal manifestations of Life and nature. They are creatures of two realms, inhabiting the physical world of Azeroth while their spirits are bound to the ethereal Emerald Dream. Many Wild Gods appear in the form of enormous animals, such as wolves, bears, tigers, or birds.

##### Eternal Ones

The Eternal Ones, who rule the afterlife realms of the Shadowlands, are incarnations of the cosmic force of Death, and they're sometimes known as the Pantheon of Death (in contrast to the titans of the Pantheon of Order, described below).

#### Order and Disorder

The forces of Order and Disorder govern the cosmic systems of the universe. Order is most commonly perceived as arcane magic. This type of energy is intensely volatile, and wielding it requires precision and concentration.

Disorder manifests as highly destructive fel magic. This brutal and addictive energy is fueled by drawing life from living beings. The Burning Legion (described in x) is the ultimate expression of Disorder in the physical world.

##### The Pantheon

Titans are colossal, godlike beings of Order, composed of the primordial matter from which the universe was born.

A group of titans called the Pantheon (or the Pantheon of Order) roam the cosmos, imbued with the raw power of creation. The titans use this incredible force to find and awaken others of their kind who still slumber, in the form of Worldsouls, among the many worlds of the Great Dark. These titans created the keepers, lesser but still godlike beings who shaped and ordered Azeroth, created the other titan-forged, and empowered the Dragon Aspects.

The original pantheon included seven titans, all but one of whom imbued one or more keepers with their likeness and power:

**Aman'Thul**, Highfather of the Pantheon, has mastery over time magic. His keepers are Odyn and Ra.

**Eonar** the Life-Binder is the protector of life, nature, and health. Her keeper is Freya.

**Golganneth** the Thunderer is the lord of the skies and oceans. His keepers are Hodir and Thorim.

**Khaz'goroth**, the Shaper and Forger of Worlds, is the ultimate artisan and guardian of the deep places of the world. His keeper is Archaedas.

**Norgannon**, known as Keeper of Celestial Magic and Lore, is the master of all magic, knowledge, secrets, and mysteries. His keepers are Loken and Mimiron.

**Sargeras**, known first as the Defender and then as the Destroyer, was the champion of the Pantheon until he decided that the only way to prevent the void lords from corrupting a Worldsoul and making titans of their own was to destroy the cosmos and start creation over again. To that end, he created the Burning Legion (see x).

**Aggramar**, the Avenger, was the lieutenant of Sargeras and inherited his role as champion of the Pantheon. He is charged with combating the Burning Legion. His keeper is Tyr.

#### The Elements

The elements of air, earth, fire, and water serve as the basic building blocks of all matter in the universe. Shaman call on the primordial forces of Spirit and Decay to attain power over the elements. Those who seek to bring balance to the elements rely on Spirit (sometimes referred to as the "fifth element" by shaman). This life-giving force interconnects and binds all things in existence as one. Decay is the tool of shaman seeking to subjugate and weaponize the elements.

##### Elemental Spirits

Elemental spirits are wild and chaotic beings of air, earth, fire, or water. They were among the first sentient creatures to inhabit the nascent worlds of the waking universe. They appear in an almost infinite variety of shapes and sizes, with personalities and temperaments strongly influenced by the spirit's elemental nature. The mightiest of these elemental spirits are the elemental lords, described in more detail in x.

*[Image: World Map of Azeroth (book/WoW/074-05-009.azeroth.webp)]*

*[Image: Player Version (book/WoW/075-05-009.azeroth-player.webp)]*

### World of Azeroth

Azeroth is a realm not merely shaped by war, but defined by it. No region, no nation, arguably not one contiguous square mile has escaped it. Wars have raged through all the ages.

When modern citizens of Azeroth contemplate war, most think first of the long-running conflict between the Alliance and the Horde. This struggle is the cause of three of the great wars of modern history. Yet the Alliance–Horde struggle is but one of many conflicts: tribe against tribe, nation against nation, mortals against demons, mantid versus pandaren, naga incursions against land dwellers, the forces of nature against corruptions of the x.

This dreadful cycle has raged at least ten millennia, since the Highborne night elves drew the cosmic eye of Sargeras the Destroyer because of their exorbitant use of the Well of Eternity. The corrupted titan gathered countless demons he had imprisoned in Mardum, the Plane of Banishment, and unleashed them on Azeroth as the x.

This War of the Ancients wreaked untold destruction and led to the devastation of the Great Sundering. Continents shattered and land sank into the oceans, reshaping coastlines. The Well of Eternity was destroyed and became the Great Sea's all-consuming Maelstrom.

In those grim days, extinction loomed, and the seeds of most of Azeroth's subsequent rivalries and hatreds were planted.

The world has been at war ever since.

Yet Azeroth is more than just a giant battlefield. In villages, cities, and towns, people work, worship, celebrate, and love. Natural wonders from vast forests to lush grasslands stretch between mountain and river, hill and sea.

Life thrives and civilization endures. Secret magic and lost technologies wait to be rediscovered in the deepest dungeons—marvels that, depending on who finds them first, could improve lives or end them en masse. Villains and monsters, many unrelated to the ongoing wars, threaten the innocent.

Azeroth is a world that might never lose its need for soldiers, but more than that, it needs heroes.

#### Alliance and Horde

An armistice has held the Alliance and the Horde in a semblance of peace since the end of the Fourth War. Yet their conflict continues through scattered skirmishes on a hundred fronts—even in regions that largely stayed outside this terrible struggle.

Some areas are under the control of one faction or the other, and members of the opposing faction are unwelcome. Most areas are contested—both factions have a presence, and open conflict sometimes erupts there. A few neutral places welcome members of both factions and frown on conflict. The druidic territory of Moonglade (in Kalimdor) is a prime example of a neutral territory; druids set aside their factional differences to learn and commune with nature together in this tranquil valley.

#### Adventures in Azeroth

The many regions of Azeroth contain ample opportunities for adventure. Some locations merit special attention due to their historic, cultural, or mystical significance.

Several of the regions covered in this chapter list such locations. A few are detailed substantially in chapter 6 and are referenced here only briefly. The summaries of other sites offer an understanding of what the dungeon or adventure site is and why it's important. DMs can use these descriptions as springboards for expansion and development.

*[Image: Orgrimmar is the capital of Durotar and a safe haven for the Horde. (book/WoW/076-05-002.orgrimmar.webp)]*

#### Zone Progression

In early versions of *World of Warcraft*, characters often followed a predictable path through the world as they advanced in level, entering more dangerous regions as their power increased. While that strict progression is no longer in place, some parts of the world are still more dangerous than others—and regions on other worlds and planes are more dangerous still. Thus, each region's description in this chapter includes a parenthetical notation of the tier of play (1–4, 5–10, 11–16, 17–20, or endgame) during which it's most appropriate for characters to adventure in that region. DMs can use this information along with the conflict arcs in 6 to decide where in the vast world of Azeroth their adventures will unfold—or DMs can ignore it entirely and let the players decide where their characters will roam.

#### Kalimdor

Kalimdor is the broken western remnant of the much larger continent of the same name that was torn apart during the Great Sundering. It still bears the scars of that ancient catastrophe and the more recent Cataclysm wrought by Deathwing.

The northern forests and mountains of Kalimdor bear the imprint of thousands of years of night elf civilization. Night elves built majestic cities before the War of the Ancients, and the ruins of cities such as Mathystra, Ameth'Aran, Ordil'Aran, and Kel'Theril still remain.

Night elves zealously defend the portions of Northern Kalimdor they still hold, as this is the heart of their former empire and the home of the World Trees. The night elves joined the Alliance to face the Horde, but a significant minority consider both factions equally distasteful.

They maintain that their home territories belong to the night elves alone; humans, x, and draenei are no more welcome than orcs, trolls, or goblins. Some of these night elves keep to themselves, but others wage a guerrilla insurgency against all other groups, adding more uncertainty to areas torn by struggles between the Alliance and the Horde.

Southern Kalimdor is a blasted and broken land that bears the scars of the Cataclysm, the failed experiments of titans, the castoffs of the Old Gods, and the ruins of empires older than the night elf civilization to the north. From flooded chasms to baking deserts, it is a place of misery, want, and ancient death, with only a few stretches of verdant forest or savanna in which life can thrive.

A defining feature of Southern Kalimdor is the remnant of the ancient qiraji empire. Fallen spires, broken towers, and sand-buried avenues are testaments to an empire formed by the the insectile qiraji, who were corrupted by the influence of the imprisoned Old God C'Thun. By accessing the prison-laboratory of the titans, the qiraji learned secrets now buried with the rest of their nation.

*[Image: Devilsaurs are among the most dangerous predators of Un'Goro Crater. (book/WoW/077-05-003.devilsaur.webp)]*

##### Kalimdor Regions

Kalimdor stretches from the charred remains of Teldrassil in the northwest, across the Great Divide that cuts across the Barrens, and down to the southern tip of Uldum.

###### Ahn'Qiraj

Ahn'Qiraj, the fallen kingdom of the insectile qiraji, is a wasted ruin older than most civilizations, still containing ancient horrors linked to the Old Gods. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Ashenvale

Tensions between night elves in the north and west and Horde loggers in the south and east have long defined the lush forest of Ashenvale. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Azshara

Azshara is a region cloaked in eternal autumn, stalked by naga, and haunted by ghosts. It has been partially pacified by Bilgewater Cartel goblins. (Horde; 5–10)

###### Azuremyst Isles

Consisting of Azuremyst Isle and the neighboring Bloodmyst Isle, the Azuremyst Isles have been under the uncontested control of the draenei since their arrival in the Exodar. Bloodmyst Isle, though, is polluted by an arcane red mist that chokes the land and causes dangerous mutations of local fauna. (Alliance; 1–4)

###### Darkshore

The shadow-choked strip of coastline called Darkshore is littered with the monster-occupied ruins of ancient night elf fortifications as well as recent battlefields. (Alliance; 1–4)

###### Desolace

Having grown increasingly green and fertile since the climate-altering effects of the Cataclysm, the former wasteland of Desolace is now the site of constant struggle between the Alliance, the Horde, the Burning Blade, the Cenarion Circle, and multiple centaur tribes. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Durotar

Durotar is a harsh region of dusty badlands, deep canyons, flooding rivers, and vicious predators. It is also the new homeland of the orcs and a haven for all the Horde. (Horde; 1–4)

###### Dustwallow Marsh

Neither the Alliance nor the Horde has made much headway in Dustwallow Marsh, a viciously inhospitable wetland. The Alliance holds a few outposts of the now obliterated Theramore Isle, and a few small communities such as Brackenwall Village and Mudsprocket support the Horde. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Felwood

The corrupted forest of Felwood, full of infernally tainted elementals and rotting treants, stands between Alliance and Horde territories. It is a highly contested land; neither side wants it, but neither side can afford to cede it. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Feralas

Deadly clans and communities of local inhabitants, of which the Gordunni ogres are the most numerous, prevent both the Alliance and the Horde from claiming much of the resource-rich Feralas jungle. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Moonglade

x druids and their powerful Moonglade Warden guards swiftly quash any sectarian violence that threatens the peace of the lush Moonglade vale. (Neutral; 11–16)

###### Mount Hyjal

Boasting the roots of the World Tree Nordrassil and the Second Well of Eternity beneath its peak, Mount Hyjal is frequently the target of countless factions, armies, and would-be warlords who desperately seek its power. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Mulgore

The game-rich grasslands of Mulgore are tauren's ancestral home. While this area is relatively peaceful, it is also the home of dangerous beasts and the violent extremists of the Grimtotem tribe. (Horde; 1–4)

###### Northern Barrens

The vast savanna of the Northern Barrens is now separated from the south by the lava-filled Great Divide. Horde warriors coming of age use this region to prove their readiness for battle. (Horde; 1–4)

###### Silithus

Silithus is a blazing desert containing portions of lost Ahn'Qiraj. Despite dangers such as the all-consuming silithid, the allure of the mystical Silithyst crystal dust attracts the interest of Alliance, Horde, and independent explorers.

At the end of the Burning Legion's third invasion of Azeroth, Sargeras tried to destroy the world by plunging his enormous sword into the heart of the planet. He struck Silithus, creating a massive wound that now dominates the desert surrounding the titan's sword. The wound has caused the lifeblood of Azeroth to seep to the surface, not just in Silithus but across the world, in the form of a mineral called Azerite. Efforts to control the damage and heal the wound continue. *(Contested; 11–16)*

###### Southern Barrens

After the Southern Barrens were blasted by Deathwing and rocked by earthquakes during the Cataclysm, many local Horde strongholds were lost or abandoned. The Alliance has since launched multiple incursions into this ill-defended region. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Stonetalon Mountains

The slopes of the Stonetalon Mountains are home to fortified outposts of the Alliance and the Horde. Stonetalon Peak houses caverns sacred to tauren and night elves who, despite their allegiance to opposing factions, avoid shedding blood on holy ground. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Tanaris

The desert of Tanaris is haunted by many of the same monsters as Silithus, but it also serves as home to the Steamwheedle Cartel goblins in communities such as Steamwheedle Port and Gadgetzan. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Teldrassil

Formerly one of the great World Trees, Teldrassil was burned during the War of Thorns. Night elves work to restore life to its charred remnants and rebuild the community it once held. (Alliance; 1–4)

###### Thousand Needles

Countless stone spires and small mesas rise from the waters of a massive flooded canyon, giving the Thousand Needles region its name. Many of these islets host camps and small communities that must deal with pirates and dangerous fauna. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Uldum

The wastes of Uldum, which link the larger deserts of Silithus and Tanaris, are home to the feline tol'vir—and to uncounted creations and weapons of the ancient titans. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Un'Goro Crater

Full of surprisingly lush greenery considering its position amid great deserts, Un'Goro Crater might have served as both home and laboratory for the titans. Its strange creatures and alien magic lend credence to that idea. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Winterspring

The snowy vale of Winterspring—one lair of the x, which watches over the flow of magic—sees frequent conflict between goblin trading posts and night elves working to reclaim their ancient fortifications. (Contested; 11–16)

##### Alliance and Horde in Kalimdor

Judging solely by the size of claimed territories, Kalimdor would seem solidly Horde aligned. The Bilgewater goblins dominate Azshara. Durotar is held by the orcs, and Orgrimmar serves as the capital of both the orcs and the Horde. The Mulgore plains, ancestral birthplace of the tauren and now populated by countless tribes, anchor the Horde's claims in the southern part of the continent. Whether they're from cities such as the tauren capital of Thunder Bluff or wandering nomadic bands, even peace-loving tauren are quick to arms when it comes to defending their communities.

Compared to those bastions, the territories of the Alliance are minuscule. Yet small doesn't equal unimportant. Kalimdor is the ancestral home of the night elves, whose bonds to these lands are historical, cultural, and spiritual. The draenei are equally bound to and fortified within the Azuremyst Isles, where they first arrived on the world of Azeroth. The night elves and draenei fanatically defend their homelands, and their willingness to battle over neighboring territories has long prevented the Horde from cementing control over all Kalimdor.

Perhaps the fiercest conflicts occur in the forests of Ashenvale and Felwood, which stand directly between Alliance and Horde strongholds. The factions skirmish in Ashenvale over territory and its rich lumber and herbal resources. On the other hand, nobody wants the toxic and monster-ravaged Felwood, but its strategic location means neither side can allow the other to claim it.

While the Northern Barrens are firmly Horde territory on paper, guarding the large and open area is a logistical nightmare. Alliance scouting parties regularly slip into the Barrens to spy on or sabotage Horde endeavors. These missions become bloody when Alliance teams encounter inexperienced Horde warriors training on the proving grounds. Eliminating bands of soldiers is a tempting and strategically sound objective for Alliance teams.

Across the southern reaches of Kalimdor, the Alliance and the Horde maintain strongholds in scattered outposts, towns, and small cities, all of which stand in contested territories. None of these strongholds are secure enough to consider themselves safe, let alone to serve as a launching point for wider conquest.

Given the dangers that haunt Kalimdor, neither the Alliance nor the Horde would likely bother trying to expand its grasp were it not for the ancient magic and other secrets so prevalent in the region. The Alliance regularly lands military forces on the coasts of the Great Sea, hoping to overwhelm—or at least bypass—existing Horde strongholds and acquire those mystical or mysterious wonders.

##### Kalimdor Adventures

The adventure sites of Kalimdor range from cultural and holy places of lost or fallen cultures to hidden repositories of the most ancient powers of Azeroth.

###### Blackfathom Deeps

The dungeon of Blackfathom Deeps, located on the Zoram Strand in Ashenvale, is detailed in x.

###### Dire Maul

Formerly the Highborne elven city of Eldre'Thalas, the vast ruin known as Dire Maul is overrun by ogres, undead, and satyrs. It is detailed in x.

###### Maraudon

Maraudon is a combination of elemental temple and centaur burial ground located within the Valley of Spears in Desolace. Giants, oozes, and demons roam the tunnels of this sprawling complex, as do centaur ghosts and corrupted dryads.

###### Master's Glaive

Deep in the wilds of southwestern Darkshore, a colossal adamantine blade protrudes from the near-fossilized remains of a grotesque, tentacled aberration. The Master's Glaive was the weapon of Kronn, the only survivor of a band of twenty giant soldiers sent by the titans to battle Soggoth the Slitherer, herald of the Old Gods. Kronn might have left his weapon in Soggoth's corpse as a symbol of victory, or perhaps because he was so greatly wounded that he lacked the strength to retrieve it. Many believe the blade's presence prevents Soggoth from reawakening. The area is occupied by Twilight's Hammer, a nihilistic sect that seeks to revive the Slitherer—and then the Old Gods.

###### Onyxia's Lair

The lair of the black dragonflight's broodmother, Onyxia, is located in Dustwallow Marsh and detailed in x.

###### Ragefire Chasm

Ragefire Chasm is a cleft within the city of Orgrimmar. Despite its location in the heart of the city, it has long served as a hideout for cultists—first demonic cultists of the Burning Blade clan, and later a group called the Dark Shaman, made up of former members of Twilight's Hammer after the cult's fall.

###### Ruins of Theramore

Once a great fortified city and the Alliance's mightiest stronghold in Southern Kalimdor, Theramore is now a blasted ruin. Destroyed by an incomprehensibly powerful mana bomb, the city is slowly being reclaimed by the Dustwallow Marsh. Salvagers of the Alliance and the Horde, as well as independent adventurers, scout partially standing buildings and any accessible underground chambers, hoping to recover weapons, goods, and treasures of old. In addition to the threats posed by rival scavengers, those who trespass on this mass grave also face ghosts and small cults and sects that maintain secret hideouts in the ruins.

**Temple of Ahn'Qiraj**. The ancient, sprawling Temple of Ahn'Qiraj is home to alien qiraji emperors and their secrets.
