###### Tower of Althalaxx

In the distant past, the Tower of Althalaxx was the home of Athrikus Narassin, faithful vassal of the night elf Queen Azshara. Years of boredom and stunted ambition led Athrikus astray, and many millennia after he vanished, he reappeared as the leader of the Cult of the Dark Strand, an offshoot of the demon-worshipping Shadow Council. Athrikus was eventually defeated and his home partly burned, but the remnants of Althalaxx still attract scavengers, researchers, and adventurers. The warlock's fiendish research and the Soul Gems that empowered him—still containing the spirits of trapped Highborne night elves—are rumored to lie deep within the spirit-haunted ruins.

###### Wailing Caverns

The corrupted Druids of the Fang dwell in a complex network of underground chambers and defiled waters called the Wailing Caverns in the Northern Barrens. These night elf druids came to the caverns imagining that they could connect the waters inside to the Emerald Dream to restore life and growth to the Barrens, but once inside they succumbed to the Emerald Nightmare. Corruption spread through the waters and twisted the druids into serpent cultists.

###### Zul'Farrak

Once the adobe-walled city of the Sandfury trolls in northwestern Tanaris, Zul'Farrak was slowly whittled away by the encroaching desert and constant war. Since the Cataclysm, it has been a sun-blasted ruin, half buried in the sands. While the surviving trolls have moved on, the ruined city isn't wholly abandoned. The population of warbeasts formerly kept by the Sandfury has grown out of control, hives of silithid have moved in, and undead trolls lurk in the city's deepest levels. These threats, and strange magic worked by Sandfury occultists, remain to be discovered by adventurers.

#### Eastern Kingdoms

*[Image: The former capital of Lordaeron, transformed into Undercity, is a bastion of the Forsaken. (book/WoW/078-05-004.former-capital-of-lordaeron.webp)]*

The Eastern Kingdoms is the eastern remnant of the ancient continent that broke apart in the Great Sundering. Before that catastrophe, these lands were held by the Amani and Gurubashi troll empires. In modern times, the Eastern Kingdoms have been the primary battlefield in the wars between the Alliance and the Horde, and the land still bears scars of those conflicts.

The northernmost portion of the Eastern Kingdoms, a distinct landmass called Lordaeron, was the seat of the human-led Empire of Arathor, which later fragmented into the Seven Kingdoms: Alterac, Dalaran, Gilneas, Lordaeron, Stromgarde, Kul Tiras (on an island to the southwest), and Stormwind (far to the south). These kingdoms existed in uneasy peace alongside the high elves of Quel'Thalas and the Wildhammer clan of dwarves in the Hinterlands until first the Horde and then the undead x ravaged the entire region.

The southern part of the Eastern Kingdoms, dominated by the kingdom of Stormwind and the dwarf capital of Ironforge, also holds the Blasted Lands, the site of the Dark Portal where the Horde first entered the world.

##### Eastern Kingdoms Regions

The Eastern Kingdoms are made up of three landmasses:

- **Lordaeron.** Lordaeron Lordaeron in the north is divided from the rest of the Eastern Kingdoms by a narrow strait crossed by the magnificent Thandol Span, a feat of dwarvish engineering.
- **Khaz Modan.** Khaz Modan Khaz Modan is the central part of the Eastern Kingdoms, encompassing the region from the Thandol Span in the north to Blackrock Mountain in the south.
- **Azeroth.** Azeroth The southernmost region, stretching from Blackrock Mountain and the Burning Steppes in the north to the tip of the Cape of Stranglethorn in the south, is named Azeroth, but the name is more commonly used for the world as a whole.

The regions that make up each of these landmasses are described below.

###### Alterac Mountains

Once home to a small but thriving human kingdom, the resource-rich Alterac Mountains are now locked in a struggle between the Crushridge ogre clan and the noble-led criminal organization known as the Syndicate. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Arathi Highlands

The great strongholds of both the Alliance (Stromgarde Keep in the west) and the Horde (Hammerfall in the east) in the Arathi Highlands hold a tentative peace, one that extremists on both sides strive to sabotage. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Badlands

The blasted and inhospitable expanse of the Badlands is claimed by both the Alliance and the Horde but is controlled by neither. It is home to fearsome beasts, savage troggs, and members of the x. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Blasted Lands

Scorched and devastated by the magic surrounding the appearance, destruction, and reappearance of the Dark Portal, the Blasted Lands are full of wandering beasts native to multiple worlds and timelines. Alliance and Horde teams occupy small outposts, ever watchful for further incursions. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Burning Steppes

Land traffic from the southern regions to Dun Morogh and points north must traverse the mountain passes and jagged badlands of the Burning Steppes. Dark Iron dwarves, reinforced by soldiers from Stormwind, help keep travelers safe from marauding orcs of the independent Blackrock clan. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Deadwind Pass

Horde invaders from Stonard in the Swamp of Sorrows occasionally attempt to take sections of Deadwind Pass. Greater threats in this gray, rotting, and haunted reach, however, are the Nightbane worgen, Deadwind ogres, and armies of ghosts. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Dun Morogh

The snowcapped forested mountains of Dun Morogh are the center of two major Alliance cultures, housing the dwarves' capital, Ironforge, and the war-torn, catastrophe-shattered city of Gnomeregan. (Alliance; 1–4)

###### Duskwood

Duskwood has been cursed to a twisted darkness and suffers the depredations of undead and other bloodthirsty creatures. Its many inhabitants cling defiantly to their homes and their fealty to Stormwind. (Alliance; 5–10)

*[Image: Stormwind City celebrates the victories of the Alliance. (book/WoW/079-05-005.stormwind-city.webp)]*

###### Elwynn Forest

The communities of the lush hardwood Elwynn Forest—such as Goldshire and Stormwind City—are the heart of the Kingdom of Stormwind and modern human culture. (Alliance; 1–4)

###### Eversong Woods

The Eversong Woods—ancestral home of the blood elves—enjoy a near-perpetual spring. But portions of the forest are still scarred by the Scourge and suffer the constant rise of undead. (Horde; 1–4)

###### Ghostlands

In the Ghostlands, a portion of the Eversong Forest never reclaimed by the blood elves, the undead blight is widespread. The dangerous Amani tribe of forest trolls also maintains a stronghold in the region. (Horde; 1–4)

###### Hillsbrad Foothills

Although largely under Forsaken control, the Hillsbrad Foothills and surrounding plains see many of the bloodiest and most frequent battles between the Alliance and the Horde, rendering any claim over the area tentative at best. (Horde; 5–10)

###### Hinterlands

The mountainous Hinterlands are home of the Wildhammer dwarves and their famous trained gryphons. This region sees a constant back-and-forth between the Wildhammer dwarves and high elves on one side and Forsaken and Revantusk trolls on the other. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Loch Modan

Drained during the Cataclysm, the dry lake bed of Loch Modan and its fertile surroundings remain under dwarves' control despite constant threats of troggs, kobolds, and Mo'grosh ogres. (Alliance; 1–4)

###### Plaguelands

The aptly named Plaguelands were badly infested by x, resulting in a region of constantly rising undead. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Quel'Danas

The forested isle called Quel'Danas contains the Sunwell, a source of arcane and Light energy sacred to the blood elves. Its tremendous magical power makes it an attractive target to forces both mortal and demonic. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Redridge Mountains

The normally idyllic Redridge mountain range, home to many Stormwind communities, sees occasional raids from Blackrock orcs and the undead Scourge. (Alliance; 5–10)

###### Ruins of Gilneas

After many years of changing hands, the Ruins of Gilneas have been returned to the Alliance and the subjects of Queen Tess Greymane. Horde extremists still harry its streets in violation of the treaty, and scattered remnants of the Scarlet Crusade's occupation forces commit atrocities against both factions. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Searing Gorge

Despite being blackened and broken by an ancient eruption and recent industrial pollution, the Searing Gorge is a constant target for the Horde, for through it runs the only viable passage between Khaz Modan and the southern part of the continent. The powerful Dark Iron dwarves live in the gorge. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Silverpine Forest

The massive, primeval Silverpine Forest is silent and haunted, pockmarked with homesteads and hamlets abandoned in the struggle between the Forsaken in the north and desperate pockets of living resistance in the south. (Horde; 1–4)

###### Stranglethorn

Stranglethorn is a patch of tropical jungle divided by a massive sinkhole. It boasts a wide variety of venomous flora and fauna, native trolls, and thriving communities of Blackwater Cartel pirates. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Swamp of Sorrows

Due to its proximity to the Dark Portal, the Swamp of Sorrows contains nearly as many Horde communities as it does Alliance. It is the site of some of the most frequent and largest faction battles fought on the subcontinent, even though it offers little in the way of desirable resources or terrain. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Tirisfal Glades

Green-skied and blight-tainted Tirisfal is a Forsaken stronghold, but the undead of the Horde are beleaguered by Scarlet Crusade soldiers and terrors of the Scourge. (Horde; 1–4)

###### Tol Barad

The desolate Tol Barad peninsula is home to the mystical prison of Baradin Hold, which is under the control of its powerful inmates. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Twilight Highlands

The Twilight Highlands are a boiling cauldron of conflict as the Wildhammer dwarves, the Dragonmaw orcs, the x, a few lingering twilight dragons, and the nihilistic Twilight's Hammer cult vie with one another for control over the region's fortresses and resources. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Westfall

Westfall was the site of much destruction across wars and catastrophes. It briefly broke away from Stormwind due to the costs of war. Strong economic assistance and renewed protection turned this fiefdom into the kingdom's breadbasket. (Alliance; 1–4)

###### Wetlands

The Alliance claims the cold, gray swamp of the Wetlands, primarily because of the useful port of Menethil Harbor. A small community of night elves, Greenwarden's Grove, and the dwarf-built fortresses of Dun Algaz and Dun Modr extend the Alliance claim over the eastern part of the swamp, but Dragonmaw orcs and a growing population of murlocs threaten the Alliance's hold. (Alliance; 5–10)

##### Alliance and Horde in the Eastern Kingdoms

The conflict between the Alliance and the Horde defines the modern history of the Eastern Kingdoms. After four wars, starting with the opening of the Dark Portal and continuing intermittently over the course of almost thirty-five years, the conflict has simmered down to a tenuous truce. The Horde's main power centers are the blood elf kingdom of Quel'Thalas in the north and the Ruins of Lordaeron, the new capital of the Forsaken. The Horde has only scattered holdings in the southern part of the region, with a few groups of orcs maintaining holdings in the Twilight Highlands, the Redridge Mountains, and the Swamp of Sorrows.

Gilneas is the only major Alliance holding in Lordaeron, but the Alliance is much stronger in the rest of the Eastern Kingdoms, with Ironforge, Stormwind, and Gnomeregan controlling most of the southern continent.

Despite the many horrors and indiscriminately violent groups that occupy the area—or perhaps because of them—Lordaeron has also witnessed some of the most significant strides toward peace between the Alliance and the Horde in the modern era. In the wake of Sylvanas's departure from the Horde, the Desolate Council negotiated a peaceful turnover of the Ruins of Gilneas to the human and worgen subjects of Queen Tess Greymane, and King Danath Trollbane of Stromgarde signed treaties with Overlord Geya'rah of the Mag'har orcs. In both instances, this cooperation involves full military cooperation against shared enemies. The armies of Queen Tess and the Forsaken worked together to clear Scarlet Crusade intruders from the Ruins of Gilneas, while Danath and Geya'rah combine elements of the Kor'kron Guard and the 7th Legion into a single strike force to confront threats against both peoples.

Both situations are perilous, especially given that some members of each faction would love to see the peace fail. But if these partnerships can hold, they might provide an example of a better way forward for other factions of the Alliance and the Horde.

##### Eastern Kingdoms Adventures

*[Image: The Wildhammer dwarves, based in the Hinterlands, are famous as gryphon trainers. (book/WoW/080-05-006.wildhammer-dwarves.webp)]*

Given the ravages of x, many key Lordaeron sites are haunted, necromantic, or otherwise connected to undeath. To the south, a wide variety of adventure sites are linked to disasters caused by the greed, pride, or fear of the people living nearby.

###### Baradin Hold

Originally an immense fort with potent mystical defenses, Baradin Hold was repurposed as a prison for the worst criminals and dangers Azeroth—and worlds beyond—had to offer. Today, it holds power-hungry archmages, possessed x fanatics, and demons and entities from the deep Void. Baradin's Wardens have long since been driven off, leaving the inmates in control of the prison. While few adventurers would dare the halls of Baradin Hold, someone must travel there to ensure that the defenses still hold, that none of the inmates have escaped, and that no demon-worshipping or nihilistic factions are working to bring down the walls.

###### Blackrock Mountain

The many-layered dungeons beneath Blackrock Mountain include Shadowforge City, the capital of the Dark Iron dwarves; the Molten Core, where Ragnaros the Firelord enslaved those dwarves; and Blackrock Spire, where orcs, ogres, and trolls of the Dark Horde allied with the x led by Nefarian.

###### The Cauldron

The massive dig site called the Cauldron is perhaps the greatest project of the Dark Iron dwarves. For much of its history, the Dark Iron clan forced prisoners to do much of the physical labor. The dwarves were taskmasters or drove mighty excavation machines. Since they rejoined other Alliance dwarven clans in the region, the Dark Iron dwarves have officially abandoned those practices, but some individuals and small companies refuse to alter their methods. Dark Iron dwarves employ adventurers to delve deep into the Cauldron's subsections to retrieve prisoners and punish those who still use them as slaves. The Cauldron is home to a variety of fire-related elemental creatures, and possibly ancient creations of the titans or the x.

###### Dark Portal

In the Blasted Lands, the orc warlock Gul'dan and the human Guardian of Tirisfal, Medivh, first opened the Dark Portal, allowing the orcs of the Old Horde to cross into Azeroth from the world of Draenor. All the struggle, bloodshed, anddevastation of the conflict between the Alliance and the Horde stem from this initial event.

The portal also led to further interplanar conflicts and invasions by linking parallel realities and alternate histories. The greatest wizards and sages aren't certain how the portal operates.

Although history provides substantial motivation for the Alliance, the Horde, and smaller factions to keep watch over the Dark Portal, the short span of mortal memory makes those observers few in number and unprepared for decisive action.

###### Deadmines

A sprawling set of underground gold mines extends beneath Westfall and northern Stranglethorn. Once a major source of Stormwind's wealth, the mines were abandoned during the First War and later thought to be haunted, so they went largely untouched until the brigands of the Defias Brotherhood established a base there. The Deadmines are detailed in x.

###### Gnomeregan

Gassed and irradiated by a sabotaged effort at defending against trogg invasions, the gnomish kingdom of Gnomeregan was occupied by troggs and the so-called "leper gnomes" who had been mutated by the catastrophe. Today, the gnomes and their Alliance allies work to retake what was theirs. From New Tinkertown, a segment of Gnomeregan already reclaimed, they plan and launch missions into the depths of the many-layered community. Outside adventurers, whether employed by the gnomes or sneaking in as independent treasure hunters, can expect to confront troggs, leper gnomes, cunning traps of mixed technology and magic, and horrors born in the deeps.

###### Karazhan

Karazhan is the tower of Medivh, the Guardian responsible for the opening of the Dark Portal. The tower is rumored to hold numberless magical secrets and dangerous artifacts, as well as the haunt of countless ghosts and other evil spirits.

###### Nethergarde Keep

The great citadel and fortified town of Nethergarde Keep was constructed on a bluff overlooking the main road through the Blasted Lands. While its primary purpose was to guard the Dark Portal, it also served as a trading hub and a laboratory from which arcane organizations could study the portal and its magic. Nethergarde fell to the Iron Horde in the most recent otherworldly incursion, and though the ruin was eventually retaken by combined Alliance and Horde forces, it hasn't been rebuilt or refortified. Stormwind would like to reoccupy Nethergarde once any remaining dangers within are eliminated and any eldritch discoveries are cataloged and rendered safe.

###### Scarlet Monastery

Once the headquarters of the fanatical Scarlet Crusade, the haunted Scarlet Monastery is described in x.

###### Scholomance

Scholomance is a necromantic academy controlled and staffed by the Cult of the Damned on behalf of the Scourge. Adventurers might venture into the place in search of tomes containing forbidden knowledge, or they might hope to break the academy's curse by bringing about the final death of its headmaster, a Cult of the Damned leader called Darkmaster Gandling.

###### Shadowfang Keep

Shadowfang Keep was first held by a power-hungry archmage, then by the Forsaken who used it to develop necromantic weapons, and then by worgen of the Bloodfang pack. The keep is described in x.

###### The Stockade

The prison of Stormwind City is called the Stockade. It has often held leaders of the Defias Brotherhood (see "x" in chapter 6), as well as captured Horde spies, gnoll marauders, and other troublemakers.

###### Zul'Gurub

Zul'Gurub is an ancient temple-city in Stranglethorn. It was once the heart of the trolls' Gurubashi Empire. The trolls who remain here are swift, brutal, and deeply resentful over what they have lost. Zul'Gurub holds relics of the greatest Gurubashi priests and rituals that might reawaken the avatar of their deity, Hakkar the Soulflayer.

#### Northrend

*[Image: Angrathar the Wrathgate bars passage from Dragonblight into Icecrown. (book/WoW/081-05-007.icecrown.webp)]*

Perched at the top of the world, the frigid wasteland of Northrend boasts inhabitants as unfriendly as its climate. The taunka, thick-haired cousins to the tauren, are far more territorial than their kin, though some have found common cause with the Horde. The walrus-like tuskarr see almost everyone as an enemy, and the towering vrykul—the link between titan-forged and humans—eagerly embrace any excuse for battle.

While Northrend's ancient titan ruins, prisons, and laboratories aren't entirely abandoned and draw the interest of the Alliance, the Horde, and other explorers, its place in history would be relegated to ancient myth and the occasional eldritch discovery—were it not that Northrend was also the lair of the Lich King and the source of the world-threatening x.

Ner'zhul's soul was long imprisoned in Icecrown, emanating evil throughout the region, tainting the continent, raising the dead, and corrupting the world's connection to the Shadowlands. Once that soul bound itself to Prince Arthas, it gained a physical form that enabled it to walk free once more. It was powerful enough to spread its blight and assemble the undead into a nigh unstoppable Scourge.

Arthas was finally defeated, his successor stripped of his power, and the Helm of Domination destroyed—putting an end to the Lich King once and for all. But his wickedness hasn't entirely released its grip on the land. The Scourge's greatest strongholds are still in Northrend, though it is now more ravaging mob than disciplined army, and lingering evil remains in Icecrown. Even though the veil above Icecrown has largely healed, the links between life and death still bear the scars of Ner'zhul's machinations.

##### Northrend Regions

Northrend is what remains of the northernmost region of the ancient continent of Kalimdor from before the Great Sundering. It forms a crescent arcing from the western horn of the Borean Tundra to the eastern horn of the Howling Fjord.

###### Borean Tundra

Along with the Borean Tundra's frigid climate, its multiway conflicts involving the walrus-like tuskarr, the taunka, the local naga, and the undead Scourge, make the region hostile. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Crystalsong Forest

The inhabitants and flora of Crystalsong Forest were crystallized by an ancient accident involving the magic of elves and dragons. The forest now torments the few communities within its reaches with wretched horrors such as crystal dryads, crystal satyrs, and corporeal and spiritual undead. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Dragonblight

The wyrm graveyard called the Dragonblight holds countless bones, which the Scourge once sought to use to create undead frost wyrms. It is home to outposts of several cultures, but dragons appear to have departed. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Grizzly Hills

Despite the presence of warring furbolg tribes, the alpine-forested slopes of the Grizzly Hills have drawn settlers from both the Alliance and the Horde, as well as profit-driven business interests loyal to neither faction. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Howling Fjord

The jagged coastlines and high cliffs of the Howling Fjord are home to the warlike vrykul, descendants of titan servitors and progenitors of humanity. The Alliance and the Horde boast substantial presences here. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Icecrown

Although the mountainous, glacial region of Icecrown is no longer host to the lingering evil that was the Lich King, it remains a realm of death and gloom. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Sholazar Basin

Sholazar Basin is a lush jungle sprouting in the midst of frozen Northrend, occupied by myriad communities and fearsome beasts. Magical pylons, created in antiquity by the titans, protect the basin from both the icy environment and the undead Scourge. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Storm Peaks

The towering Storm Peaks, where the wind and the cold can kill even the most prepared explorers, are rife with ancient titan complexes. Some are still the homes of masters who have survived from bygone eras. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Wintergrasp

The frozen Wintergrasp valley sees some of the largest clashes between Alliance and Horde forces, as each seeks to lay claim to Wintergrasp Fortress—and the stores of titan-crafted relics locked in the Vault of Archavon. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Zul'Drak

The sprawling temple-city Zul'Drak, the final remnant of the Drakkari Empire, is now an icy ruin. Wandering its frozen streets are an array of undead horrors and the last Drakkari trolls, their minds shattered by the sacrifice of their animal gods. (Contested; 11–16)

##### Alliance and Horde in Northrend

The Borean Tundra is home to contingents of both the Alliance and the Horde, as most travel to Northrend passes through it. The great fortress of Warsong Hold serves as headquarters for the Horde on the frozen continent, while the fortified town of Valiance Keep serves that purpose for the Alliance. Smaller outposts of both factions exist across the icy expanse, and both sides watch each other warily, but overt hostilities are kept to minor skirmishes rather than full-on war. Each faction knows that it lacks the forces to decisively hold the entire tundra. Open war would deprive both sides of their only major access to Northrend and leave them open to tuskarr or taunka attack.

Beyond the Borean Tundra, both factions have established multiple strongholds across the continent, from the Alliance's Valgarde and the Horde's Vengeance Landing in the Howling Fjord to Frosthold and Camp Tunka'lo in the Storm Peaks. Bastions of the Alliance and the Horde can scarcely hold what territories they already have, let alone expand, due to their struggles against one another and the hazards of the merciless environment.

##### Northrend Adventures

Although the Lich King is no more, his evil still scars the lands of Northrend. Many of the continent's key locations still harbor his influence, whether through the surviving Scourge or the presence of his former allies.

###### Azjol-Nerub

Azjol-Nerub—a sprawling underground network of corridors, crevices, and caves that was once the kingdom of the arachnoid nerubians—is detailed in x.

###### Naxxramas

Naxxramas is a former bastion of the Scourge and a flying necropolis that hovers over the Dragonblight. It was originally an ancient, subterranean ziggurat built by the nerubians of Northrend, but the Scourge conquered it and transformed it into a mobile citadel. The terrible necromancer (and later lich) Kel'Thuzad commanded the fortress and used it to begin the Scourging of Lordaeron.

###### Utgarde Keep

Towering Utgarde Keep was long a center of power for the vrykul, descendants of the titan-forged iron vrykul. Under the command of King Ymiron, these vrykul allied themselves with the Lich King and his undead Scourge. The war turned against them, and Ymiron was ultimately made undead before he was destroyed. The allegiances of the vrykul who still occupy Utgarde Keep are unknown. They conduct raids on travelers and local outposts, but the promise of ancient treasures and magical secrets makes Utgarde a tempting target for adventurers.

###### Vault of Archavon

Deep beneath Wintergrasp Keep, relics of the titans wait within the Vault of Archavon. The Alliance and the Horde constantly struggle over the fortress, but neither has acquired the secrets of the vault due to the immensely powerful and seemingly immortal giants and elemental revenants that dwell within.

#### Islands and Isles

In addition to its major continents, Azeroth also boasts a number of large islands and archipelagos.

##### Broken Isles

The chain of Broken Isles holds remnants of multiple ancient civilizations. It once hosted the portion of the night elf empire whose nobles dabbled with demonic forces and birthed the first naga. It is also home to a population of vrykul zealots. But it is perhaps most infamous for the Tomb of Sargeras, the x unholy passage to Azeroth.

###### Azsuna

Once the crown of night elf civilization, the island of Azsuna was corrupted by the demonic dealings of Queen Azshara. Night elf ghosts, naga, and servitors of the Burning Legion prowl the island. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Broken Shore

Sunken and then magically raised, the blasted Broken Shore has been partly restored through the efforts of druidic circles. Yet no druid magic can cleanse the Tomb of Sargeras or the extraplanar evil that emanates from within. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Eye of Azshara

The Eye of Azshara is heavily occupied by x determined to unearth and master powerful magic. They hope to use it to support Queen Azshara's quest to dominate and destroy other peoples. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Highmountain

The great peaks and deep vales of Highmountain churn with the ongoing war between local tauren and the deep-dwelling drogbar, who stole the sacred Hammer of Khaz'goroth. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Stormheim

The vrykul of the rocky, chasm-crossed isle called Stormheim stand united under their God-Queen Sigryn, ready to battle the Burning Legion or any other threat to what they view as holy land. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Suramar

Once isolated from the world by a millennia-old mystical shield, Suramar—the island home of the nightborne elves—still suffers the scars of its prior government's alliance with the Burning Legion. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Val'sharah

Formerly a land of natural protection under the stewardship of night elf druids, the rich forests of Val'sharah have been corrupted by creatures of the Emerald Nightmare. (Contested; 5–10)

##### Dragon Isles

The Dragon Isles, the ancestral homeland of the dragons, were separate islands even before the Great Sundering, and they teem with life that has been shaped by the powerful draconic magic suffusing the isles.

###### Amirdrassil

A new World Tree, nurtured in the Emerald Dream and planted with the blessing of the x, serves as a new homeland for the night elves after the destruction of Teldrassil in the Fourth War. (Alliance; endgame)

###### Azure Span

The ancient home of the x, the Azure Span, is a beautiful region of snowy peaks, misty forests, and icy tundras inhabited by tuskarr, gnolls, and furbolgs. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Forbidden Reach

The Forbidden Reach was created by Neltharion the Earth-Warder as a training ground for the dracthyr soldiers he created. (Neutral; 1–4)

###### Ohn'ahran Plains

The verdant Ohn'ahran Plains, the ancient home of the x, are now inhabited by centaur tribes. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Thaldraszus

The mountainous heights of Thaldraszus are the seat of the Dragon Aspects, who rule the isles from the city of Valdrakken. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Waking Shores

The Waking Shores are the ancestral home of the x and x and a wild land rife with elemental magic. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Zaralek Cavern

Deep beneath the Dragon Isles, Zaralek Cavern is the laboratory where Neltharion experimented in creating life and first communed with the x, setting him on the path to become the cataclysmic force called Deathwing. (Contested; endgame)

##### Kezan and the Lost Isles

The small island regions of Kezan and the Lost Isles make up the original home of the goblins. Although goblins have since become far more prevalent in other areas, they still retain control here, for themselves and the Horde. Although much of Kezan has been destroyed by volcanic eruptions, goblin communities still survive on the surface and underground. The Bilgewater goblins landed on the Lost Isles archipelago after fleeing Kezan. While many of them moved to Azshara, others remain to mine local resources. *(Horde; 1–4)*

##### Khaz Algar

Khaz Algar consists of a small island (the Isle of Dorn) atop a larger subterranean expanse. Long forgotten by the outside world, Khaz Algar was rediscovered when the Worldsoul of Azeroth revealed its name to Magni Bronzebeard, accompanied by dire warnings of danger. The Council of Six, leaders of the Kirin Tor and rulers of Dalaran, agreed to teleport the flying city to Khaz Algar to address this danger, but the city was destroyed. The survivors have taken up residence in Dornogal while adventurers explore the forgotten realm.

###### Azj-Kahet

The deepest part of the subterranean realms below the Isle of Dorn is Azj-Kahet, the capital of the last remaining empire of living nerubians (see x). Xal'atath, the Harbinger of the Void, has forged an alliance with the nerubian queen, Ansurek, who has shaped her people into an army corrupted by the blood of the Old Gods, poised to assault the surface world. (Contested; endgame)

###### Hallowfall

The vast subterranean realm of Hallowfall is poised between the shallower Ringing Deeps and the depths of Azj-Kahet. It is illuminated and warmed by an immense crystal called Beledar, lodged in the cavern ceiling like an artificial sun. An expeditionary force from the distant Arathi Empire found its way to this cavern a decade ago and established a colony there, centered in the city of Mereldar. The Arathi consider Hallowfall to be a holy land, revealed to their emperor in a divine vision from the Sacred Flame. (Contested; endgame)

###### Isle of Dorn

The surface of Khaz Algar is the Isle of Dorn, a lush island populated by earthen (see x). It is a verdant paradise with sweeping vistas, cascading waterfalls, and majestic mountains, with the massive fortress and earthen capital of Dornogal at its heart. (Contested; endgame)

###### Ringing Deeps

The colossal cavern known as the Ringing Deeps is home to the Machine Speakers, the earthen who maintain the gigantic machines built by the titans ages ago, as well as to large numbers of kobolds. (Contested; endgame)

##### Kul Tiras

Home to the kingdom of the same name, Kul Tiras was settled by human pilgrims from Gilneas. Although the ruling houses and the mighty Kul Tiran fleet are bound by treaty to the Alliance, political squabbles and constant pirate activity have allowed the Horde to make substantial territorial gains.

###### Drustvar

Under the oversight of House Waycrest, mountainous Drustvar provides ore for all Kul Tiras. Witch cults, elementals, and survivors of the Drust vrykul clan constantly endanger that vital trade. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Mechagon

Civil war has ravaged Mechagon, home of the mechagnomes. The island boasts stores of not only gnomish technology but also mechanical wonders of the titans. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Stormsong Valley

Stormsong Valley is a forested highland that provides food and lumber to Kul Tiras. It is threatened by naga, invading Horde forces, and more. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Tiragarde Sound

The waters of Tiragarde Sound are the heart of Kul Tiras. The capital city of Boralus, situated on a small island in the sound, is marked by political squabbling between House Proudmoore and other noble houses, which prevents the government from taking effective action against pirate armadas and criminal gangs. (Contested; 5–10)

##### Pandaria

*[Image: Pandaren settlements in Pandaria blend harmoniously into their lush surroundings. (book/WoW/082-05-008.pandaria.webp)]*

Home to the proud pandaren, Pandaria was hidden behind a veil of mists until that veil was dispersed in the Cataclysm. Pandaria recently (and barely) fought off the forces of Lei Shen, Thunder King of the mogu, a vicious titan-forged people. Although slowly recovering, Pandaria remains under threat from the Thunder King's followers, the insectile mantid, and the influence of sha—physical avatars of negative emotions. Pandaria maintains relations with both the Alliance and the Horde, though some communities ally with one over the other.

###### Dread Wastes

Separated from the rest of Pandaria by the Serpent's Spine, the Dread Wastes are the home of ravening, insectile mantid. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Isle of Giants

The obscure Isle of Giants is home to primordial beasts—rare or even extinct in other lands—and the Zandalari trolls who raise them. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Isle of Thunder

Lei Shen waged his war from the Isle of Thunder. Although the king is gone, his surviving mogu forces threaten to rise once more. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Jade Forest

A lush rainforest full of bamboo groves and ancient ruins, the Jade Forest has the greatest concentration of Alliance and Horde forces in Pandaria. Different villages have sided with different factions, rekindling old rivalries. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Krasarang Wilds

The Krasarang Wilds are home to viciously territorial saurok lizardfolk. The gloom-shrouded jungle is also subject to the Sha of Despair's oppressive miasma. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Kun-Lai Summit

Despite (or perhaps because of) the mogu population, both the Temple of the White Tiger and the hideout of the Shado-Pan, two of Pandaria's most potent groups of defenders, stand within the towering peaks of Kun-Lai. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Timeless Isle

Neither the divine August Celestials nor the yak-like, fire-worshipping yaungol are as strange as the Timeless Isle itself. It exists only at seemingly random interludes, vanishing from the world for months or years at a time. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Townlong Steppes

The Shado-Pan can scarcely contain the dangers raging across the Townlong Steppes, which include not only the clashing yaungol and mantid but also the influence of the Sha of Hatred. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Vale of Eternal Blossoms

The sacred Vale of Eternal Blossoms, a region of powerful nature magic and the remnants of titan experiments, has suffered the depredations of the mogu, extremist Horde factions, and the x. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Valley of the Four Winds

Containing the lush fields of Pandaria's breadbasket and watered by blessed rivers from the Vale of Eternal Blossoms, the Valley of the Four Winds is imperiled by mantid breaching the protective wall called the Serpent's Spine. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Wandering Isle

For many generations, the boldest pandaren gathered on the back of the colossal sea turtle Shen-zin Su to venture across the world. The turtle's back is the Wandering Isle, a small isle that traverses the seas. Its inhabitants are the descendants of a rare breed of pandaren: explorers who left their mist-shrouded homeland behind in search of knowledge and adventure. (Neutral; 1–4)

##### Zandalar

What was once a lush land of forests and fields is now partly sunken and ravaged by time and magic. This was the heart of the Zandalari Empire when trolls dominated most of the world. Today, it hosts the proud and resentful remnants of that mighty realm.

###### Nazmir

Nazmir is a stinking swamp littered with ruins and inhabited by blood trolls and occult practitioners who seek to free the Old God G'huun. (Contested; 5–10)

###### Vol'dun

Vol'dun is a scorching wasteland used as a place of exile by the trolls of Zuldazar. It is otherwise home only to vulpera and serpentine sethrak. (Contested; 11–16)

###### Zuldazar

Called the City of Gold, sprawling Zuldazar is the most ancient city in the world. It is the last remnant of the trolls' Zandalari Empire. (Contested; 11–16)

##### The Great Sea

Of all Azeroth's seas, the Great Sea warrants most attention. At its center churns the Maelstrom, an eternally swirling vortex where the Well of Eternity was destroyed during the Great Sundering. It is home to multiple underwater civilizations.

###### Nazjatar

An undersea city of stone structures and coral groves, Nazjatar is the center of x power but has splintered into powerful competing factions during the absence of Queen Azshara. (Contested; 17–20)

###### Vashj'ir

Sunken during the Great Sundering, the ruins of Vashj'ir are home to a large naga population that seeks to steal the power of the elemental lord Neptulon the Tidehunter. (Contested; 11–16)

### Beyond Azeroth

Not all adventures that begin on Azeroth stay on Azeroth. Explorers, soldiers, archaeologists, and treasure hunters of particular skill—or particular misfortune—sometimes find themselves thrust beyond the existence they know to other realms and planes.

*World of Warcraft* has brought adventurers to other worlds beyond Azeroth. The world of Draenor, the birthplace of orcs and ogres, was shattered after the Second War, leaving only the broken realm of Outland drifting like flotsam in the Twisting Nether. The world of K'aresh, home of the ethereals, was all but destroyed by the void lord Dimensius and is similarly broken. Argus, the original home world of the eredar, became the stronghold of the Burning Legion.

Adventurers have also visited other planes of existence. The Elemental Plane was constructed after the ancient war between the keepers and the Old Gods as a dimensional prison to hold the elemental lords and their servants. The Emerald Dream represents the untouched natural world of Azeroth. And the Shadowlands are the realms of the dead.

## Chapter 6: Adventures in Azeroth

*[Image: Anduin, Alleria, and Thrall battle Nerubians in Azj-Kahet. (book/WoW/083-06-001.nerubians-splash.webp)]*

The far-ranging story of conflicts told in the *World of Warcraft* game—as well as in the other works in the *Warcraft* line—offers limitless possibilities for adventures in a D&D campaign. You can use the information in this chapter to craft a campaign within the ongoing official timeline of the world of Azeroth, to timewalk through different eras of play (perhaps with Chromie's help), or to build a new storyline unique to your campaign. Other sources of Warcraft lore (including those mentioned in this book's introduction), as well as your own *World of Warcraft* experience, can help you fill in the details.

This chapter details the conflicts that drive the main storylines of *World of Warcraft* and a selection of the game's most iconic dungeons for you to incorporate into your own adventures.

### Adapting Azeroth

As you craft adventures for a Dungeons & Dragons campaign set in the world of Azeroth, you can find plenty of inspiration in *World of Warcraft*'s quests, dungeons, raids, and storylines. When adapting quests from WoW for the D&D game, keep in mind the following principles about how a tabletop roleplaying game differs from an MMO:

- **Avoid the Grind.** Avoid the Grind Repetitive tasks are more fun in online games than they are at a table with friends, and MMO characters fight more monsters than D&D characters do. Avoid quests that involve gathering numerous unimportant objects from slain enemies and quests that involve killing a lot of monsters until a particular item drops as treasure by random chance.
- **Make a Difference.** Make a Difference A D&D adventure should give characters the opportunity to make a difference in the world. Focus on MMO quests that advance the story of the game or significantly change the world.
- **Make Success Last.** Make Success Last When the characters accomplish something, don't reset that accomplishment. While MMO characters frequently repeat adventures, D&D players will be frustrated if everything their characters accomplish is undone so they have to do it again. If characters venture into a dungeon multiple times, change the dungeon between visits so the players can see the effects of what their characters did previously.
- **Shine a Spotlight.** Shine a Spotlight The characters in a D&D campaign are the stars of their story, and they shouldn't be overshadowed by other adventurers or even famous figures like Thrall, Anduin Wrynn, Jaina Proudmoore, or Sylvanas Windrunner. Let the characters in your campaign enjoy the spotlight and be the heroes that Azeroth needs.
- **Minimally Multiplayer.** Minimally Multiplayer Most D&D games have no more than four or five adventurers, with the rest of the world populated by nonplayer characters and monsters controlled by the DM. That means characters can't put loot they don't want up in the auction house for other players to buy—so customize treasure so it's useful for the players at your table.

### Conflicts of Azeroth

As 4 in the Dungeon Master's Guide, a helpful way to think about a campaign setting is to outline the conflicts that drive adventures in that world. Six interrelated conflicts have driven the action of the *World of Warcraft* game:

- x
- x
- x
- x
- x
- x

A D&D campaign in Azeroth might focus on only three of these, as the *Dungeon Master's Guide* suggests, or might shift conflicts in and out of the spotlight as the characters advance in level. This section describes each of these conflicts, including sketches of how they might manifest during each tier of play. Unlike the conflict arcs in the *Dungeon Master's Guide*, those described here include suggestions for "endgame" play—continuing play with the characters at level 20, gaining bonus feats as Bonus Feats at Level 20 in the Player's Handbook.

#### Alliance and Horde

The hostilities between the x and the x are a central conflict of the Warcraft setting. Ever since orcs first came through the 6 from Draenor to Azeroth and waged war against the humans of the Eastern Kingdoms, the Alliance and the Horde have sought each other's destruction or at least submission. Though other conflicts have forced the Alliance and the Horde to cooperate to repel greater threats, such truces are uneasy. To date, the longest has lasted eight years and still holds, though many wonder how much longer the peace can continue.

You can approach this conflict in a couple of ways. In the first, the characters belong to a single faction, either Alliance or Horde, and their adventures bring them into conflict with the champions and armies of the other side. This approach fits a campaign set between the opening of the Dark Portal and the end of the Fourth War, some thirty-five years later.

In the other approach, the adventurers might include characters aligned with both factions, and the conflict between the factions is an obstacle they must overcome—perhaps in the name of peace or as a means to overcome a greater threat, such as another conflict discussed in this chapter.

With this approach, the Alliance–Horde conflict doesn't have a campaign arc; rather, it complicates other arcs you're using. At every tier of play, introduce scenarios where the characters have to overcome tension, disagreements, or violence between the Alliance and the Horde so the factions can achieve peace or deal with the other threat.

##### An Alliance–Horde Arc

The conflict between the Alliance and the Horde might play out in a campaign according to this broad outline. This outline assumes that the characters have aligned themselves with one faction.

###### Levels 1–4

While engaged in other adventures, low-level characters might encounter the opposing faction. For example, when Alliance characters enter the x, they might find a prominent Horde leader being held prisoner. Or Horde characters exploring x might discover lost SI:7 agents sent by the Alliance. Alternatively, the characters might travel through areas where the Alliance and the Horde are skirmishing.

###### Levels 5–10

The characters get drawn into battle against the opposing faction. For these scenarios, you can find abundant inspiration from PvP battlegrounds in *World of Warcraft*. For example, the characters might be recruited to fight the other faction in Warsong Gulch or the Arathi Basin. In addition to battling rank-and-file soldiers of the opposing faction, the characters also encounter adventurers of a skill level similar to their own. Any NPC adventurers who survive these battles might become recurring opponents in the campaign.

###### Levels 11–16

As the characters reach higher levels, they might be drawn into more significant conflicts against the opposing faction. You might have the characters be among the first outsiders to visit x, setting off a rivalry with the opposing faction (whether or not that rivalry is fueled by the increasingly outrageous plans of Horde Warchief Garrosh Hellscream, as it was in the Mists of Pandaria expansion). Alternatively, the characters could find themselves in the midst of the Fourth War (the time of the Battle for Azeroth expansion), undertaking island expeditions and advancing the war campaign for their faction.

###### Levels 17–20

The characters are viewed as the champions of their faction by others—including the champions of the opposing faction—making the characters a target. The characters might strive for leadership of their faction, try to overthrow the leadership of the opposing faction, or pursue peace between the factions. Alternatively, the faction conflict could complicate another conflict; for example, the characters might battle the opposing faction in x to claim powerful artifacts for their faction to use against the Lich King.

###### Endgame

After the characters reach level 20, they are likely to join forces with the opposing faction to counter one of the world-threatening dangers described later in this chapter. They might need to set aside grudges against recurring champions of the opposing faction who have caused them much distress.

#### Burning Legion

*[Image: Illidan Stormrage learned the magic of the Burning Legion to wield it against the demonic armies. (book/WoW/084-06-002.illidan-stormrage.webp)]*

The Burning Legion is one of the most destructive forces in the physical universe. The fallen titan Sargeras created this vast demonic army to scour all creation. The Burning Legion moves from world to world, destroying everything in its path with devastating fel magic. No one knows exactly how many worlds and mortal civilizations the demons have annihilated in their unholy Burning Crusade.

The demons that make up the Burning Legion include creatures drawn from the many worlds the Legion has conquered. Sargeras corrupted two of the three leaders of the eredar people of Argus—Archimonde and Kil'jaeden—turning them and their people into demonic beings. (The third leader, Prophet Velen, still leads the uncorrupted eredar, now called draenei.) Archimonde empowered the monstrous pit lords and conscripted them to serve as living siege engines. Kil'jaeden brought in devious succubi and incubi (collectively called sayaad) to infiltrate prospective worlds for conquest and gather intelligence about their civilizations. The brutal doomguard, demon warriors of unsurpassed strength and cruelty, fought as the Legion's shock troops. The zealous shivarra became the Legion's foremost mystics and advisers, fostering a fanatical loyalty to Sargeras. These wicked creatures, among many others, bolstered the might of the Burning Legion. For more on agents of the Legion such as doomguard, felguard, felhounds, infernals, pit lords, satyrs, and shivarra, see chapter 7. Succubi and incubi (see the Monster Manual) also serve the Legion.

The Burning Legion has invaded Azeroth three times. In the War of the Ancients, ten thousand years ago, the Legion's invasion led to the Great Sundering that shaped the world. The Legion corrupted the orcs of Draenor and sent them through the 6, beginning the First War. The Scourge (see "x" below) has its origins in the fel magic of the Burning Legion, and the Scourge's advance in the Third War paved the way for the Burning Legion to enter the world a second time. Years later, a new portal allowed the Legion access to Azeroth—but the heroes of Azeroth took the fight to Argus and defeated the Legion there.

> **The Legion and the Fall of Argus**
> The story of the *Legion* expansion for *World of Warcraft* ended with the death of Argus's Worldsoul and the legion's defeat. After that, demons lose the Demonic Restoration trait in their stat blocks.

##### Goals of the Burning Legion

Sargeras formed the Burning Legion because he believed creation was fatally flawed. The presence of the void lords corrupting Worldsouls across the universe (see "x" below) was a prospect that terrified the titan. This threat ultimately convinced him that the only way to spare the universe from the potential of a Void-corrupted titan was to purge all creation in fire and begin again from the ashes. So he released the demons he had previously banished to a pocket dimension and formed them into his agents of apocalypse.

Sargeras and his lieutenants have a particular grudge against Azeroth, the only world they have failed to conquer—not once, but multiple times. The power of Azeroth's Worldsoul is undeniable, which makes it (in Sargeras's mind) that much more important that it be destroyed before it can be corrupted into an unstoppable void titan.

##### A Burning Legion Arc

A campaign featuring the Burning Legion might unfold according to this outline.

###### Levels 1–4

Early in their adventuring careers, characters might encounter the fringes of the x, such as satyrs in northern x or one of the cults serving the Burning Legion (for example, the Cult of the Dark Strand in x, the Veiled Hand in x, or the Burning Blade in x). These encounters can be an opportunity for characters to learn about the history of the Legion's interference on Azeroth.

The characters might aid Sentinel Elissa Starbreeze in Auberdine (in Darkshore), Delgren the Purifier in Maestra's Post (in x), and Delgren's agent Balthule Shadowstrike, who are investigating the Cult of the Dark Strand along Kalimdor's northwestern coast. That investigation culminates in the x in northern Darkshore. Alternatively, characters might join a similar investigation into the Burning Blade cultists residing atop Dreadmist Peak in the Barrens.

###### Levels 5–10

Having defeated one small cult, the characters discover that such cults are only parts of a much larger effort by a group called the Shadow Council to advance the power of the Burning Legion on Azeroth. The characters might confront lesser demons of the Legion in Demon Fall Canyon or Felfire Hill in Ashenvale, Mannoroc Coven in x, or Serpent's Coil in the x. These adventures might make it clear that the Burning Legion isn't just a past threat with lingering echoes in the world—it is a malignant force eagerly awaiting its next opportunity to destroy Azeroth. Worst of all, too many inhabitants of Azeroth seem willing to cooperate with the Legion in bringing about the destruction of their world.

###### Levels 11–16

The characters learn—perhaps while dealing with satyrs in x, detailed later in this chapter—that a doomguard has discovered a way to open a portal that will allow the Burning Legion to launch a new invasion of Azeroth. The demon might be reopening the Dark Portal, opening a portal to Argus, or creating a new pathway to another demon-infested world or plane of existence. Perhaps an artifact falls into the adventurers's hands and the characters fail to prevent the doomguard from using it, or perhaps the characters—intentionally or accidentally—use it themselves. Or perhaps the artifact is a ruse distracting the characters from the real plan to open the portal.

###### Levels 17–20

An advance force of the Burning Legion storms through the newly opened portal, and the characters face demons of such power and in such numbers as to challenge even their mighty capabilities. At Khadgar's suggestion, the characters might enter x to learn how to battle the Legion. In a climactic battle, they beat the Legion back to the portal, preventing any more demons from entering Azeroth.

###### Endgame

After fighting the Legion back to the portal, the characters pass through the portal themselves! In a realm crawling with demons, they confront ever more powerful commanders of the Burning Legion until they encounter the leader responsible for this incursion, which might be Sargeras (Empyrean with the Fiend creature type).

#### Dragonflights

*[Image: Alexstrasza the Dragonqueen led the dragonflights' return to the Dragon Isles. (book/WoW/085-06-003.alexstrasza-the-dragonqueen.webp)]*

In the primordial past of Azeroth, five mighty proto-dragons helped the keeper Tyr battle a horrific monster of their kind, the enormous Galakrond. To reward them for their aid, Tyr and most of the other keepers imbued these five with mighty powers so they could safeguard Azeroth. These were the original Dragon Aspects. The keepers also magically altered hundreds of proto-dragon eggs so the creatures that emerged from them would be born in the image of the Aspects. The dragons born from these eggs formed five distinct flights, each serving a different Aspect.

##### Dragon Aspects

The Dragon Aspects and their flights are described below, including recent changes among the Aspects.

###### Alexstrasza, the Life-Binder

Alexstrasza and the x steward Azeroth's living creatures. Due to her supreme wisdom and limitless compassion for all living things, Alexstrasza was crowned the Dragonqueen and given dominion over her kind.

###### Malygos, the Spell-Weaver

Malygos and the x were charged to explore, share, and protect the limitless realms of magic and hidden arcana. During the War of the Ancients, Deathwing slew most of the blue dragonflight, and Malygos spent millennia consumed with grief. During the war against the Lich King, Malygos and the resurgent remnant of the blue dragonflight launched an all-out war against the mortal mages he blamed for the world's troubles. Kalecgos has since taken on the Spell-Weaver's mantle.

###### Neltharion, the Earth-Warder

Neltharion and the x were given charge over the mountains and the deep caverns of the earth. During the War of the Ancients, Neltharion convinced his fellow Aspects to sacrifice a portion of their power and infuse it into the Dragon Soul, an artifact he designed to scour the Burning Legion from Azeroth. But unbeknownst to the other Aspects, Neltharion had fallen prey to the whispers of the Old Gods (see "x" below), and he turned the power of the Dragon Soul against his mortal and dragon allies, slaughtering almost the entire blue dragonflight. From that point on, Neltharion was known as Deathwing, and the black dragonflight retreated into a life of fear and seclusion as its members were hunted to the brink of extinction to avenge their Aspect's betrayal. After the reawakening of the Dragon Isles, Ebyssian became the new Earth-Warder, working to purify the black dragonflight of the Old Gods' corruption and help its members atone for their misdeeds.

###### Nozdormu, the Timeless One

Nozdormu and the x hold mastery over time itself. They move freely along the timeways, observing the timeways' flow and ensuring that nothing alters the true timeline.

###### Ysera, the Awakened

Ysera and the x were charged with watching over the growing wilds of Azeroth from their domain, the Emerald Dream. During the Cataclysm, Ysera awakened from her endless dream and now dwells in Ardenweald in the Shadowlands, while her daughter, Merithra, has taken her place as the Green Aspect.

###### New Aspect

In addition to the recent changes in the leadership of the blue, black, and green dragonflights, a sixth Dragon Aspect has arisen: Vyranoth, called the Frozenheart. The Aspect of Storms represents the Netherwing dragonflight and the Thorignir storm dragons of the x in the Valdrakken Accord (see x).

##### Dragon Stat Blocks

Stat blocks for dragons of the five original dragonflights appear in 7. Use the Monsters by Group in the *Monster Manual* for the proto-dragons in x, in the x, and elsewhere, though most of them are Neutral.

##### Goals of the Dragonflights

The Dragon Aspects and their dragonflights are complicated and powerful forces on Azeroth. The dragons watch over Azeroth and protect it from other threats discussed in this chapter, particularly the Burning Legion. However, individual dragons and even entire dragonflights have been corrupted or otherwise swayed from their protective missions:

- **Black Dragonflight.** Black Dragonflight Neltharion and the black dragonflight fell under the influence of the x and betrayed the other flights. Much later, Deathwing allied with Ragnaros the Firelord under the Old Gods' influence to bring about the Cataclysm.
- **Blue Dragonflight.** Blue Dragonflight Malygos turned against mortal mages instead of fostering their study of arcane secrets. Other dragons of the blue flight have opposed mortals seeking magical secrets the dragons deemed too dangerous for them to wield, as when Azuregos and his dragonspawn guarded the Highborne relics in the ruins of Eldarath in x.
- **Bronze Dragonflight.** Bronze Dragonflight Some bronze dragons have been corrupted by Old Gods or other forces and sought to alter the timeways.
- **Green Dragonflight.** Green Dragonflight Some green dragons were corrupted by the Emerald Nightmare—the Old Gods' version of the Emerald Dream. They were known as the nightmare dragonflight. Several of them escaped into Azeroth to spread madness and terror throughout the mortal realms.
- **Red Dragonflight.** Red Dragonflight During the Second War, an orc warlock used Deathwing's Dragon Soul, which he renamed the Demon Soul, to enslave Alexstrasza and a number of her flight. The red dragons were forced to serve as war mounts for the Old Horde.

**Ahn'Qiraj**. Three dragons—a red, a green, and a blue—were imprisoned by the Old God C'Thun in the temple of x.

Of all the dragonflights, the black flight is the most likely to drive conflicts in an Azeroth campaign, thanks to the pervasive corruption of the Old Gods. The schemes of Deathwing's brood are far reaching and never ending. Deathwing's consort, Sintharia, created her own dragonflight in an effort to dominate Azeroth. And two of their children, Nefarian and Onyxia, have schemed against the mortal peoples of Azeroth, using human-seeming visages to sow chaos and distrust. Even Wrathion, one of the few black dragons who remains uncorrupted by the Old Gods, plotted to secure the safety of Azeroth by helping the Alliance eliminate the Horde. This pattern might soon change, though, as the black dragons have all but purged the Old Gods' corruption from their midst in recent years.

##### A Dragonflights Arc

In a campaign featuring the dragonflights, a dragon most likely serves as a patron for the characters, potentially establishing a relationship that will last for the adventurers' entire careers. The nature of the dragon patron can shape the campaign:

- **Bronze Dragon.** Bronze Dragon A bronze dragon such as Chromie (Chronormu) might invite the characters to undertake timewalking quests to experience various points in Azeroth's history. Later, when they have proved themselves capable, the bronze dragonflight could rely on them to stave off some force that is trying to alter the true timeline of Azeroth.
- **Dragon Aspect.** Dragon Aspect A benevolent but mysterious Dragon Aspect such as Alexstrasza or Ysera, seeing low-level adventurers' potential, might send them on quests that foreshadow conflicts and threats they will face later in their careers.

In *World of Warcraft*, many conflicts have revolved around the black dragonflight. This outline sketches a campaign centered on the corruption and redemption of the black dragons, largely inspired by the events of the *Dragonflight* expansion.

###### Levels 1–4

A black dragon such as Ebyssian (appearing as the Highmountain tauren spiritwalker Ebonhorn) or Wrathion the Black Prince (in his human visage) serves as patron to the characters as they deal with the weakest minions of the Old Gods, such as naga (see x) in x. The patron's draconic nature might be a secret at this point, or the patron might be open about their desire to redeem the black dragonflight.

###### Levels 5–10

The adventurers' patron sends them to investigate reports of black dragon whelps terrorizing a mountainous region after recent volcanic activity. After the characters deal with a few of the whelps, they spot a drake or two. Their patron asks the characters to determine whether the drakes are guarding a broodmother who could pose a severe threat to the region, but also urges them to avoid combat with the drakes, let alone a full-grown dragon!

###### Levels 11–16

Another would-be patron contacts the adventurers and causes them to question the true identity of their patron, dropping hints that the patron isn't what they appear to be. However, this new character is a disguised black dragon—perhaps even Onyxia or Nefarian—who wishes to foil the patron's plans. Meanwhile, the characters gather help and ultimately battle the broodmother and her guardian drakes.

###### Levels 17–20

The characters might venture into x to confront Nefarian or into x to face Onyxia (see "x" later in this chapter), serving their patron's goal of purging corruption from the black dragonflight. Their patron might join the battle alongside them, finally revealing themself as a black drake. The mission complete, the patron invites the characters to Wyrmrest Temple in x to meet the Dragon Aspects and celebrate their victory.

###### Endgame

Having proved their power and valor, the characters might continue their adventures in service to the Dragon Aspects. They might deal with the dragons in the 6 or help the Aspects against Deathwing when he rises in the Cataclysm.

#### Elemental Lords

*[Image: Adventurers confront Ragnaros in the Molten Core, the heart of Blackrock Mountain. (book/WoW/086-06-004.ragnaros.webp)]*

The elemental lords are the rulers of the primordial elements of air, earth, fire, and water. Forces of chaos and conflict, these elemental powers have been sequestered away in the Elemental Plane for millennia, but their cultists—and the cultists of the x, whom the elemental lords have often served—spread chaos across Azeroth in their name.

##### History of the Elemental Lords

Before the titans brought order to Azeroth, the world was ruled by destructive elemental spirits in a constant state of war with one another. For countless millennia, the four elemental lords and their subjects battled for dominance over the primordial world. Al'Akir the Windlord sent his minions to spy on his enemies and sow distrust to weaken them. Neptulon the Tidehunter fought strategically, playing his enemies off against one another before crushing their weakened forces. Ragnaros the Firelord used brute force to boil the seas, melt mountains to slag, and choke the skies with ash. Therazane the Stonemother barricaded herself behind towering mountain ranges, waiting for her enemies to exhaust themselves before emerging to crush them.

###### Arrival of the Old Gods

When the Old Gods came to Azeroth, the elemental lords joined forces to fight them, but even these mighty beings couldn't stand against the endless tides of the Old Gods' minions—the x and the insectile aqir. Eventually, the Old Gods subjugated the elemental lords, and armies of elemental spirits spread the Old Gods' Black Empire across the world. The elemental lords were enslaved by the Old Gods and served as their lieutenants across the Black Empire.

###### Imprisonment by the Pantheon

When the titan-forged armies of the Pantheon came to order the world, led by the godlike keepers, they imprisoned the elemental lords and their armies in the Elemental Plane, sealed the Old Gods deep beneath the surface of Azeroth, and shattered the Black Empire. Even in their exile, the chaotic elemental lords battled one another for thousands of years. During this long Elemental Sundering, Ragnaros the Firelord feasted on the essence of a mighty air elemental, Thunderaan the Prince of Air. Thunderaan's remaining essence was stored within a talisman, which Ragnaros broke in two and gave to his two most trusted lieutenants.

###### War of the Three Hammers

During the War of the Three Hammers, Sorcerer-Thane Thaurissan of the Dark Iron dwarves accidentally summoned Ragnaros from the Elemental Plane to the x. Ragnaros's entry into the world scarred the surrounding area, creating the x and the x. Ragnaros took up residence in x and enslaved the Dark Iron dwarves. Only the Firelord's struggle against the black dragon Nefarian for control of the mountain's vast caverns and halls kept Ragnaros from becoming a greater threat to the world during the next few centuries. Meanwhile, Neptulon used elemental lieutenants and mortal pawns to strike against Ragnaros and his elementals on Azeroth.

###### Alliance with Deathwing

After attempting to destroy the other dragonflights during the Second War, the black dragon Deathwing retreated to Deepholm (the earth region of the Elemental Plane) to recuperate and plan his next efforts. When he emerged from Deepholm, he shattered the World Pillar, which kept the Elemental Plane separate from the material world of Azeroth. Parts of the Elemental Plane began to bleed into Azeroth. Deathwing invited the elemental lords to join him in sowing destruction throughout the world, and Ragnaros and Al'Akir gladly accepted. Ragnaros and his fire minions launched an assault on x to destroy the World Tree Nordrassil, while Al'Akir focused his attention on 6, which was the seat of his power under the Black Empire.

Ultimately, adventurers destroyed both Ragnaros and Al'Akir on their home plane, and new elemental lords rose to take their place. Smolderon became the new Firelord, and Thunderaan—resurrected by the x—took on the mantle of Windlord. The shaman of the Earthen Ring marshaled the power of the elemental lords to repel the Burning Legion's third invasion, but the defeat of the Legion makes it unlikely the elemental lords will ever again have reason to unite with each other, though the Earthen Ring continues to strive for harmony and balance among the four elements.

##### Stat Blocks

A stat block for Ragnaros appears in x. For the other elemental lords, use the Elemental Cataclysm stat block; choose an appropriate damage type for its Elemental Burst attack, and forgo the random roll for its Cataclysmic Event. The elemental lords are served by the type=elemental in the *Monster Manual*, from mephits to genies, as well as by Humanoid cultists such as Cultists, Cultist Fanatics, Elemental Cultists, and Cultist Hierophants.

##### Goals of the Elemental Lords

Given free rein, the elemental lords would return Azeroth to its primordial chaos, in which the primal energy of the four elements would rage uncontrolled and elementals would war constantly against each other for dominion, as they did in ages past. That would be only the first phase of their victory; each elemental lord dreams of then vanquishing the others and transforming Azeroth into a mirror of their own domain of the Elemental Plane.

Of course, the elemental lords haven't had free rein for countless ages. Even before the Pantheon of Order came to Azeroth, the Old Gods dominated the elemental lords, and the Old Gods still seek to use them. Just before the Cataclysm, N'Zoth ordered the Elemental Lords to fight for the Old Gods in the coming war. When Neptulon and Therazane resisted, N'Zoth's servants imprisoned Neptulon, and the Twilight's Hammer cult fought Therazane.

##### An Elemental Lords Arc

To build a campaign around the elemental lords, you might draw inspiration from the role of the Princes of Elemental Evil in classic D&D adventures. In the same way that Imix and his ilk have been corrupted toward evil by the influence of the Elder Elemental Eye, Ragnaros and the other elemental lords have long been influenced by the Old Gods. You could use Princes of the Apocalypse as a model for a campaign featuring the elemental lords or draw inspiration from the Elemental Evil in the *Dungeon Master's Guide*.

Alternatively, you could build a campaign that focuses on the rivalries between the elemental lords as they lash out in destructive fury against the titan-ordered world. Such a campaign might follow this outline.

###### Levels 1–4

A shaman—pretending to be a benevolent member of the x—asks the adventurers to investigate what's happening in x. A cult devoted to the Firelord has taken up residence in the chasm and plans to swallow x in lava and ash. On emerging from the chasm, they find their patron has disappeared, and the Earthen Ring denies any knowledge of the quest, even while acknowledging that the fire cult needed to be stopped.

###### Levels 5–10

Several elders of the Earthen Ring—who seek to persuade the characters that they do actually represent the Earthen Ring, unlike the characters' last patron—ask the characters to look into a conflict among the centaur tribes in x. The Galak centaur tribe (which serves Al'Akir) is mustering terrible storm magic to assail a new centaur group, drawn from different tribes, that is devoted to Therazane the Stonemother. The Earthen Ring is perplexed at the rise of this cult of Therazane and its ability to unite disparate centaur tribes.

###### Levels 11–16

Destructive cults of all four elements begin a simultaneous attack against settlements across Azeroth—and against each other. While naga (see x) cultists of Neptulon assault coastal settlements, air elementals rain down terror from the skies. Earth and fire cultists alike pour out of the earth in volcanic regions to bring havoc to the settled world. The adventurers can't deal with all the attacks at once, and at least one of the cults gains a significant foothold. In the midst of this assault, the characters might also venture into x (in Desolace) and confront the elemental forces there.

###### Levels 17–20

Whichever cult has gained a foothold in the world summons its elemental lord patron into the world. The adventurers must venture into the cult's stronghold and banish the elemental lord back to the Elemental Plane before the triumphant elemental rampages across the world.

###### Endgame

After banishing the elemental lord from Azeroth, the adventurers might need to journey to the Elemental Plane to face the elemental lords on their home ground.

#### Old Gods

*[Image: Adventurers face C'Thun in the Temple of Ahn'Qiraj. (book/WoW/087-06-005.c-thun-battle.webp)]*

When the elemental lords warred across the face of primordial Azeroth, eldritch beings of the Void called void lords sought to harness the power of the titans to serve their nihilistic ends. The titans proved incorruptible, so the void lords resorted to trying to find and subvert a slumbering Worldsoul—an entity that would one day become a titan but hadn't yet manifested its full power. They sent parasitic entities out across the universe to attach themselves to many different worlds, hoping to find a young Worldsoul they could corrupt. Four of these parasitic entities—immense mountains of flesh, eyes, tentacles, and mouths—landed on Azeroth and implanted themselves in its surface to grow like tumors. They dominated the elemental lords and spread their influence over Azeroth, forming the Black Empire. These beings would become known as the Old Gods:

**C'Thun**. Resembling an enormous, chitin-plated sac ringed with tentacles, studded with eyeballs, pierced with toothy maws, and topped with a gigantic eye, C'Thun is a force of chaos. The keepers of the Pantheon of Order imprisoned it beneath x, where it was served by the qiraji.

**N'Zoth**. Called the Corrupter, the Deep One, and the Lord of Ny'alotha, N'Zoth resembles a gigantic octopus with eyes ringing its body and a toothy maw at the top of its head. Its eyes and its maw radiate fiery light. N'Zoth is responsible for the corruption of Neltharion, of Queen Azshara and the naga (see x), and of the tidesages of x. The titan-forged keepers imprisoned N'Zoth beneath what would become x.

- **Yogg-Saron.** Yogg-Saron Appearing as a great maw topped with many smaller, tooth-filled maws that almost resemble eyes and surrounded by writhing tangles of tentacles, Yogg-Saron is aptly called the Beast with a Thousand Maws, the God of Death, the Monster in Nightmares, and That Which Must Not Be Named. Imprisoned beneath Ulduar, in what would become x, Yogg-Saron was responsible for initiating the curse of flesh to make titan-forged beings mortal and therefore more corruptible. It also corrupted the World Tree Andrassil and planted a seed of evil within the Emerald Dream, creating the Emerald Nightmare. Its physical essence pervades Northrend in the form of the mineral saronite, which the armies of the Scourge use as fuel, building material, and weaponry.

**Y'Shaarj**. The so-called God of Seven Heads, Y'Shaarj was said to be the most powerful and wicked of the Old Gods, but it was also the first to be defeated by the Pantheon of Order. The titan Aman'Thul ripped it out of the planet, killing it in the process. Its death did such violence to Azeroth—creating a scar where the life essence of Azeroth spilled out, which became the Well of Eternity and later the x—that the keepers imprisoned the other Old Gods rather than destroy them. The keepers sealed the heart of Y'Shaarj beneath the x (in what would become x), where its dying breath created the seven prime sha—the Sha of Anger, Hatred, Violence, Fear, Doubt, Despair, and Pride.

The minions of the Old Gods include Humanoid cultists (Cultists, Cultist Fanatics, Aberrant Cultists, and Cultist Hierophants), as well as creatures of the Void such as aboleths, mind flayers (known on Azeroth as k'thir, they're a lesser type of n'raqi or "faceless one"), and several kinds of monsters described in chapter 7: x, x, and x.

##### Goals of the Old Gods

The Old Gods are responsible for many of the evils that have plagued Azeroth from its earliest days. The void lords sent them to Azeroth to corrupt the Worldsoul at the heart of the planet so it matures into an unstoppable void titan. This possibility was so horrifying to the titan Sargeras that he launched the Burning Crusade to prevent it (see "x" earlier in this chapter).

###### Ny'alotha

The dreams of the imprisoned Old Gods created Ny'alotha, the Slumbering City—an alternate reality where the Black Empire had once again conquered Azeroth. When N'Zoth was freed from its imprisonment, it sought to tear down the boundaries between Azeroth and Ny'alotha, imposing the Old Gods' horrific vision of reality onto the existing world and advancing their plot to corrupt Azeroth's Worldsoul.

###### Hour of Twilight

An ancient prophecy speaks of the Hour of Twilight, when the Old Gods would reemerge to end all life on Azeroth. The titans and keepers, glimpsing this possibility, empowered the dragonflights (see "x" in this chapter) to fight the minions of the Old Gods and prevent the Hour of Twilight, but the Old Gods' corruption of Neltharion, the black Dragon Aspect, nearly brought about the end of the world in the Cataclysm. For many years, the Twilight's Hammer cult has schemed to bring about the Hour of Twilight.

##### An Old Gods Arc

Antagonists devoted to the Old Gods can be found across the world and at every level of play, and the *World of Warcraft* game features abundant quests involving such antagonists. A campaign featuring the Old Gods and their servants might unfold according to this outline.

###### Levels 1–4

The characters might encounter Twilight's Hammer cultists in Cliffspring Falls and x in x, Mo'grosh ogres in x, or doomsday cultists at Jaggedswine Farm (outside x) or Mirror Lake Orchard (in x). In these initial encounters, the cultists behave irrationally and spout nonsense about the end of the world.

###### Levels 5–10

The characters' investigations might take them to x (described later in this chapter), where they directly experience the corrupting influence of the Old Gods. They might also confront the Necrodark in x as these cultists strive to free Uul'gyneth the Darkness from its bindings.

###### Levels 11–16

The bizarre proclamations of the end of the world start to seem like accurate visions of impending doom. The characters might confront the satyr Lord Vyletongue in x (in x), uncover the corruption of House Stormsong and the tidesages in x (in x), or face corrupted furbolgs at Vordrassil in the x (in x). And word might come from Silithus that the Old God C'Thun is stirring in x. At this level, they might face powerful minions of the Old Gods such as n'raqi (see x).

###### Levels 17–20

One of the imprisoned Old Gods begins to break free and extend its malignant power into the world. The characters might venture into the Temple of Ahn'Qiraj to face C'Thun. In addition, they could fight Yogg-Saron's n'raqi champion in x (described later in this chapter) or battle corrupted twilight dragons in the x.

###### Endgame
