After the defeat of C'Thun, N'Zoth tears down the walls of reality that separate Azeroth from Ny'alotha, the Slumbering City. The characters might battle versions of all the Old Gods across their dream re-creation of the nightmarish Black Empire.

#### The Scourge

*[Image: The Lich King is the iron-fisted commander of the Scourge. (book/WoW/088-06-006.the-lich-king.webp)]*

The Scourge is the result of foul necromantic magic unleashed on the peoples of Azeroth to pave the way for the second invasion of the Burning Legion.

##### History of the Scourge

The demon lord Kil'jaeden forced the first Lich King, the orc shaman Ner'zhul, to create the plague of undeath. The plague spread across x and then into the human lands of x, slowly killing those afflicted and then reanimating them as undead under the Lich King's control. The result was a massive army of skeletons, zombies, ghouls, wraiths, abominations, and other undead creatures, supported by living cultists and sinister plague-spreading devices. This horrifying force carved a swath of destruction into Quel'Thalas and used the power of the Sunwell to transform the necromancer Kel'Thuzad into a lich. The devastation of this attack led the survivors of Quel'Thalas to take on the name "blood elves."

###### Rise of Arthas

Ner'zhul chafed against the demons of the Legion and sought to escape from their domination. To that end, he manipulated Arthas Menethil into slaying the nathrezim Mal'Ganis, then lured Arthas to become a death knight and the Lich King's greatest lieutenant. Eventually, Arthas used the runeblade Frostmourne to shatter the Lich King's Frozen Throne and free the essence of Ner'zhul from his prison. Arthas absorbed the spirit and became the new Lich King—far more powerful than the first.

After this merging, the new Lich King slept atop the Frozen Throne for several years. While he dreamed and schemed, his minions rebuilt his fortifications at Icecrown Citadel. Finally, the Lich King awakened—having entirely consumed Ner'zhul's essence—and launched a three-pronged assault on x, x, and x, while personally leading his forces against the Argent Dawn (see x) forces in the x. In retaliation, both the Alliance and the Horde sent forces and champions to Northrend to dismantle the Scourge at its heart.

###### Bolvar Fordragon's Sacrifice

As the Lich King faced defeat, however, the spirit of King Terenas Menethil II—freed from Frostmourne to aid the Lich King's enemies—warned that the Scourge would be more dangerous to the world without a leader than it was under the Lich King's control. In a noble act of self-sacrifice, Bolvar Fordragon—an Alliance commander and former regent of Stormwind—donned the Lich King's Helm of Domination, taking command of the Scourge but damning himself to imprisonment in the Frozen Throne. The new Lich King slowly began reining in the terrors of the Scourge.

Years later, the Banshee Queen Sylvanas Windrunner defeated the Lich King and sundered his crown, forever freeing the Scourge from a Lich King's command. While the masterless Scourge proved a great threat, as the spirit had warned, Bolvar Fordragon had diminished that threat significantly during his reign on the Frozen Throne. To this day, the Scourge remains a danger, but one without clear purpose or direction.

##### Goals of the Scourge

The goals of the Scourge changed with its varying leadership over the years.

###### Lich King Ner'zhul

Under Lich King Ner'zhul, the Scourge was a puppet of the Burning Legion and shared the Legion's ultimate goal: to purge Azeroth of life so creation can begin again without its fatal flaw (see "x" earlier in this chapter).

###### Lich King Arthas

As Lich King, Arthas continued to serve the Burning Legion but also sought to justify his own deeds. His simultaneous attacks against Orgrimmar and Stormwind City were intended to draw the greatest heroes of both the Horde and the Alliance to Northrend, where he would make them face challenges to weed out the weak and make the strong question their morality just as he had done, planning to raise the mightiest of them as undead lieutenants of the Scourge in a mirror of his journey.

###### Cult of the Damned

Members of the Cult of the Damned are living servants of the Lich King (including Cultists, Cultist Fanatics, Death Cultists, and Cultist Hierophants). The archmage Kel'Thuzad (who later became a Lich) formed the cult and still leads it. Vile people who lusted for power were drawn to the cult in hope of immortality, and Kel'Thuzad founded the sinister school of x to expand the influence of the cult and share the secrets of necromancy. After the fall of Arthas, the Cult of the Damned became independent of the Scourge, spreading undeath in the Plaguelands.

###### Lich King Bolvar

Bolvar Fordragon's goal as Lich King was to weaken the Scourge, or at least to keep it contained. However, his efforts were undermined from within by the Cult of the Damned, and pockets of Scourge undead broke free of the Lich King's control and continued their relentless assault on the living. With the renewed attacks of the Burning Legion, Bolvar mustered the death knights of the Knights of the Ebon Blade to stand against the Legion's third invasion.

###### Dreadlords

One of the architects of the plague of undeath was the dreadlord Mal'Ganis. Though dreadlords are affiliated with the Burning Legion and wield the same fel magic as other demons, they are secretly servants of Sire Denathrius, the ruler of Revendreth in the Shadowlands, created to spread the influence of Death within the other forces of the cosmos. The role of Mal'Ganis and other dreadlords in creating and leading the Scourge hints at a sinister purpose beyond simply weakening Azeroth in preparation for a Legion invasion. Rather, the dreadlords sought to channel the Worldsoul of Azeroth itself through Icecrown Citadel and into the Shadowlands so death might reign supreme.

##### A Scourge Arc

The threat of the Scourge might unfold in a campaign according to this outline, assuming Arthas still sits on the Frozen Throne.

###### Levels 1–4

Low-level characters might encounter the Scourge in places such as the x and x—terrifying remnants of the marauding army that swept across x during the Third War. Though they are mindless, these skeletons, zombies, and other weak undead strike terror into the hearts of the living.

###### Levels 5–10

As the characters battle the Scourge, they might take the fight to Shadowfang Keep (see "x" later in this chapter). They might also encounter the Scarlet Crusade, whose goals initially seem aligned with their own—except for the zealots' utter abhorrence for anyone different from themselves. The characters might confront these misguided zealots in the x (also described in this chapter).

The characters might also face the Scourge in x, as the characters respond to Arthas's provocations and confront him on his home turf. This could lead the characters into partnership with the Argent Dawn, who show themselves to be everything the Scarlet Crusade should have been (see x).

###### Levels 11–16

As the Cult of the Damned becomes more troublesome, the adventurers might venture into x to put an end to the cult's vile teachings. In Northrend, they might face the Scourge and minions of the Old Gods in Azjol-Nerub (see "x" below). As they draw closer to the Lich King's seat of power—perhaps advancing through the x, the x, or the x—they face ever more powerful minions of the Scourge.

###### Levels 17–20

The adventurers might confront Kel'Thuzad in x before battling the Lich King in Icecrown Citadel. Perhaps the characters will face Bolvar Fordragon's choice: Will one of them don the Helm of Domination to keep the Scourge subdued, damning themself to the Frozen Throne in the process?

###### Endgame

After defeating the Lich King, the characters might venture into the Shadowlands to face the dreadlords and Sire Denathrius and finally end the Scourge.

### Dungeoneer's Guide

The rest of this chapter presents six iconic dungeons from *World of Warcraft* as adventure locations for your Dungeons & Dragons game. Here are a few things to bear in mind about the dungeon experience in D&D.

#### Pace of Play

The pace of play in a tabletop game is different from in an MMO. Exploring a dungeon like the ones described in this chapter is a task that might take several sessions of play for the real people around the game table and days for their characters. While WoW characters can quickly refresh their resources after a fight and enter every boss battle fresh, D&D characters need to take time to rest, regain expended spell slots, restore their Hit Points, and otherwise recuperate from the activities of adventuring. They might be able to rest safely in the dungeon, or they might retreat from the dungeon to make camp outside the entrance.

#### Pillars of Play

A D&D adventure—even one that centers around a dungeon—relies on the three pillars of play described in the *Player's Handbook*: Social Interaction, Exploration, and Combat. Expect players to examine the books on the shelves in the library, talk to neutral or even enemy nonplayer characters, and search for creative ways to bypass obstacles or encounters. Don't cling too tightly to how you think an adventure is supposed to go, and reward the players for clever ideas even if that means skipping over large parts of the adventure you expected to run.

#### Encounter Building

The dungeons presented here are less detailed than you'd find in a dedicated adventure product but more fleshed out than a simple adventure site. Each dungeon is intended for five characters within a certain tier of play. Naturally, characters at the higher end of that tier will have an easier time than characters at the lower end. Use the Plan Encounters in the *Dungeon Master's Guide* to craft encounters of appropriate difficulty.

These dungeons are populated with a mix of monsters from the Monster Manual and 7 of this book alongside dungeon bosses with unique stat blocks. Many of those bosses have traits that cause them to transform or revitalize during a fight, replicating the familiar MMO pattern of a boss battle with distinct phases. In effect, fighting these bosses is like fighting two or even three encounters and gives the characters a commensurate reward.

Along similar lines, use judgment when awarding treasure from the treasure tables in this chapter. Feel free to overrule the dice if they indicate a treasure no character will use, and choose specific armor and weapon types to match the characters' preferences.

#### Renown

Many of the adventure hooks presented for these dungeons include parenthetical notes about the potential for earning renown with the factions described in 4 by completing quests in these dungeons. Use characters' established relationships with these factions to flesh out the adventure hooks and draw characters naturally into their adventures.

### Azjol-Nerub

*For Level 17–20 Characters • The Scourge*

Azjol-Nerub, the old kingdom of the nerubians, lies in ruin. This arachnid people once dwelled in glorious cavern-cities carved through the stone of Northrend by enslaved jormungar worms. But times have changed. After the Lich King swept through Azjol-Nerub and reanimated the dead in his wake as his x minions, the surviving nerubians fled deeper into the earth. Their tunneling awakened a greater evil: the faceless minions of the x Yogg-Saron. Faced with war from above and below, a group of living nerubians called the Azjol-anak hid underground, plotting the downfall of both the Scourge and the Faceless Ones, as well as the reclamation of their lost realm.

#### Azjol-Nerub Adventures

Use the suggestions in the Azjol-Nerub Adventure Hooks table to create adventures there. Multiple hooks could be active at once, creating a main quest and a side quest.

*[Image: image (bestiary/WoW/Anubarak.webp)]*

**Azjol-Nerub Adventure Hooks**

| 1d4 | Adventure Hook |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | While the characters flee the Scourge across Northrend, the ground collapses beneath them, casting them into nerubian tunnels. Now they must find a way back to the surface. |
| 2 | Seer Ixit of the Azjol-anak approaches the characters and offers them great riches if they'll help slay Anub'arak (for more on Ixit and Anub'arak, see "x" below). (Renown: +3 with the x) |
| 3 | The Alliance or Horde seeks a way past the Wrath Gate into Icecrown Citadel and asks the characters to secure the route Arthas took through Azjol-Nerub long ago. (Renown: +3 with one x or x subfaction) |
| 4 | Explorer Brann Bronzebeard has returned to Dalaran, terrified by the presence of Yogg-Saron within the titan vault of Ulduar. He asks the adventurers to coerce the Old God's herald, Volazj, into being a guide for a return expedition. (Renown: +3 with the x) |

#### Azjol-Nerub Areas

*[Image: Map: Azjol-Nerub (book/WoW/090-06-029.map-azjol-nerub.webp)]*

*[Image: Player Version (book/WoW/091-06-029.map-azjol-nerub-player.webp)]*

The characters will likely travel through the following areas of Azjol-Nerub in the listed order:

- **Gilded Gate.** Gilded Gate Ice caves lead to a locked gate guarded by six Nerubian Crypt Fiends and a towering nerubian sage called Krik'thir the Gatewatcher (see "x"). Krik'thir holds the key to the gate; the lock is complex (1 minute to pick) and of superior quality (DC 20).
- **Web-Choked Chasm.** Web-Choked Chasm A slender bridge leads across a chasm but is broken halfway across. A wall of webs blocks further progress to the east, but a makeshift floor spun from webs allows characters access to a tunnel that spirals downward to the northeast. Halfway down, the characters are attacked by six Nerubian Crypt Fiends that rush down the long staircases in the west of the chamber from their barracks (not shown).
- **Hadronox's Lair.** Hadronox's Lair The spiraling tunnel leads down to a funnel web where Hadronox (see "x") waits for prey. Bones are piled into vast mounds beneath the stairs.
- **Passage of Lost Fiends.** Passage of Lost Fiends At the bottom of the funnel web, some 100 feet below the bridge from the Gilded Gate, a 300-foot drop leads through centuries of nerubian architecture to the depths of the Brood Pit.
- **Brood Pit.** Brood Pit In the Brood Pit, thousands of nerubian eggs, already infected with undeath, prepare to hatch into new warriors for the Lich King. A N'raqi Faceless One called Herald Volazj (see "x") meditates at the bottom, watched by two Nerubian Crypt Fiend brood keepers. Volazj psychically communes with Yogg-Saron while awaiting an audience with Anub'arak.
- **Throne of the Traitor King.** Throne of the Traitor King Anub'arak (see "x") dwells here, guarding the exit into Icecrown. A circular platform stands in the middle of the room; its top is 50 feet above a floor of webs. When the characters enter, he is burrowed into the platform, waiting to ambush anyone who crosses. Two Nerubian Venomancers lurk in the webs below.

> **About Azjol-Nerub**
> Introduced in the *Wrath of the Lich King* expansion, Azjol-Nerub is a dungeon designed for five characters of great power. The dungeon's story is part of the ongoing war against Arthas, the Lich King. By preventing him from enslaving the living nerubians of Azjol-Nerub, the characters simultaneously gain powerful allies and rob Arthas of potential Undead reinforcements.
> As presented here, Azjol-Nerub incorporates elements of a related dungeon, Ahn'kahet, called the Old Kingdom. While the focus of adventures in Azjol-Nerub is overthrowing the undead Traitor King Anub'arak, adventures in Ahn'kahet (the lower reaches of an ancient subterranean city) include the n'raqi and the malign influence of Yogg-Saron.

#### Resting in Azjol-Nerub

Back-to-back boss battles can exhaust the characters quickly, forcing them to rest. Nerubian structures are built into the cliffs above and below what's shown on the map. By ascending or descending 100 feet, the characters can find an empty room to rest in. The characters must make a DC 15 Dexterity (Stealth) check to try to conceal themselves during a Short Rest or a DC 25 check during a Long Rest.

##### Interrupting a Rest

If more than half the party fails this check, their rest is interrupted by a patrol of four Nerubian Crypt Fiends.

#### Treasure

When the characters defeat a boss listed below, choose the treasure labeled with that boss's name as a reward, or roll 4d6 to choose randomly.

**Azjol-Nerub Treasure**

| 4d6 | Treasure |
| --- | --- |
| 4–8 | Nerubian sculpture and jewelry worth 5,500 (10d10 × 100) GP to the Explorers' League |
| 9–11 | Krik'thir the Gatewatcher: A gnomish crab tank (Apparatus of Kwalish) that failed to breach the gate |
| 12–13 | A slain death knight's runeblade named Exile (Nine Lives Stealer) |
| 14–15 | The corpse of a dead adventurer carrying 10,000 GP worth of art and a Ring of Feather Falling that slipped off his finger |
| 16–17 | Herald Volazj: A saronite +3 Rod of the Pact Keeper etched with a thousand mouths |
| 18–19 | Hadronox: Life-Staff of the Web Lair |
| 20–24 | Anub'arak: Dwarven Plate and a Dwarven Thrower bearing the Bronzebeard forgemark |

#### Azjol-Nerub Characters

Azjol-Nerub's key NPCs are mostly minions of the Lich King, but those who fight the Scourge could become unlikely allies of explorers. These NPCs are presented in the order they're most likely encountered.

##### Seer Ixit

> Our enemies believe us vanquished. We will show them the consequence of their ignorance.

Seer Ixit, who might serve as a quest giver for this dungeon (see "x"), is an ambitious and resilient sorcerer with an enormous ego and an uncommon fondness for surface folk. He dreams of reviving the Nerubian Empire by banishing the Faceless Ones and annihilating the Scourge, starting with Traitor King Anub'arak. His dreams can be achieved only with the aid of the Alliance or the Horde.

Ixit is a Nerubian Venomancer.

### Blackfathom Deeps

*For Level 5–10 Characters • The Old Gods*

*[Image: image (book/WoW/095-06-011.blackfathom-deeps-splash.webp)]*

The ruins of Lathar'Lazal have become a nest of evil. Once a temple of Elune on x rain-drenched west coast, it has been defiled by the Twilight's Hammer, worshippers of the Old Gods. They were drawn to the ruin by a slithering scion of their slumbering masters: Aku'mai the Devourer.

To protect this burgeoning evil power, the cultists have allied with the naga and murlocs who occupy this ruin, threatening both the Alliance and the Horde.

#### Blackfathom Deeps Adventures

Use the suggestions in the Blackfathom Deeps Adventure Hooks table to draw adventurers there.

**Blackfathom Deeps Adventure Hooks**

| 1d4 | Adventure Hook |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Kaal Grimfang, commander of Zoram'gar Outpost, has ordered a strike against the naga that haunt the Zoram Strand. He asks the adventurers to strike into their lair in Blackfathom Deeps. (Renown: +1 with x) |
| 2 | Raene Wolfrunner, a night elf tracker, seeks a lost party of Sentinels, including a trainee. She thinks they were captured by the Horde and asks the characters to search the coast. While there, the characters discover that the Sentinels were taken to Blackfathom Deeps by naga. (Renown: +1 with x) |
| 3 | Je'neu Sancrea, a troll shaman of the Earthen Ring, has seen naga witches carrying gems thrumming with elemental power. Consulting the spirits, he heard a name: Aku'mai. Unsure of its meaning, he asks the characters to search Blackfathom Deeps. (Renown: +1 with the x) |
| 4 | Thaelrid Silversky, a night elf warrior of the Argent Dawn, has been hunting Kelris Doomfang, a shaman-turned-warlock who committed a string of ritual murders across Kalimdor. His cult lurks within Blackfathom Deeps. Thaelrid asks the characters to find him and bring him to justice—dead or alive. (Renown: +1 with the x) |

#### Blackfathom Deeps Areas

*[Image: Map: Blackfathom Deeps (book/WoW/096-06-030.map-blackfathom-deeps.webp)]*

*[Image: Player Version (book/WoW/097-06-030.map-blackfathom-deeps-player.webp)]*

The characters will likely travel through the following areas of Blackfathom Deeps in the listed order.

##### Drowned Sacellum

This half-flooded grotto is filled with crumbling kaldorei ruins. Characters who touch the water alert x of the Blindlight tribe lurking below the surface, which rush to begin combat.

##### Pool of Ask'ar

x warriors patrol the waters of this pool, always alert for aquatic invaders.

###### Central Island

Ghamoo-Ra (see "x" below) attacks any intruders on this island. When Ghamoo-Ra attacks, two Naga Myrmidons come to investigate from 30 feet away. If a character enters the water while fighting Ghamoo-Ra, there is a 50 percent chance each round the disturbance attracts another **Naga Myrmidon**.

###### North Cavern

The naga witch Lady Sarevess (see "x" below) conducts rituals at her bone-strewn altar, guarded by two Naga Myrmidons.

###### South Cavern

Night elf Sentinels Aluwyn and Relwyn Shadestar and trainee Issara are tied up here to be sacrificed on Sarevess's altar, guarded by a Naga Siren and a Naga Myrmidon.

##### Blindlight Grotto

Murlocs and naga patrol the Blindlight Grotto, kept in tenuous peace by their alliance with the Twilight's Hammer. The northernmost cave is the lair of Gelihast (see "x") and ten Murloc Foragers who attend him. If Gelihast is slain here, his altar to the Old Gods reacts to this tribute, giving each character a Charm of Heroism.

##### Moonshrine Ruins

Once-majestic bridges stretch over a vast pool toward a ruined moonwell, the centerpiece of this former temple to Elune. Members of the Twilight's Hammer patrol the entrance to their inner sanctum.

An underwater cave lies hidden under the platform leading into the Moonshrine Sanctum. A huge threshadon called Old Serra'kis, a creature similar to a plesiosaurus (use the Giant Shark stat block), lurks in that cave and rarely emerges.

##### Moonshrine Sanctum

When the naga took over the Moonshrine Sanctum, they erected a statue of Queen Azshara; when the Twilight's Hammer seized the temple, the cultists kept the statue, for Azshara communed with the Old Gods. Twilight Lord Kelris (see "x" below) makes sacrifices on an altar here, then offers the bodies to Aku'mai.

###### Lair Doors

Behind the altar are stone doors that open when all four braziers are lit (see below).

###### Ritual Braziers

Twilight Lord Kelris's rituals corrupt the elements with the power of the Void. Four ritual braziers at the corners of the altar must be lit to open Aku'mai's door. On Initiative count 20 of each round of combat with Kelris, one of these braziers lights with purple flame, summoning Kelris's allies in unoccupied spaces within 20 feet of the altar, split between the east and west sides of the area:

- **First Flame:.** First Flame: Eight makrura (Giant Crabs)
- **Second Flame:.** Second Flame: One snapjaw (Archelon)
- **Third Flame:.** Third Flame: Eight makrura (Giant Crabs)
- **Fourth Flame:.** Fourth Flame: Two Water Elementals

Once all four braziers are lit, the doors open. If Kelris dies before they are all lit, the characters can light them without summoning any more creatures.

##### Aku'mai's Lair

A dank cavern filled with the bones of countless sacrifices leads to an inky pool where Aku'mai (see "x" below) awaits fresh tribute.

> **About Blackfathom Deeps**
> Blackfathom Deeps is a classic dungeon from the original *World of Warcraft*, originally designed for five characters of moderate level. In its first incarnation and as presented in this chapter, the dungeon focuses on the naga's budding alliance with the Twilight's Hammer cult.
> After the Cataclysm, a new Twilight's Hammer leader came to Blackfathom, fed Twilight Lord Kelris to Aku'mai, and displaced most of the naga. In this era, the dungeon is populated with Cult Fanatics, Elemental Cultists, and Water Elementals, though the Blindlight murlocs are still numerous. Ghamoo-Ra remains in the ruins, stubbornly resisting the Twilight's Hammer's attempts to turn the turtle into a weapon. The other dungeon bosses include these key characters:
> **Domina** is a Forsaken Cultist Hierophant with the Undead type. She sacrificed Sarevess to Aku'mai after the naga failed to protect the cult's operations and now occupies Sarevess's cave.
> **Executioner Gore** is an orc Gladiator who specializes in dragging screaming innocents to the altar for sacrifice to Aku'mai. He lurks in the Moonshrine Ruins and might come to Thruk's aid if combat breaks out near the entrance.
> **Subjugator Kor'ul** is a n'raqi (Mind Flayer) that was charged with bringing Blackfathom Deeps under the cult's firm control. Kor'ul slew Gelihast first, and the murlocs in Blindlight Grotto are now under Kor'ul's command.
> **Thruk** is an ogre lord (Hill Giant) who is focused on killing the Guardian of the Deep and claiming its treasure. The Guardian of the Deep is a monstrous crab (Chuul) that displaced Old Serra'kis from the cave beneath the Moonshrine Sanctum. Thruk is most often encountered near the entrance to the Moonshrine Ruins, trying to lure the crab out of the depths.
> **Twilight Lord Bathiel** was once mortal, but the power of the x transformed him into an elemental ascendant (use the Marid stat block; Bathiel doesn't have access to Wish).

### Deadmines

*For Level 1–4 Characters • Alliance Versus Horde*

*[Image: image (bestiary/WoW/Edwin VanCleef.webp)]*

Deep within the Deadmines, the Defias Brotherhood plots Stormwind's downfall. These tunnels wind beneath the gold-mining town of Moonbrook in Stormwind's border province of x. Amid the chaos of the First War, the mines were occupied by orcs and ogres, then were abandoned for years until the Defias Brotherhood arrived.

The Defias Brotherhood was once the Stonemasons Guild of Stormwind, which rebuilt the city after it was razed by the Horde in the First War. The guild was a symbol of hope among the common folk. But when the corrupt nobles of Stormwind refused to pay them for years of labor, the guild started a riot that swiftly turned bloody. In the chaos, Queen Tiffin Wrynn was slain by a stray stone, and the bereaved King Varian outlawed the guild.

Edwin VanCleef, the guild's leader, tied a red bandanna over his mouth—a symbol of the innocent blood shed by corrupt nobility—and swore to raze the city. He forged the ragtag guild into the Defias Brotherhood, and his vow of vengeance against the nobility devolved into a reign of terror. He and his followers allied with gnolls and extorted the common folk to fund a weapon to destroy Stormwind City: a Juggernaut battleship being constructed in secret in the Deadmines. VanCleef's red-masked thugs coordinate kidnappings, smugglings, bribes, and assassinations across Alliance lands and are unwittingly in league with the dragon Onyxia.

#### Deadmines Adventures

The Defias Brotherhood has spread its tendrils through the heart of the Alliance. Its brigands bleed the villages of Goldshire, Lakeshire, Darkshire, and beyond and funnel the money to contractors working on the Juggernaut. Roll on or choose from the Deadmines Adventure Hooks table to determine how the characters learn of this conspiracy.

**Deadmines Adventure Hooks**

| 1d4 | Adventure Hook |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | When the characters help the victims of an extortionist called Morgan the Collector (Bandit Captain), they eventually discover that his house in Elwynn Forest has a ledger of the protection fees he's sent to Westfall. (Renown: +1 with x) |
| 2 | Riverpaw Gnoll Warriors raid a town the characters are resting in. In the raiders' base, their leader, Hogger (Gnoll Fang of Yeenoghu), has a record of payment from "EVC" for raids. (Renown: +1 with x) |
| 3 | Wilder Thistlenettle, a miner drinking in Stormwind, wants someone to find his brother, Eugene Thistlenettle of the Explorer's League. Wilder warns them the supposedly abandoned mine is full of bandits. (Renown: +1 with the x) |
| 4 | Edwin's right-hand man, a Bandit Captain named Bazil Thredd, flees the Stormwind Stockade pursued by four Guards. He runs into the characters and asks them to help him flee to Westfall, promising to pay them the gold he stashed there for their help. |

#### Deadmines Areas

*[Image: Map: Deadmines (book/WoW/100-06-031.map-deadmines.webp)]*

*[Image: Player Version (book/WoW/101-06-031.map-deadmines-player.webp)]*

The characters will likely travel through the following areas of the Deadmines in the listed order.

##### Mine Tunnels

Winding tunnels connect the entrance of the dungeon, the Mast Room, the Goblin Foundry, and Ironclad Cove. Heavy iron doors (AC 19, HP 72, open DC 20) separate these areas from the mines.

###### Laborers

Dozens of captive Stormwind citizens (Warrior Infantry) toil for Foreman Rhahk'Zor (see "x") who patrols the northern tunnels. These laborers aid the characters in killing Rhahk'Zor if they are freed. Their Speed is reduced to 5 feet by a ball and chain on their ankles, which can be removed by taking the Utilize action and making a successful DC 13 Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) check using Thieves' Tools.

###### Blasting Barrels

Every 20 feet along the tunnel walls are wooden barrels of blasting powder (AC 10, HP 5, Vulnerability to Fire damage). They explode when destroyed, dealing 11 (2d10) Fire damage to creatures and objects in a 5-foot-radius Sphere.

##### Collapsed Passage

The dwarves Wilder and Eugene Thistlenettle led a mining expedition into what they thought was an abandoned mine a few months ago. The tunnel collapsed, and only Wilder escaped. The Defias avoid this area, claiming they can hear the weeping of lost prospectors. Indeed, Eugene Thistlenettle (Wight) and his crew (Ghouls) are now Undead.

This passage leads east to a tower overlooking Ironclad Cove. It also leads south to a 30-foot drop into the cold water of a flooded cave.

##### Mast Room

Sneed's lumber mill turns Duskwood pines into timbers for building ships. If intruders attack, Sneed (Goblin Boss) leads the defense from inside Sneed's Shredder (see "x"). His six Goblin Minions clamber onto a mast hanging from the ceiling and hurl bombs (from a stash of twelve), using the following action:

###### Throw Bomb

The goblin throws a bomb at a point it can see within 60 feet. Dexterity Saving Throw: DC 12, creatures in a 5-foot-radius Sphere centered on that point. Failure: 10 (3d6) Fire damage. Success: Half damage.

##### Goblin Foundry

Gilnid's foundry smelts the ore from the mine's upper levels into engines and artillery. An enormous crucible suspended from the ceiling pours molten metal into molds and discharges the slag into drains. A crew of Goblin Minions and Bandits patrols here, but they flee or surrender once Gilnid (see "x") activates his prototype Foe Reapers (use the Animated Armor stat block, except their Slam attack deals Slashing damage).

##### Ironclad Cove

At the Deadmines' deepest point is Ironclad Cove, a cavern large enough to fit an ogre village. A series of locks has flooded the cavern, allowing goblin engineers to construct the nearly completed Juggernaut. The docks leading to the Juggernaut are crawling with Defias laborers (Bandits) and Bloodsail Buccaneers (Pirates) hired to crew the ship. Flocks of parrots (Blood Hawks) swoop overhead, hunting for intruders.

To board the Juggernaut, the characters must defeat or avoid three random encounters (from the Ironclad Cove Encounters). They can avoid encounters by sneaking past or bluffing their way in with disguises (see the sidebar "x").

###### Sneaking Aboard the Juggernaut

The stacks of cargo on the pier make good hiding places. If the characters successfully hide, they evade one random encounter.

###### Disguises and Suspicion

The Defias and the Bloodsail pirates mistrust each other and avoid interacting. If the characters each wear a red bandanna or a pirate hat and succeed on a DC 10 Charisma (Deception) check, they evade one random encounter by being ignored.

###### Exits

A footpath tunnel on the eastern wall leads to the southern coast of Westfall. The sea gate that occupies the entire northern wall leads through a series of locks to another sea gate on the northwest coast of Stranglethorn.

##### The Juggernaut

The Juggernaut's crew serves under Captain Greenskin and his tauren first mate, Mister Smite, who in turn report to Edwin VanCleef for the duration of their contract. Destroying the Juggernaut won't stop VanCleef, nor will killing him stop his minions from using the Juggernaut—only doing both will end this threat.

###### Reaching VanCleef

Edwin VanCleef (see "x") is in his cabin on the top deck. He is scheming with Mister Smite (Warrior Veteran) and two Goblin Warriors. The characters face one random encounter as they climb the scaffolding outside the ship.

###### Sabotaging the Juggernaut

Captain Greenskin and Cookie oversee the skeleton crew constructing the Juggernaut's steam engine and cannons. The characters face one random encounter in the ship's interior. Damaging the ship's engines or arsenal alerts Captain Greenskin (see "x") and Cookie (a Murloc Oracle). They arrive after 2 rounds and fight.

> **The Subtle Approach**
> The Deadmines is a good example of some of the principles outlined in the "x" section at the beginning of this chapter. In Dungeons & Dragons, a dungeon isn't necessarily just a gauntlet of combat encounters. Since characters can interact with the world in any way they can imagine, sometimes they concoct clever solutions to the challenges presented in a dungeon and take the adventure in unexpected directions. As the Dungeon Master, you might need to think on your feet to adapt to their plans.
> For example, players might come up with a plan to reach Edwin VanCleef without fighting his entire pirate crew—they disguise themselves as pirates and plant a rumor that turns the crew against the Defias. It's up to you to determine how this plays out, relying on the guidelines in the "Resolving Outcomes" section of the *Dungeon Master's Guide*. If the characters' disguises are successful and they plant a convincing rumor, probably requiring more than one successful Charisma (Deception) check, they might spark a brawl between the pirates and the Defias brigands.

#### Sample Encounters

Use the encounters tables here to help craft planned encounters for key locations within each area of the dungeon. You can also roll on the tables if characters make enough noise in the dungeon to attract guards from nearby areas to investigate. The Mine Tunnels Encounters covers the mines on the west half of the map, and Ironclad Cove Encounters covers the cove and ship on the east half of the map.

**Mine Tunnels Encounters**

| 1d4 | Encounter |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | 1d4 Ghouls and a dwarf Wight (Eugene Thistlenettle; see "x") chasing 1d4 Defias Bandits |
| 2 | A chain gang of 2d6 Stormwind Warrior Infantry brawling with two Defias Mage Apprentices |
| 3 | 1d6 Defias Bandits tormenting captive Stormwind Commoners whom they've forced to serve them cocktails while they work |
| 4 | Three prototype Foe Reapers (Animated Armor) chasing 1d6 Goblin Minions |

**Ironclad Cove Encounters**

| 1d4 | Encounter |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | 1d4 + 2 Bandits arguing with two Pirates |
| 2 | An Ogre shouting orders as 1d6 + 3 Goblin Minions struggle to hoist a cannon |
| 3 | Three Murloc Foragers looking for food |
| 4 | 1d4 parrots (Blood Hawks) looking for intruders; if the characters aren't disguised or hidden, 1d4 Pirates soon arrive to investigate |

> **About the Deadmines**
> The Deadmines dungeon has been part of *World of Warcraft* from the launch of the game, and its story has roots in the very first Warcraft game. In its original version and as presented here, it is the lair of the Defias Brotherhood under that group's original leader, Edwin VanCleef. During the Cataclysm, the dungeon's story advanced: Edwin VanCleef was slain, but his young daughter took his place and adopted his mission of revenge—fueled still further by watching her father die at the hands of adventurers. (Vanessa VanCleef uses the same stat block as her father.)
> Besides the VanCleef family, some other personnel changes took place in the Deadmines:
> **Glubtok**, a two-headed ogre mage (Large Mage Apprentice with the Giant creature type), took over Rhahk'Zor's role as foreman.
> **Helix Gearbreaker** took over for Sneed in the Mast Room. Instead of a shredder, Helix (Goblin Boss) is guarded by a hobgoblin called the Lumbering Oaf (use the Bugbear Stalker stat block).
> **Foe Reaper 5000** now keeps watch over the Goblin Foundry. An improved version of the earlier prototypes, this one uses the Scarecrow stat block and is aided by molten slags (Magmins).
> **"Captain" Cookie** has usurped the position of Captain Greenskin, though the worgen Admiral Ripsnarl (Werewolf) commands the Juggernaut.

##### Captain Greenskin and His Crew

### Dire Maul

*For Level 11–16 Characters • The Burning Legion*

*[Image: image (book/WoW/102-06-014.dire-maul-splash.webp)]*

Dire Maul is an ancient night elf ruin occupied by vengeful spirits, cruel arcanists, sinister satyrs, and opportunistic ogres. Once known as Eldre'Thalas, it was renamed Dire Maul by the Gordrunni ogres who dwell there now.

Twelve thousand years ago, the city was ruled by a scholarly Highborne prince named Tortheldrin and the Shen'dralar, his secret order of elf mages. They safeguarded the arcane libraries of Queen Azshara, which fell under attack by the x during the War of the Ancients.

The war ended with the destruction of the Well of Eternity, the source of the Highborne elves' immortality. Prince Tortheldrin's people discovered with horror that they had become addicted to the well's boundless arcane power, and their cravings soon turned to lethargy and agony. Panicked, the prince summoned a demon called Immol'thar and imprisoned it so his people could nourish themselves by siphoning its endlessly replenishing magic.

The secluded mages kept this secret for millennia. During that time, the fel power Tortheldrin consumed made him cruel. When the mages' population grew beyond Immol'thar's ability to sustain, Tortheldrin ordered the culling of his own people, sparing only his Shen'dralar, who retreated into a solipsistic obsession with ancient lore. Now, thieves and squatters have made the ruin their home, reveling just out of sight of the tormented mages within.

#### Dire Maul Adventures

The night elves of Darnassus seek to cleanse Eldre'Thalas of corruption. Meanwhile, the Horde's Stonemaul ogre allies seek to make Dire Maul their new stronghold. Roll on or choose from the Dire Maul Adventure Hooks table to involve the characters with these plots.

**Dire Maul Adventure Hooks**

| 1d6 | Adventure Hook |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Furgus Warpwood (Ancient of War) pleads with the night elf General Shandris Feathermoon to send adventurers to the Capital Gardens to slay his corrupted kin, Tendris Warpwood. (Renown: +2 with x) |
| 2 | Orhan Ogreblade (Medium Warrior Commander), leader of the Stonemaul clan of ogres, wants to conquer the Gordok ogres of Dire Maul by installing one of the characters as their king (see "x" below). |
| 3 | A Highborne archmage named Mordent Evenshade casts Sending, begging the characters for rescue. He tried to flee Prince Tortheldrin's reign of terror but was captured by ogres (see "Gordok's Seat" in "x"). (Renown: +2 with the x) |
| 4 | Prince Tortheldrin craves entertainment and loves epics. He invites the characters through his hawk familiar to dine in Eldre'Thalas and share tales of their adventures. If they accept, they discover his villainy. |
| 5 | The Crimson Ring pit masters have captured one of the characters' allies. The friend was kidnapped from the arena by the satyr Zevrim Thornhoof (see "x"), to be sacrificed in the vile blood rites of Hydrospawn. |
| 6 | A druid in the party has a nightmare of a night elf being strangled by demonic vines controlled by a sinister satyr. She begs the druid to find Eldre'Thalas and save the "Fruit of Fertility" before its corruption destroys Feralas. This elf is Millicent Serene, and the satyr has corrupted her masterwork (see "x" below). (Renown: +2 with the x) |

> **About Dire Maul**
> Dire Maul has been part of *World of Warcraft* almost since the beginning and includes three connected dungeons intended for a group of five mid-level characters. Its story is rooted in the ancient history of the world and is thus timeless, although current lore suggests that the dungeon has been cleared of its most dangerous inhabitants—including the Highborne elves that feed on Immol'thar's fel magic.

#### Dire Maul Areas

*[Image: Map: Dire Maul (book/WoW/103-06-032.map-dire-maul.webp)]*

*[Image: Player Version (book/WoW/104-06-032.map-dire-maul-player.webp)]*

Dire Maul is a sprawling complex made of three wings accessible via a central ruin.

##### Central Ruin

The ruins at the center of Dire Maul house ogre commoners and other scoundrels.

###### Eldreth Row

The front gates of Dire Maul are smashed open. Ogre sentries extort tolls from the scoundrels that congregate in the Broken Commons, and crush them if they refuse to pay. The ogres avoid the side passage into the Warpwood Quarter, because an Ancient of War with the Petrified condition blocks the path. It moves if the condition is ended.

###### Broken Commons

Throngs of scoundrels have set up tents and bonfires amid the ruins, cheering for bloodsport in the Maul. The games are held by the Crimson Ring, a band of thieves and outcasts from the Horde, with King Gordok's permission. This rough-and-tumble group welcomes outsiders, if only so they have new rubes to fleece.

###### The Maul

The coliseum was originally a temple built in honor of Queen Azshara at the height of the Kaldorei Empire. A doorway on the west side of the Broken Commons leads to a tunnel into the pit (though showboating gladiators love to jump straight in).

##### East Wing: Warpwood Quarter

The east wing is a grand conservatory corrupted by fel magic, which has attracted a satyr cult.

###### Warpwood Quarter (Outdoors)

A balcony overlooks the canopy of the trees in the Conservatory below. Characters with a Passive Perception score of 15 or higher spot the imp Pusillin (see "x") spying on them behind a statue in the north, clutching a silver key. He flees indoors; a character who takes the Search action and succeeds on a DC 13 Wisdom (Survival) check can track him to the Hidden Reach.

###### Warpwood Quarter (Indoors)

The satyr servants of Alzzin the Wildshaper lair here. A Shivarra bound to Alzzin's service toys with a cult of satyrs who wish to earn her favor.

###### Hidden Reach

Alzzin and his cult snuck into Dire Maul through this secret back entrance. Pusillin (see "x") hides in the loft in the south of this room. A character can find him by taking the Search action and making a successful DC 18 Wisdom (Perception) check. If they leave here without finding him, he flees through the eastern passage, emerging in the wilds of Feralas.

###### Conservatory (Indoors)

Characters who enter this area from the southern ramp must pass through the pool of Hydrospawn (Marid), a mighty water elemental. Zevrim Thornhoof (see "x") and his bodyguard (Doomguard) conduct blood rituals from the loft above the pool. They join the fight the round after combat begins with Hydrospawn.

###### Conservatory (Outdoors)

Despite its corruption, this lush garden flourishes beneath a shady canopy. Old Ironbark (see "x") is petrified in the middle of the room. The heavy stone door to the Shrine of Eldretharr is flanked by two Infernals and can be opened only by a character who succeeds on a DC 25 Strength check or by Old Ironbark if he is freed.

###### Shrine of Eldretharr

Huge, thorny vines breach the walls of this shrine. Alzzin the Wildshaper (see "x") has conjured a demonic power called the Felvine, which lets him sense all creatures within the Warpwood Quarter. Two Felhounds hide in the undergrowth near the Felvine sapling in the center of the chamber. Alzzin lurks invisibly on the east side of the room until his hounds attack, then he joins in.

##### West Wing: Capital Gardens

The west wing is the last remaining redoubt of the Shen'dralar. Their dependence on Immol'thar's magic prevents them from leaving. The stone doors within this wing are locked; the locks are simple (1 action to pick) and of superior quality (DC 20). Alternatively, they can be opened with Pusillin's Crescent Key (see "x").

###### Shield Generators

The dome of force in the Prison of Immolthar is generated by five pylons in this wing. See "x" for more information; they are shown on the map as bright blue crystals.

###### Capital Gardens

The courtyard of the Capital Gardens is a gnarled field of petrified trees oozing with fel sap. A ramp west of the garden leads down to Tendris Warpwood (see "x"), who guards the gate to the Prison of Immol'thar (marked D on the map) with two Treant allies (they lack the Animate Trees action). Stairs in the northwest part of the hall (marked E on the map) lead up to the Court of the Highborne.

###### Court of the Highborne

The undead forms of those Prince Tortheldrin slaughtered linger in the Court of the Highborne as Wraiths and Graveyard Revenants, endlessly repeating the motions of life. Illyanna Ravenoak and her giant bear Ferra (see "x") are cursed to guard this terrace. When she attacks, all remaining Undead in this area join in.

###### Prison of Immol'thar

Like a crass mimicry of the Well of Eternity, the imprisoned demon Immol'thar's fel magic is siphoned constantly by 2d6 + 10 Shen'dralar Mages to slake their thirst for magic. A dome of force prevents Immol'thar from escaping (see "x").

###### The Athenaeum

A passage from the northeast of Immol'thar's prison leads up a ramp (marked C on the map) to the Athenaeum—a library holding ten thousand years of lore in its countless precious tomes. Prince Tortheldrin scrawls endless, inane texts here, guarded by two Cultist Hierophants. He is also attended by 2d10 + 10 Cultist Fanatics, who cower in fear if he's attacked. A narrow passage (marked B) connects the Athenaeum to Gordok Commons in the north wing.

##### North Wing: Gordok Commons

The north wing holds a temple to Elune desecrated by the upper echelons of the Gordrunni clan, while the common ogres of the clan live in the Broken Commons or in camps in the wilderness.

###### Gordok Commons

Ogres use Gordok Commons for raucous parties, brawls, and crude theatre. It is patrolled by Guard Mol'dar, Stomper Kreeg, and Guard Fengus (see "x" and "x" below).

###### Halls of Destruction

King Gordok's guards rest in the Halls of Destruction, protecting the only path to and from their king's chamber. Guard Slip'kik oversees this area. He reports to x, who wards the entrance to Gordok's Seat. A goblin thief named Knot Thimblejack is a prisoner in the northeast corner (see "x"). A ramp in the southwest part of the hall (marked A on the map) leads up to Gordok's Seat.

###### Gordok's Seat

In Gordok's Seat, dozens of Hyenas sleep in the jungle heat while ogre servants swat flies and use giant palm leaves to fan King Gordok and his vizier, Cho'Rush the Observer (see "x" for both). The ogres scramble to protect their king, but any ogre who challenges the king to a duel of honor can vie for the crown in single combat (see "x" below). A Shen'dralar named Mordent Evenshade (see "x") is forced to be King Gordok's jester.

#### Sample Encounters

Use the encounter tables below to help craft planned encounters for key locations within each area of the dungeon. You can also roll on the table if characters make enough noise in the dungeon to attract guards from nearby areas to investigate. If the characters leave the dungeon and return after completing a Long Rest, new creatures replace those the characters defeated.

**Warpwood Quarter Encounters**

| 1d4 | Encounter |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Two Treants oozing fel sap defend the gardens from invaders, despite being corrupted. |
| 2 | A cult of 2d4 Felsworn Satyrs perform blood sacrifices while 2d6 Shadowstalker Satyrs keep watch from afar. |
| 3 | Two Felguards and two Felhounds hunt a Mage from the Maul. |
| 4 | A horde of 3d12 lashers (Vine Blights) try to immobilize the characters so a corrupted Ancient of War can kill them. |

**Capital Gardens Encounters**

| 1d4 | Encounter |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | An ancient equine spirit is held captive within a Violet Fungus Necrohulk made of elf corpses. If saved, it bestows a Charm of Heroism on each of the characters. |
| 2 | Two Graveyard Revenants and three Wraiths vent their rage on intruders. |
| 3 | Four arcane elementals (see "x") are incinerating a nosy Gladiator. |
| 4 | A bored Shen'dralar Cultist Hierophant and her arcane golem (Shield Guardian) take potshots at the shambling skeletons of her kin. |

**Gordok Commons Encounters**

| 1d4 | Encounter |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Stomper Kreeg has returned victorious from the Maul and is reveling. He has the Poisoned condition, and his party of 1d12 drunken Ogres have the Incapacitated condition, but their 2d8 Hyenas and two Dire Worgs are alert and begging for scraps. |
| 2 | Four two-headed ogre mages (Oni) are bullying another two-headed ogre (Ettin) that can't use magic. |
| 3 | An ogre guard (see "x") has taken four human Gladiators alive as a prize from the Maul and plans to boil and eat them. The captured warriors are in chains and have the Restrained condition. |
| 4 | An Eye of Kilrogg (a visible, green sensor otherwise like that created by the Arcane Eye spell, with a Passive Perception of 20) drifts by. If it spots the characters, it summons two Doomguard, then vanishes. |

#### Treasure

When the characters overcome an encounter, roll 2d6 on the Dire Maul Treasure table below. When they defeat a boss listed below, roll 4d6 or choose the treasure labeled with the boss's name.

**Dire Maul Treasure**

| 2d6 or 4d6 | Treasure |
| --- | --- |
| 2–8 | Highborne tomes worth 500 (2d4 × 100) GP |
| 9–11 | +3 Weapon or 20 pieces of +3 Ammunition |
| 12–13 | +2 Armor in sleek, Suramar City fashion |
| 14 | Hydrospawn: Bowl of Commanding Water Elementals |
| 15 | Pusillin: Horseshoes of a Zephyr |
| 16 | Zevrim Thornhoof: A fel-seeping, vine-etched +2 Rod of the Pact Keeper |
| 17 | Alzzin the Wildshaper: Nine Lives Stealer |
| 18 | Tendris Warpwood: Staff of the Woodlands |
| 19 | Illyanna Ravenoak: Giant Slayer |
| 20 | Immol'thar: Dimensional Shackles |
| 21 | Prince Tortheldrin: A fel-seeping Oathbow |
| 22 | Captain Kromcrush: Horn of Blasting |
| 23 | King Gordok: Crown of the Ogre King |
| 24 | Cho'Rush the Observer: Staff of Healing |

##### Ogre Tribute

Ogre tradition holds that any ogre who slays King Gordok in a one-on-one battle becomes the new King Gordok. If at least one character is disguised as an ogre, killing King Gordok in such a duel makes them the king.

###### Duel of Honor

A solo fight against King Gordok is a death sentence for most characters. You can make the fight easier by not using his Legendary Actions. However, the goal of a solo fight is to survive until someone realizes that King Gordok cheats by letting Cho'Rush the Observer secretly heal him (see the Adviser's Aid Bonus Action in his stat block). Revealing this causes the ogre audience to roar for the whole party to join the fight and kill the "cheater king."

###### Aftermath

If Cho'Rush survives, he declares the winner the new king. Without his declaration, the ogres all brawl to become the new king. To hail the new king, the surviving ogres bestow tribute or grant Blessings and Charms (see the Dungeon Master's Guide) with shamanic magic:

- **Guard Fengus:.** Guard Fengus: 3d10 × 10,000 CP
- **Guard Mol'dar:.** Guard Mol'dar: Charm of Vitality
- **Guard Slip'kik:.** Guard Slip'kik: Charm of Clairvoyance (functions as a Potion of Clairvoyance)
- **Stomper Kreeg:.** Stomper Kreeg: Strong rum worth 8d6 × 100 GP
- **Captain Kromcrush:.** Captain Kromcrush: Blessing of Valhalla that summons Ogres instead of Berserkers

**Cho'Rush the Observer**: Unread Tome of Leadership and Influence pilfered from the elves

#### Warpwood Quarter Characters

*[Image: Alzzin the Wildshaper (bestiary/WoW/Felsworn Satyr.webp)]*

Dire Maul's east wing has been taken over by the Wildspawn satyr cult, drawn to its demonic power. The creatures here are listed in the order they're most likely encountered.

##### Pusillin

> Eeeheehee, you thinks you has Pusillin cornered? Just looks at what Pusillin has up his sleeve!

A craven Fel Imp who knows every inch of Dire Maul, Pusillin has a Crescent Key, which opens any door in the Capital Gardens.

###### Conjure Scapegoat

Pusillin has learned a ritual from the warlocks of Dire Maul and keeps it on the brink of completion. He has the following Reaction:

###### Ritual of J'eevee (1/Day)

Trigger: Pusillin becomes Bloodied but is conscious. Response: Pusillin summons an Infernal, which appears in an unoccupied space within 30 feet of him and remains until killed.

The summoned infernal is enraged at being yanked through the fabric of space and indiscriminately battles Pusillin, the characters, and any other creatures in the area. Pusillin attempts to use the distraction to escape the area.

##### Zevrim Thornhoof

> Your blood shall catch Lord Xavius's eye! (Please?)

Zevrim is a Felsworn Satyr and the second-in-command of the Wildspawn satyr cult. He has a secret deal with the Crimson Ring to sacrifice gladiators who lose their bouts but survive. His blood rites have whipped the elemental Hydrospawn into a murderous frenzy.

Zevrim hates living in Alzzin's shadow; he tries to impress the Burning Legion in hopes of a promotion.

###### Magical Healing

Zevrim has the following additional Bonus Action available:

###### Gift of the Xavian (1/Day)

Zevrim regains 13 (3d8) Hit Points.

##### Old Ironbark

> Thank you, mortals. I shall act as your instrument of destruction before I return to the Emerald Dream.

Old Ironbark is a Friendly Ancient of War that has the Petrified condition. If freed, he helps the characters defeat Alzzin the Wildshaper. Attempts to end the Petrified condition on him (such as with a Greater Restoration spell) fail unless Zevrim Thornhoof is slain first.

#### Capital Gardens Characters

*[Image: image (book/WoW/106-06-016.capital-gardens.webp)]*

Dire Maul's west wing is home to the Shen'dralar and the spirits of their victims. The Shen'dralar are initially Friendly toward the characters but turn Hostile if the characters fight them or steal their tomes. The Shen'dralar are bound to the demon Immol'thar's power and can't leave the Capital Gardens for long. The creatures here are listed in the order they're most likely encountered.

##### Tendris Warpwood

> Ancients, rise up against these intruders!

Tendris Warpwood (Ancient of War) was an unquestioning defender of Eldre'Thalas, his heart inexorably entwined with the fate of the city. Just as fel magic corrupted his prince, so too did it infect the ancient's spirit.

##### Illyanna Ravenoak

###### Illyanna Ravenoak

*Speed:* walk 30 ft.

##### Immol'thar

*[Image: image (bestiary/WoW/Immolthar.webp)]*

###### Immol'thar's Prison

Immol'thar's prison is a dome of force sustained by five shield generators (shown as blue crystals on the map). Each generator is powered by four arcane elementals (see below). A generator shuts down if its defenders die or move more than 30 feet from it. When all five generators are offline, the shield falls. The Shen'dralar mages in Immol'thar's prison try to restrain the demon, but it slays them all with a burst of arcane power and runs rampant through Dire Maul.

###### Arcane Elementals

The arcane elementals use the Fire Elemental stat block, but they have Immunity to Force damage instead of Fire damage, and their traits and actions that deal Fire damage deal Force damage instead.

#### Gordok Commons Characters

*[Image: image (bestiary/WoW/King Gordok.webp)]*

The upper echelons of Gordrunni society have turned Dire Maul's north wing into their den. The creatures here are listed in the order they're most likely encountered.

##### Captain Kromcrush and His Guards

> Who you callin' gnoll lover? You da gnoll lover!

Kromcrush is the captain of the guard. He got his position by sweet-talking King Gordok, not through any exceptional skill. Kromcrush and his lieutenants use the Berserker Commander stat block, but as ogres, their size is Large and their creature type is Giant.

Kromcrush has four lieutenants:

- **Guard Fengus.** Guard Fengus Ever since Cho'Rush the Observer told Fengus he was destined to meet a mate while on duty, Fengus has spent almost every waking moment patrolling Gordok territory.
- **Guard Mol'dar.** Guard Mol'dar Mol'dar is a zealously loyal guard. He is convinced King Gordok gifted him with a pair of enchanted gauntlets that have made him the Gordrunni arm wrestling champion for six years running. In truth, King Gordok threw his old armor away—Mol'dar simply found it first.
- **Guard Slip'kik.** Guard Slip'kik Slip'kik is treacherous but lazy. Rather than openly challenge King Gordok, Slip'kik tinkers with traps and magical devices, hoping to eventually deliver a coup de grace without an ounce of risk or a drop of sweat.
- **Stomper Kreeg.** Stomper Kreeg Though Stomper Kreeg is often passed out drunk on duty, nothing rouses the ogre into a berserker fury faster than the sound of his precious drink splattering on the stone floor.

##### Knot Thimblejack

> Please tell me that you came to get me out of here!

Knot Thimblejack (Goblin Minion) is a tailor who made costumes for gladiators in the Maul, specializing in ogre suits. He was snatched in the night by ogres for his disrespect and thrown in irons. A character can take the Utilize action to try to pick the lock using Thieves' Tools, doing so with a successful DC 25 Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) check; alternatively, stealing the key from Guard Slip'kik's pocket requires a successful DC 15 Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) check. If the characters save Knot, he gives them a magic cap. It functions as a Hat of Disguise that allows the wearer to appear as an ogre.

##### Mordent Evenshade

> We Highborne must leave the past behind us.

Mordent Evenshade is a Shen'dralar Archmage who has weaned himself off Immol'thar's fel magic. He feels the pangs of arcane withdrawals keenly, but he is disciplined enough to endure until he can unite with the night elves and their sustaining moonwells.

###### Mordent's Goal

Mordent aimed to flee to Teldrassil and beg Tyrande Whisperwind for aid in freeing the rest of the Shen'dralar from Tortheldrin's evil, but his escape was cut short by the Gordrunni ogres.

###### One Last Trick

Mordent put up a desperate struggle to escape but was nonetheless captured. His captors demand that he use his spells to entertain King Gordok as a jester. He plans to cast his Fly spell when adventurers free him and distract the ogres.

###### Purifying the Felvine

Mordent Evenshade knew Millicent Serene when they were young and gave her the nickname Milli. He thinks that her masterwork, the Fruit of Fertility, could be saved if it were watered with water from the roots of Nordrassil in Mount Hyjal—possibly starting an adventure arc that sends the characters to the ends of Azeroth and pits them against the Burning Legion.

### Onyxia's Lair

*For Level 17–20 Characters • Dragonflights*

*[Image: image (book/WoW/109-06-019.onyxia.webp)]*

A jagged stone edifice looms in the southern wilds of Dustwallow Marsh. Near enough to threaten Theramore and spy on Orgrimmar, Onyxia, daughter of Deathwing, spins her schemes from the seething depths of her volcanic lair. The regent of Stormwind and the Warchief of the Horde will both pay handsomely for Onyxia's head.

#### Onyxia's Lair Areas

Onyxia's fiery magic has created a magma tunnel. It has the following featured areas:

- **Cavern Mouth.** Cavern Mouth The pitch-black mouth of the cave slopes downward and contains a magical trap. When a Humanoid walks down the path as it loops under itself, the path above detonates, collapsing in a shower of boulders in a 20-foot-radius, 40-foot-high Cylinder centered on the triggering creature. This seals the exit, and creatures in the Cylinder make a DC 19 Dexterity saving throw, taking 99 (18d10) Bludgeoning damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.
- **Path of the Warders.** Path of the Warders Two dragonspawn warders (use the Black Dragonflight Drake stat block from chapter 7, but remove their Fly Speed) defend this cavern. They are Onyxia's loyal guards. Each has a quadrupedal lower body and a scaled, humanlike torso with vestigial wings and a draconic head. Two more warders appear here if characters try to fight Onyxia from this area.
- **Whelp Caves.** Whelp Caves Onyxia's eggs incubate in the whelp caves. Each 10-foot square contains one whelp egg. If a Humanoid comes within 10 feet of an egg, the egg hatches into a Black Dragonflight Whelp and begins combat. A creature can avoid causing eggs to hatch until the end of its turn by taking the Hide action and making a successful DC 18 Dexterity (Stealth) check.
- **Onyxia's Hoard.** Onyxia's Hoard Onyxia (Black Dragonflight Dragon) lairs here. In her lair, she can use Legendary Resistance five times per day, and she can use four Legendary Actions per round. Defeating her in her lair earns a party 50,000 XP. She starts the fight on the ground, starts flying when she is Bloodied, then lands to flee through the secret exit. When Onyxia is Bloodied, one Black Dragonflight Whelp hatches on Initiative count 20 of each round. Replace Onyxia's Pounce Legendary Action with Tail Swipe:

###### Tail Swipe

Melee Attack Roll: +16 to hit, reach 20 ft. Hit: 36 (6d8 + 9) Bludgeoning damage, and the target is pushed 60 feet straight away from Onyxia.

- **Secret Exit.** Secret Exit An illusory wall in the northwest of Onyxia's hoard hides a secret exit that leads to the surface. A similar illusion conceals the tunnel, which opens into the rocky, southern hills of x. Physical interaction with either image reveals it to be illusory. A creature that takes the Study action to examine an image can determine that it is an illusion with a successful DC 22 Intelligence (Investigation) check.

*[Image: Map: Onyxia's Lair (book/WoW/110-06-033.map-onyxias-lair.webp)]*

*[Image: Player Version (book/WoW/111-06-033.map-onyxias-lair-player.webp)]*

> **About Onyxia's Lair**
> Onyxia's Lair was one of the forty-character raids that appeared in *World of Warcraft* when the game first launched. It is a classic adventure to slay a powerful, cunning dragon.

#### Onyxia's Lair Characters

Onyxia is the only character of note here. She might have guests, such as naga, Dark Iron supplicants, or her brother Nefarian.

##### Onyxia

> How fortuitous. Usually, I must leave my lair to feed.

Onyxia is manipulating Azeroth's factions. She has her father's arrogance and enough cunning to deserve it. Unlike her brother, Nefarian, she has little desire to monologue or stroke her ego. She flees fights she doesn't think she can win—such as if her human visage is revealed deep in enemy territory—and swiftly and confidently attacks when confronted in her lair.

In x, she uses her visage to masquerade as Lady Katrana Prestor, adviser to King Varian Wrynn. In his addled state, the king hangs on her every word. There, she is protected by dragonspawn disguised with illusions as Stormwind guards. She has made fewer inroads within the Horde, but from her lair in x, she secretly stokes tensions between x and x, preventing the Horde and the Alliance from uniting against the black dragonflight forces mustering in x.

### Scarlet Monastery

*For Level 5–10 and 11–16 Characters • The Scourge*

*[Image: image (book/WoW/112-06-020.scarlet-monastery-splash.webp)]*

The warriors of the Light have sacrificed much in the campaign to purge the ravening Scourge from the despoiled kingdom of Lordaeron. But pride and loss have warped zeal into fanaticism. The extremists of the Scarlet Crusade believe all who don't wear their scarlet tabard are plagued. The Crusade has abandoned the Alliance to purge Lordaeron not just of the Scourge, but of all non-humans.

The Scarlet Monastery is the Crusade's headquarters and looms on the edge of x. Scarlet Commander Renault Mograine has transformed this sprawling temple into a fortress.

> **About Scarlet Monastery**
> The Scarlet Monastery has been in *World of Warcraft* from the beginning, first appearing as four linked dungeons. They were designed for five characters, and each was more challenging than the one before it. The graveyard was suitable for characters not yet at the middle of the level range, while the cathedral was a challenge for high-level characters. The four dungeons were simplified into two in the *Mists of Pandaria* expansion, with many dungeon bosses remaining in place as the Scarlet Crusade resisted all efforts to oust them from their stronghold. During the x third invasion, however, the Knights of the Ebon Blade wiped out the crusaders and raised High Inquisitor Whitemane as a death knight.

#### Scarlet Monastery Adventures

Use the suggestions in the Scarlet Monastery Adventure Hooks table to draw adventurers there. An adventure could take place in one part of the monastery or take the characters from wing to wing.

**Scarlet Monastery Adventure Hooks**

| 1d6 | Adventure Hook |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | The leaders of the Argent Dawn ask the characters to claiming the Scarlet Commander's head. (Renown: +2 with the x) |
| 2 | Another group of adventurers tried to storm the cathedral, but they were captured. A mutual friend asks the characters to save them from the x. |
| 3 | Sages of Thunder Bluff mistrust the Forsaken and want to turn the tauren chieftain against them. They ask adventurers to retrieve the Compendium of the Fallen, a book of the Crusade's research on undead schemes, from the Athenaeum. (Renown: +1 with x) |
| 4 | Mograine and High Inquisitor Whitemane have left the monastery on a mission. The Argent Dawn asks the characters to infiltrate the cathedral and steal intelligence on their next campaign. (Renown: +2 with the x) |
| 5 | Herod, a crusader who trains recruits, never takes off his helmet. A faction commander instructs the characters to kill Herod quietly and replace him with a spy. (Renown: +2 with one x or x subfaction) |
| 6 | An Argent Dawn priest who seeks information about the sword Ashbringer known only by Fairbanks, the first High Inquisitor, asks the characters to find and interrogate his corpse. (Renown: +1 with the x) |

#### Scarlet Monastery Areas

*[Image: Map: Scarlet Monastery (book/WoW/113-06-034.map-scarlet-monastery.webp)]*

*[Image: Player Version (book/WoW/114-06-034.map-scarlet-monastery-player.webp)]*

The Scarlet Monastery is a sprawling complex consisting of a graveyard, library, armory, and cathedral. Its exterior is covered with gleaming stained glass windows, which have AC 15 and 30 HP and have Immunity to all damage from sources outside the monastery's walls. Secret doors within the monastery are standard (DC 15 to detect). The monastery includes these featured areas.

##### Grand Vestibule

At any given time, 2d6 Warrior Veterans and 2d6 Mage Apprentices loiter in the grand vestibule, drinking, brawling, and studying scripture. Two double doors to the north are sealed with Arcane Lock spells, but the Scarlet Key opens them (see "Athenaeum" in "x"). Pulling a torch sconce in the statueless southwest alcove opens a secret door to the Forlorn Cloister. The grand vestibule is suitable for levels 5–10.

##### Graveyard

Despite the Scarlet Crusade's unending war against the Scourge, the curse of undeath has taken root within the monastery grounds. The graveyard is suitable for levels 5–10.

###### Chamber of Atonement

The Chamber of Atonement is a morgue that Interrogator James Vishas (Medium, Lawful Evil Assassin) and his torturers (1d4 + 1 Cultist Fanatics) use as a torture chamber, teaching foes and new recruits to fear the Light.

###### Forlorn Cloister

Bloodmage Thalnos (see "x"), who haunts the western mausoleum, unwittingly carried the plague of undeath to this graveyard. The lawn smolders with burning corpses. The northern double door leading into the Cathedral is sealed with an Arcane Lock, but the Scarlet Key opens it (see "Athenaeum" below). The secret door leading from the grand vestibule is plainly visible and easy to open from this side.

##### Library

The library wing of the monastery contains plundered treasures. It is suitable for characters of levels 5–10.

###### Huntsman's Cloister

Scarlet beastmasters (Medium, Lawful Evil Scout Captains) train their tracking hounds (Dire Wolves) in the Huntsman's Cloister. Passing them without being noticed requires a successful DC 13 Dexterity (Stealth) check. On a failed check, the hounds begin combat, which also draws Houndmaster Loksey (see "x") and his three tracking hounds (Dire Wolves) from the south cloister.

###### Gallery of Treasures

The gallery overflows with gilded and marble art plundered from the few survivors of Lordaeron, ostensibly so the Crusade can protect them. A tapestry on the west wall hides a secret door to the Training Grounds in the Armory.

###### Athenaeum

The Athenaeum is a series of halls filled with tomes of lore and even heretical works hidden by Arcanist Doan (see "x"), who studies in the northernmost chamber. Doan holds the Scarlet Key, which opens the magically locked doors in the grand vestibule and the Forlorn Cloister. A ten-foot-tall portrait of High Inquisitor Sally Whitemane on the west wall hides a secret passage to the Crusader's Armory.

##### Armory

The monastery's armory is the focal point of its military efforts—training soldiers, housing and equipping soldiers, and commemorating its victories. The armory is suitable for levels 11–16.

###### Training Grounds

The courtyard has been turned into a target range, and the covered halls on either side are blocked off with iron portcullises. A tapestry on the east wall hides a secret door to the Gallery of Treasures.

###### Footman's Armory

The rank-and-file soldiers of the Crusade eat, sleep, and train in the filthy, cavernous cells of the Footman's Armory. Levers here control portcullises between each cell.

###### Crusader's Armory

Elite crusaders instruct their disciples in the Crusader's Armory. Artillery, battering rams, and explosives line the walls. A ten-foot-tall portrait of Commander Renault Mograine on the west wall hides a secret door to the Athenaeum.

###### Hall of Champions

Marble statues of the Crusade's founders, heroes from Stromgarde to Kul Tiras and even to Quel'Thalas (before elves were banished from the Crusade) watch over the Hall of Champions. which is Scarlet Champion Herod's sparring arena. The westernmost statue (of high elf mage Arellas Fireleaf) has crumbled. Beneath its plinth is a secret passage to a tool room in the Chapel Gardens.

##### Cathedral

The cathedral is the ritual center of the monastery, where its corrupted priests continue to pray to the Holy Light even as they serve the forces of darkness. The cathedral is suitable for levels 11–16.

###### Chapel Gardens

Statues of crusaders overlook the two-tiered Chapel Gardens. The soil is wet with blood, and the grass is flaked with ashes of "heretics" burned at the stake before the cathedral. x in both the lower and upper gardens.

A tool room to the east of the gardens contains a statue of an ancient crusader-gardener surrounded by hoes, rakes, and shovels. The statue pivots easily to reveal a trap door; a ladder leads down to a tunnel connecting the gardens to the Hall of Champions.

###### Crusader's Chapel

Scarlet Commander Mograine preaches in the Crusader's Chapel; High Inquisitor Whitemane prays in the secret crypt behind him and emerges only when Mograine dies (see "x" for both). Former High Inquisitor Fairbanks (Revenant) is interred in the secret crypt. The door can be opened from within or by pulling a candelabra on the chapel's altar.

#### Sample Encounters

Use the encounters tables here to help craft planned encounters for key locations within each area of the dungeon. You can also roll on the table if characters make enough noise in the dungeon to attract guards from nearby areas to investigate. If the characters leave the dungeon and return after finishing a Long Rest, new crusaders have mustered in the area. Unless otherwise specified, Humanoid NPCs found in the monastery are Medium and Lawful Evil.

**Graveyard Encounters (Tier 2)**

| 1d6 | Encounter |
| --- | --- |
| 1–2 | A Ghast Gravecaller and two Wraiths devouring a torture victim |
| 3–4 | A Graveyard Revenant and 1d4 + 1 Specters |
| 5–6 | 1d4 + 2 Flaming Skeletons from a body pile |

**Library Encounters (Tier 2)**

| 1d6 | Encounter |
| --- | --- |
| 1–2 | Twelve studious Priest Acolytes guarded by two Warrior Veterans* and a Mage |
| 3–4 | Three Mages bullying a paladin (Knight†) who struggles to perform holy magic |
| 5–6 | An Elemental Cultist purifying a relic from the x with 1d6 Priests |

**Armory Encounters (Tier 3)**

| 1d4 | Encounter |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Two Mages with two Fire Elemental bodyguards |
| 2 | An Elemental Cultist disciplining 1d4 + 1 Scout Captains for their poor aim |
| 3 | Berserker Commander brutally sparring with a fearful and Bloodied Knight† before a crowd of 1d6 + 6 Warrior Infantry |
| 4 | Reckless Mage and 1d4 + 4 pyromaniac Knights† eager to defend her |

**Cathedral Encounters (Tier 3)**

| 1d4 | Result |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | An Archpriest and an Elemental Cultist preparing to burn innocent civilians at the stake, cheered on by 1d10 + 10 Warrior Infantry |
| 2 | Two Death Knight Aspirants brought to the chapel for execution who have broken free and are surrounded by the crusaders they slew (risen as 2d10 Ghouls) and two Priests begging for help |
| 3 | An Archmage directing 1d6 + 4 Scout Captains to fire on intruders as 1d4 + 4 Gladiators move to intercept them |
| 4 | An Archpriest, guarded by three Warrior Veterans,* praying over six dead crusaders |

†Can cast Cure Wounds (level 4 version) once

| 20 | Loksey: Pipes of the Sewers (whistle that affects dogs instead of rats, and summons a Mastiff instead of a Swarm of Rats) |
| 21 | Doan: Vithrak, Gaze of the Deadman |

| 23 | Mograine: Mace of Disruption |
| 24 | Whitemane: Cloth miter Helm of Brilliance that marks the Crusade's High Inquisitor |

### Shadowfang Keep

*For Level 1–4 Characters • Alliance Versus Horde*

*[Image: image (bestiary/WoW/Lord Godfrey.webp)]*

For generations, Shadowfang Keep has stood on the boundary between x and the walled kingdom of x. The keep has been conquered countless times over the years, filling its halls with restless spirits.

Today, this accursed fortress is held by Lord Vincent Godfrey, a treacherous undead who's a fugitive from both the Alliance and the Horde. Both Packleader Ivar Bloodfang (Medium, Lawful Good Warrior Veteran), a worgen leader who supports the Alliance, and Deathstalker Commander Belmont (Medium Warrior Veteran), a Forsaken leader of the Horde, lead attacks on the fortress, seeking to gain revenge on Lord Godfrey and to claim this crucial redoubt in the war for Silverpine.

#### Shadowfang Keep Adventures

Use the suggestions in the Shadowfang Keep Adventure Hooks table to draw adventurers there.

**Shadowfang Keep Adventure Hooks**

| 1d6 | Adventure Hook |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | A faction commander recruits the characters to retake Shadowfang Keep from Lord Godfrey. (Renown: +1 with one x or x subfaction) |
| 2 | The characters decide to claim Shadowfang Keep for themselves and use it as a 10. |
| 3 | A patron in the Kirin Tor seeks The Book of Ur, which contains secrets of the worgen curse and is rumored to be in Shadowfang Keep's library. (Renown: +1 with the x) |
| 4 | The characters have claimed the keep and must defend it until reinforcements arrive. |
| 5 | A faction leader wants adventurers to liberate a prisoner of war held in Shadowfang Keep: either the Forsaken Deathstalker Adamant (Medium Spy) or the human Ashcrombe (Medium Mage Apprentice). (Renown: +1 with one x or x subfaction) |
| 6 | The Alliance or the Horde has conquered Shadowfang Keep; a faction commander asks adventurers to reclaim the keep. (Renown: +1 with one x or x subfaction) |

> **About Shadowfang Keep**
> Shadowfang Keep was a five-player dungeon in the original *World of Warcraft*, designed for low-level characters to confront the archmage Arugal and the worgen he liberated from the Emerald Dream and set loose on the Eastern Kingdoms. In this first iteration, best suited for level 5–10 characters, the dungeon bosses are the living Arugal (Archmage), the already ghostly forms of Baron Silverlaine and Commander Springvale, and several worgen (Werewolves), some of whose spirits Baron Silverlaine later calls on: Rethilgore, Razorclaw the Butcher, Odo the Blindwatcher, Fenrus the Devourer, and Wolf Master Nandos. Other tormented spirits and ferocious worgs also populate the dungeon.
> The version presented in this chapter represents a later stage in the keep's history, after a group of Forsaken converted the keep into a stronghold to protect themselves from enemies during the Cataclysm.

#### Shadowfang Keep Areas

*[Image: Map: Shadowfang Keep (book/WoW/119-06-035.map-shadowfang-keep.webp)]*

*[Image: Player Version (book/WoW/120-06-035.map-shadowfang-keep-player.webp)]*

Shadowfang Keep has the following featured areas:

- **Entry Gate.** Entry Gate An iron portcullis blocks access from the keep's entry to the courtyard. Baron Ashbury (see "x") leads the keep's defenses from the jail and gatehouse, which leads to the courtyard, and can raise the gate. Skeletons arisen from the keep's countless defeated assailants prowl here.
- **Courtyard.** Courtyard This courtyard provides access to the dining hall and the keep's battlements and rooftops. It is patrolled by Pustulant Monstrosities and restless Specters of the keep's staff.
- **Silverlaine Manor.** Silverlaine Manor The one area of the keep Lord Godfrey couldn't reclaim is Silverlaine Manor. It is prowled by feral worgen (use the Werewolf stat block without its Bite attack) summoned by the keep's previous master. The worgen of x have been unable to negotiate with them.
- **Dining Hall.** Dining Hall A banquet was set here to celebrate Lord Godfrey's liberation of Pyrewood Village, but the feast now rots untouched. Baron Silverlaine (see "x") haunts the hall, guarded by four Specters of his staff.
- **Battlements and Rooftops.** Battlements and Rooftops The ghostly Commander Springvale patrols here with two Springvale's Specters (see "x" for both), watched by leering Gargoyles. Loot from ancient battles is strewn across the battlements.
- **The Keep.** The Keep Wooden stairways and catwalks wind up a tower teeming with Undead. Above it is an abandoned magical laboratory that Lord Walden (see "x") is ransacking.
- **Godfrey's Chamber.** Godfrey's Chamber At the keep tower's apex is the two-story chamber of Lord Godfrey and his two Pustulant Monstrosity bodygards (see "x" for both). Its upper floor has collapsed into the first, and he shoots intruders from the top of the stairs while the intruders are busy with the bodyguards below.

#### Sample Encounters
